    CASA (C++) bugs¶

    Notes on bugs or likely bugs observed while doing Rust/C++ parity work against CASA.

    Known imaging parity defect: CASA Högbom niter off-by-one. CASA behaviours casa-rs follows for parity: AW-projection hand pairing, AW-projection prediction w, AW MT-MFS restart weights, MatrixCleaner's stop iteration, exact peak comparisons, multi-term weight normalization, a Taylor term's sum of weights per Stokes plane, a Taylor run's 'common' beam, the Airy beam's blockage term, the EVLA's 11052 MHz fit, the simulator's and tclean's two mosaic machines, a moved pointing phased on its field, datacolumn='corrected' without CORRECTED_DATA, W projection's sectioned grid edge. CASA behaviours casa-rs does not follow: FITS import of an empty primary HDU, restoring-beam removal with a malformed record, a Gaussian beam fit to collinear PSF samples, non-frequency component frequency units, nonconforming component flux conversion, duplicate and empty component removals, component labels containing invalid UTF-8, cyclic component-list type checks, complex quantity-array values, bare complex64 quantity values, automatic-mask convolution on rectangular pixels, byte order of integer quantity arrays, a Taylor run with no weight on its first correlation, odd-size scale convolutions, MatrixCleaner's step residual, MEM masks on tiled images, an unplaceable PB pointing, an unused dish choosing the PB, the cube density's two uvw frames, continuum briggsabs at robust 0 and noise 0, nsigma with fastnoise=False and no mask, polarized AW sensitivity from one correlation, signed Taylor moments with empty cross-hands, single-precision Taylor weights, AW baseline pointing groups, a mosaic's single-precision weight image, facets of an image size that does not divide, the standard gridder dropping the final Fourier-grid row and column, an unflagged non-finite sample, a tuned cube's frequency selection, a TOPO cube's cache-dependent endpoint selection, an overflowing FIELD ID, a bare FIELD escape, direction regridding of a CLEAN mask, expanding a CLEAN mask, a cube's fine frequency grid at a whole ratio, the .gridwt a cube leaves, multiscale on an off-centre PSF, a cube restart grids at the saved alternate PSF direction, a cube's beam for a plane without one, tb.getcol of an int64 column, tb.putkeyword of an int beyond 32 bits, tb slices that abort the process, tb.putcell of an int beyond 32 bits, tb.putcell with several rows, tb.addrows with a negative count, tb.removerows and tb.addcols that fail part way, MSIter's receptor angles for a window without its own FEED row, a projection name with spaces, NCP on the celestial equator, PCO coordinates on the central meridian. Simulator behaviours casa-rs does not follow: TIME as TAI, shadowing the front antenna, relabelled spectral-window frames, unconvertible coordinate systems, quantities in the wrong units, calls before setconfig, a zero integration time, readantenna's observatory fallback, readantenna's names after blank lines, the measures tool's default frame, the model predict rewrites, models the simulator cannot read, setmosaicfield's off-centre grid, observemany's rows before an unknown field, W projection without planes, total power in cross-correlations, close after a mosaic predict, the beam on a moved pointing, scratch columns added at predict and corrupt, tsys-manual's stale and borrowed system temperatures, tsys-manual scaling every noise term, the noise term a refused tsys-manual leaves, tsys-atm's atmosphere half a band away, settrop's dispersive delay alone, settrop's atmosphere half a band away, settrop's anisotropic screen, settrop's solutions stamped halfway, gain screens read past their transform, gain screens sharing a seed, random gains about zero, the last antenna's unit gain or leakage, leakage for two windows only, invalid leakage becoming one, a simulated term's table written when it is set. Measures behaviours casa-rs follows for parity: IAU 1980 sidereal time and obliquities under IAU 2000. Measures behaviours casa-rs does not follow: latitudes of unnormalized directions, IAU 2000 JMEAN to J2000, IAU 2000 GMST1. Simulator behaviours casa-rs follows: observemany's padded single name, one leakage per window for the whole run. Component-list behaviours casa-rs does not follow: physicality of an empty component list, non-finite or cyclic selection inputs, a limb-darkened disk without an exponent, a negative component under the primary beam, a single spectral index, the beam's component spectrum frame, addcomponent's single-precision flux. Component-list behaviours casa-rs follows: the cl tool's record units, addcomponent's polarization, a shape addcomponent cannot make. Concatenation behaviours casa-rs does not follow: a merged source record's time, source equivalence decided by its last column, merged sources' numbering, observation IDs after removing duplicates, an input without states, writing into its inputs. ATM data errors casa-rs corrects (tools/atmosphere/atm-corrections.patch): HCN's misplaced line, sea-level air densities, the H2(17)O weight, the HDO weight. ATM behaviours casa-rs follows: HCN above 100 km, copied bottom pressures, unpadded ozone isotopologue layers, (16)O(17)O. ATM behaviours casa-rs does not follow: the averaged non-dispersive wet phase. Calibration behaviours casa-rs follows: a gain table's resolution and total bandwidth. Calibration behaviours casa-rs does not follow: a solve that fits exactly. simutil behaviours casa-rs does not follow: the ALMA tiling's default beam, noisetemp's receiver lookup, directions without an epoch, modifymodel's axis swaps, modifymodel's Stokes labels, incell as two cells, the component-only model's unset width, component spectra in another frame. Flagging behaviours casa-rs does not follow: a re-saved version's comment, an unrecognized flag merge choice, a summary of correlations it cannot name. Coordinate behaviours casa-rs does not follow: a tabular coordinate's subimage, a FITS date's time scale, feather on a transposed image. MeasurementSet selection behaviours casa-rs follows: later timerange terms' defaults, FLAG_ROW beside other selections. MeasurementSet selection behaviours casa-rs does not follow: the state a refused bracketed range leaves.

    AW-projection pairs the partner hand's visibility with the conjugate-baseline cell¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-09
    • Status: likely CASA bug; casa-rs follows it for parity (#667, PR #668)
    • Affected code:
    • casatools/src/code/synthesis/TransformMachines2/AWVisResampler.cc (getConvFunc_p, DataToGridImpl_p, GridToData)

    Summary¶

    For a row with w ≤ 0, getConvFunc_p selects the conjugate-baseline cell of the outer correlation's Mueller element (conjMNdx, the partner hand: RR ↔ LL, RL ↔ LR), and DataToGridImpl_p then grids the visibility of the selected cell's hand (visVecElement = muellerElement % nDataPol), not the outer correlation's. The RR grid receives RR × conj(CF_RR) for w > 0 and LL × CF_LL for w ≤ 0; GridToData likewise reads the partner's model grid into the outer visibility. Stokes I of an unpolarized source is unaffected (the two hands are equal); per-hand grids of data whose hands differ, such as a circularly polarized source, mix the hands on the w ≤ 0 rows.

    Why this looks like a bug¶

    The Mueller element of a real power pattern has a Hermitian transform, so the RR kernel at −|w| is the conjugate of the kernel of the mirrored RR beam at +|w|; for the EVLA squint (R and L beams offset in opposite directions) the mirrored RR beam is the LL beam, which is why the partner cell stands in for the conjugate. The visibility being convolved is still the outer hand's. The index muellerElement % nDataPol returns the column of the selected cell's own element instead of the column of its position in the conjugate Mueller matrix.

    Effect¶

    On refim_mawproject (CASA 6.7.6.14, Stokes I, wbawp=True, conjbeams=True) the two pairings differ by 3.06e-3 normalised RMS in the dirty image. casa-rs compiles CASA's pairing (casa-imaging-operator, aw::routing into MuellerRouting) and agrees with CASA to 2e-7.

    AW-projection predicts with the unrotated w¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-09
    • Status: CASA inconsistency between gridding and prediction; casa-rs follows it for parity (#667, PR #668)
    • Affected code: AWVisResampler.cc (GridToData reads vbs.vb_p->uvw()(2, irow); DataToGridImpl_p reads the rotated vbs.uvw_p)

    Summary¶

    AWProjectFT hands the resampler the uvw rotated to the image phase centre and the phase shift (vbs.uvw_p, dphase_p) together with the VisBuffer (vbs.vb_p). GridToData selects its w-plane and tap conjugation from the VisBuffer's unrotated w while placing the sample at the rotated u, v with the rotated phasor; DataToGridImpl_p uses the rotated w for both. The two differ only where the rotation moves a w across a plane boundary or through zero. casa-rs carries the unrotated uvw per row (RowContext::original_uvw_m, ConvolutionFunctionSet::prediction_w) and keys the AW prediction on it.

    importvla stale VLACDA cache crash on old VLA export data¶

    • Date noted: 2026-04-15
    • Status: likely CASA importvla bug
    • Affected code:
    • casatools/src/code/nrao/VLA/VLACDA.cc
    • casatools/src/code/nrao/VLA/VLABaselineRecord.cc
    • NRAO Archive file: AG189_1_46325.23029_46325.80807.exp (archive revision 11)

    Summary¶

    importvla can abort on old VLA export data when a CDA is valid in one logical record and absent in the next. The immediate failure is the offset != 0 assertion in VLABaselineRecord::attach().

    Likely mechanism¶

    VLALogicalRecord::read() reattaches all four CDAs on every logical record. VLACDA caches baseline-record objects, so if a CDA was populated on one record and then becomes absent on the next, VLACDA::attach() can try to reattach cached baseline objects using offset 0, which triggers the assertion in VLABaselineRecord.

    Proposed fix¶

    In VLACDA::attach(), if the new CDA is invalid (itsOffset == 0, or equivalently no baseline data are present), clear the cached baseline objects and return before any reattach path runs.

    mstransform channel-mode transformed-grid inconsistency¶

    • Date noted: 2026-04-10
    • Status: likely CASA mstransform bug or long-standing implementation quirk
    • Affected code: casatools/src/code/mstransform/MSTransform/MSTransformRegridder.cc

    Summary¶

    In the transformed mode="channel" / regridQuant == "freq" path, CASA appears to anchor the output-grid start edge using transCHAN_WIDTH[firstChan], while it spaces the uniform output grid using transCHAN_WIDTH[0].

    That is inconsistent once the frame transformation causes per-channel widths to vary slightly across the SPW. In the EVLA refim_Cband.G37line.ms repro case used during parity work, this produced an output-axis offset of about 0.046 Hz.

    Why this looks like a CASA bug, not a casacore bug¶

    Direct Rust-vs-casacore measures conversion for the relevant TOPO -> LSRK path matched to about 0.0015 Hz, so the underlying frame conversion itself does not appear to be the source of the discrepancy. The remaining offset is introduced later by CASA's transformed-grid construction policy in mstransform.

    Notes¶

    • This is scientifically tiny in the observed repro, but it is semantically inconsistent.
    • The effect shows up in transformed channel-mode cubes where the transformed widths differ slightly across the SPW.

    Related references¶

    • casacore PR #1464: Fix missing frame bias in IAU2000 JNAT<->APP conversions
    • casacore issue #1465: Unit mismatch in setMaximumCacheSize: bytes passed where MiB expected

    Those casacore links are not the same bug, but they came out of the same cross-checking and parity work that surfaced this CASA-side issue.

    CASA Hogbom niter off-by-one bug¶

    • Date noted: 2026-04-05
    • Status: likely CASA bug / legacy interface mismatch
    • Affected code:
    • casatools/src/code/synthesis/ImagerObjects/SynthesisDeconvolver.cc
    • casatools/src/code/synthesis/ImagerObjects/SDAlgorithmHogbomClean.cc
    • casatools/casacore/scimath_f/hclean.f

    Summary¶

    In the current CASA tclean(..., deconvolver='hogbom') path, niter=1 appears able to commit two clean components inside a single minor cycle while still reporting iterdone = 1.

    Since provider contract 31 (owner approved 2026-10-31), casa-rs defaults its extra hogbom_iteration_mode option to casa-inclusive to reproduce this behavior. Explicit strict limits physical updates to the requested budget. Migrating an older profile warns when that option was omitted and adopts the inclusive default; explicit values are preserved. CASA's 92 parameter defaults are unchanged.

    The effect is not limited to the top-level niter=1 case. Any CASA Hogbom minor-cycle call that enters hclean with a positive cycleNiter may commit one more component than the reported minor-cycle count. In a Cotton-Schwab run, that means the model, residual, restored image, and controller stop/refresh decisions can shift by up to one extra component per Hogbom minor-cycle block.

    The first repro used:

    • dataset: .../casatestdata/measurementset/vla/sim_data_VLA_jet.ms
    • setup: imsize=512, cell='12arcsec', specmode='mfs', weighting='natural', gain=0.1, threshold='0Jy', niter=1
    • observed result: CASA reported iterdone = 1, but the output .model image contained two nonzero clean components

    Likely mechanism¶

    An off-by-one caller/kernel mismatch:

    1. SDAlgorithmHogbomClean::takeOneStep seeds starting_iteration = 0
    2. the Fortran hclean kernel iterates over an inclusive do iter = siter, niter
    3. the returned count is then clamped back down to niter

    With siter = 0 and niter = 1, that inclusive loop permits two update opportunities, which matches the observed behavior.

    Repro detail¶

    The output .model image contained two nonzero pixels:

    1. (264, 331) = 0.6685306429862976
    2. (265, 331) = 0.6019284129142761

    Their sum matched the reported modelFlux, which makes this look like an actual extra component update rather than a display or reporting artifact.

    A second trace on the ALMA TW Hydra tutorial data used tclean(..., deconvolver='hogbom', niter=1, cycleniter=1, weighting='briggs', robust=0.5, imsize=250, cell='0.1arcsec'). CASA reported one minor iteration, but the model flux was 0.0569704 Jy; the dirty-image peak was 0.299844 Jy/beam, so one strict-gain component at gain=0.1 would account for only about 0.0299844 Jy. The reported model flux is therefore consistent with two committed Hogbom components in that minor-cycle call.

    Benchmark repro¶

    The same behavior appeared on a medium standard-MFS VLA benchmark dataset:

    • setup: specmode='mfs', gridder='standard', deconvolver='hogbom', imsize=1024, cell='0.25arcsec', spw='0:0~63', Briggs robust=0.5, gain=0.1, threshold='0Jy', niter=1, cycleniter=1
    • observed result: CASA reported iterdone = 1, but the output .model image contained two nonzero clean components:
    • (510, 510) = 9.177456855773926
    • (508, 507) = 8.556220054626465

    In a strict one-component casa-rs comparison this made the one-cycle residual RMS difference jump to 3.65e-2. Running casa-rs with its CASA-compatible Hogbom accounting (HogbomIterationAccounting::CasaInclusive) reproduced both components and reduced the residual RMS difference to 7.64e-5, matching the dirty gridding numerical floor. On the full niter=500, cycleniter=50 heavy row, the same CASA-compatible mode reduced the CASA/casa-rs product differences to .model RMS 1.71e-7, .residual RMS 5.68e-5, and .image RMS 5.87e-5.

    MEM reuses the first mask tile for its alpha update¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14's no-prior EntropyI::formGDG advances its model and residual lattice iterators but does not advance its mask iterator. Its separate formGDGStep does advance the mask. Consequently the alpha update reads the first mask tile repeatedly, even when the model and residual have advanced to later tiles. For a 512 × 512 image whose 256 × 128 first tile is outside an 81 × 81 box mask, an instrumented CASA run gives a zero alpha-update gradient matrix, although its step matrix has a nonzero chi-square gradient. On the refim_eptwochan.ms two-iteration test, the original CASA model differs from the native model by 0.164 normalized RMS; an offset box gives 0.164 as well.

    A local CASA probe replaced only that Gram-matrix calculation with formGDGStep, which computes the same matrix while advancing the mask. The probe is identical to unmodified CASA for a 200 × 200 single-tile box, and its 512 × 512 centred and offset box models agree with casa-rs within 2.2e-7 normalized RMS; the other image products also pass their normal tolerances. This is evidence for the cause, not a replacement CASA reference. casa-rs reads each pixel's actual mask for both calculations, rather than reproducing a result that depends on the image's storage tile layout (#1060).

    MatrixCleaner counts the iteration that stops it¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-09
    • Status: CASA accounting quirk; casa-rs follows it for parity (IF-5, #654)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/MatrixCleaner.cc (MatrixCleaner::clean), read by SDAlgorithmMSClean::takeOneStep

    clean increments itsIteration at the top of each pass and only then tests the threshold (abs(strength) < threshold()) and the 50% divergence rule, so a step that stops on either reports one more iteration (numberIterations()) than it cleaned components. Multiscale iterdone, the niter budget and the per-cycle summaryminor counts include it; on refim_point with scales [0, 6, 10] CASA reports [22, 3, 25, 3, 47] for [21, 2, 24, 2, 51] components when the extra iteration is not charged. casa-rs charges it (Solver::charges_stop).

    Multiscale and multi-term scale convolutions shift by a pixel on odd image sizes¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-09
    • Status: likely CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (IF-5, #654)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/MatrixCleaner.cc (makeScale, makePsfScales, makeDirtyScales), MultiTermMatrixCleaner.cc (computeRHS, computeHessianPeak)

    Scale functions are centred at (nx/2, ny/2) and convolved by FFT without moving that centre to the origin. Single convolutions are re-centred with FFTServer::flip, which rotates by ceil(n/2); double convolutions (PSF ⊛ scale ⊛ scale) are not flipped, relying on the two n/2 shifts cancelling. Both are exact only for even n: on an odd axis the scale- convolved residual is one pixel off its residual and the PSF cross terms one pixel off the PSF peak. casa-rs convolves with the scale centred at the origin, which agrees with CASA on every even axis.

    MatrixCleaner's step residual forgets earlier steps¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-09
    • Status: likely CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (IF-5, #654)
    • Affected code: synthesis/ImagerObjects/SDAlgorithmMSClean.cc (takeOneStep), SDAlgorithmBase.cc (deconvolve)

    With cycleniter ≥ 5000 a plane's minor cycle runs in 2000-iteration takeOneStep calls. Each call sets the residual to itsDirty − PSF ⊛ (model − prevModel), where itsDirty is the residual at the plane's first step and model − prevModel only this step's components, so from the second step on the reported peak residual omits the earlier steps' subtraction. That peak drives the minor-cycle stop codes 2 and 4. casa-rs carries each step's residual into the next.

    Peak searches let rounding choose between equal pixels¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-09
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows (exact comparisons); it makes parity rows on exactly symmetric skies rounding-dependent (IF-5, #654)
    • Affected code: casacore/scimath_f/hclean.f, casacore minMax as read by MatrixCleaner::findMaxAbsMask and MultiTermMatrixCleaner (chooseComponent), Clark's ABSMAXF

    Every CASA peak search compares exactly and keeps the first extreme in its scan order; casa-rs does the same. On a point-symmetric sky such as refim_point, mirror pixels are equal in exact arithmetic (CASA's dirty and PSF images are exactly point-symmetric even in float32), and the scale convolutions and subtractions of the minor cycle break the tie by an ulp or two, so rounding, not the data, picks the component. On refim_point CASA's rounding went both ways: MatrixCleaner took the first mirror in scan order, and the multi-term cleaner the later one (one tie, at [50,47] / [50,53], value 4.9e-5, in the last cycle). casa-rs's FFTW plans are chosen by timing, so its refim_point multiscale row comes out at 5.4e-6 or 4.5e-2 NRMS from run to run; its MT-MFS row is 3.4e-4 when the tie goes CASA's way and 3.1e-2 when it does not (as on main). Both outcomes are equally valid cleans; such rows cannot measure parity, so IF-5's multiscale and MT-MFS acceptance uses skies without the symmetry (refim_twopoints_twochan, refim_eptwochan).

    refim_point_withline.ms with spw='0:0~7', niter=50, gain=0.2, deconvolver='mtmfs' shows the same thing component by component (#1113). Running niter from 1 to 50 on each side and differencing consecutive .model images gives each iteration's component. Both sides choose the same pixels through iteration 19. At iteration 20 CASA takes [49,46] and that casa-rs series takes [51,54], the mirror pixel through the source at [50,50], with the same strengths to six figures. In five repeats in separate processes, CASA gave identical images each time; casa-rs gave two outcomes, one matching CASA and one matching CASA reflected through [50,50]. That difference (.model.tt1 2.4e-2 NRMS, .residual.tt1 6.2e-2) falls to between 6e-8 and 8e-7 on either match, so the case cannot measure parity either.

    The multi-term weight normalization override is never called¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-11
    • Status: likely CASA bug; casa-rs follows it for parity (#704, PR #707)
    • Affected code: synthesis/ImagerObjects/SIImageStore.h, SIImageStoreMultiTerm.h, SynthesisNormalizer.cc (divideWeightBySumWt)

    SIImageStore declares virtual void divideWeightBySumwt() (lower-case wt). SIImageStoreMultiTerm declares void divideWeightBySumWt() (upper case W), a different name, so it overrides nothing; its loop that divides every Taylor weight image by the principal sum of weights is never called. SynthesisNormalizer::divideWeightBySumWt calls itsImages->divideWeightBySumwt(), which reaches the base body and divides weight(), term 0, alone. MultiTermFTNew::finalizeToSkyNew writes each term's raw getWeightImage moment, so .weight.tt1 and .weight.tt2 stay the raw Σ W·s^N while .weight.tt0 is normalized. CASA 6.7.6.14 on refim_alma_mosaic (mosaic, nterms=2) agrees: weight.ttN / sumwt.ttN is 0.6913 for N = 0, 1 and 2. casa-rs publishes the same.

    AW gridding can continue after a PA cache miss¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 bug. Casa-rs reports an uncovered imported buffer (#1070).
    • Affected code: TransformMachines2/CFStore2.cc (nearestPA), VB2CFBMap.cc (makeVBRow2CFBMap) and AWProjectFT.cc (findConvFunction, resampleDataToGrid).

    CFStore2::nearestPA takes the first angle strictly within computepastep, in the imported catalog's sorted angle order. On a cache miss makeVBRow2CFBMap can leave the previous row-to-buffer mapping in place. findConvFunction can generate a missing buffer when its angle change detector fires. The gridding path also calls the map builder and continues when that lookup reports a miss.

    On refim_mawproject.ms with a 5-degree step and default rotation, CASA creates 31 PA buffers. The warm replay still logs five integrations with no in-band PA in field 1 and publishes an image. Its residual differs from the cold run by 2.95e-3 NRMS. Casa-rs reports a missing imported buffer before publication, preserving its read-only import policy (#1043). Field 0 is covered by the same cache, and all seven products match CASA with exact masks. The default 360-degree control also matches across both fields.

    CASA imports sorted PA headers through CFStore2::resize with a strict one-degree tolerance. When a new angle matches a buffer, CASA replaces its angle and loads the new buffer's cells. Consecutive angles within one degree therefore leave only the last paired buffer. The row selector then uses the requested compute step over the surviving angles. A one-integration control on refim_mawproject.ms uses distinct payloads at 177.8 and 178.2 degrees. CASA's dense and later-buffer-only references are exactly equal. Keeping both buffers in casa-rs had changed the residual by 0.011 normalized RMS and flipped 82 mask pixels. The coalesced import matches all seven products, exact masks and controller counts, and leaves the cache files untouched (#1070).

    AW cache creation and reopening can select different PA buffers¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 cold and warm selection differs when PA coverage intervals overlap. Casa-rs uses the sorted cache order in both runs (#1070).
    • Affected code: TransformMachines2/AWProjectFT.cc (findConvFunction), CFStore2.cc (resize, nearestPA) and CFCache.cc (fillCFListFromDisk).

    CASA adds generated buffers in observation order and picks the first PA strictly inside computepastep. Reloading sorts the cache by PA before applying the same first-match rule. On FIELD 0 of refim_mawproject.ms at 5 degrees, both orders cover all 108 selected integrations using the same 19 buffers. They select different buffers at 57 integrations. CASA's cold and warm residuals differ by 3.284e-3 NRMS and 16 mask pixels. With the same cache pixels, a temporary insertion-order selector matches all seven CASA cold products. The sorted selector matches all seven warm products, with exact masks in both comparisons.

    Native cache generation plans a centre at each uncovered selected feed angle and uses CASA's sorted replay order for both fresh and reused cells. Its cold and warm outputs agree with the CASA warm reference.

    AW aperture rotation can regrid an already rotated cell¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 rotation history affects the residual. Casa-rs samples the saved cell at each rotation epoch (#1070).
    • Affected code: TransformMachines2/AWConvFunc.cc (prepareConvFunction), AzElAperture.cc (rotate2) and Utils.cc (rotateComplexArray).

    The first change greater than rotatepastep regrids the current kernel with cubic interpolation and records the current feed PA. Later changes regrid those already interpolated pixels. On field 0 of refim_mawproject.ms, a zero-weight integration at -99.639° followed by the same nonzero integration at 177.839° changes CASA's 5-degree residual by 1.122e-4 NRMS versus selecting the final integration alone. Both paths rotate to the same final PA. The default 360-degree control has identical arrays in both selections.

    Casa-rs uses the same strict trigger and cubic interpolation, starting from the saved cell at each epoch. As in CASA's major cycle, an integration with no unflagged cross-correlation cannot advance the rotation. The two selections at the same final PA have identical arrays and masks in casa-rs. The independent CASA one-integration control and the full field match all seven products and masks under the existing limits. The largest full-field NRMS is 6.737e-5.

    The same history extends across Högbom major cycles. In the field 0 eight-iteration CLEAN, CASA starts each residual refresh by rotating the cell left by the previous pass from 90.5703° back to -86.0481°. Recomputing the final residual in a fresh CASA run with the saved model and the same inputs changes the residual by 1.879e-4 NRMS. The static 360-degree control repeats exactly. Casa-rs starts each rotation from the saved cell and uses the selected epoch for both prediction and gridding. It matches all eight CLEAN products, masks, and iteration counts, with worst NRMS 2.384e-4 over the full field (#1070).

    The wideband two-term case on the 1.2, 1.5 and 1.8 GHz windows of the same field runs the rotation chain separately during the Taylor passes. The stock CASA run repeats exactly but differs from casa-rs by 2.769e-3 NRMS in residual.tt0 and 3.726e-3 in residual.tt1. Fourteen alpha-mask pixels differ. In a CASA control, AzElAperture::rotate2 keeps the same trigger and output angle but hands rotateComplexArray the original cell pixels and original angle on every call. With just that intervention, both residuals agree within 1.192e-6 NRMS, both alpha masks agree exactly, and all 19 products pass, including the weak odd Taylor weight check. A debugger run without the intervention reproduces all 19 stock products exactly. The three windows share a feed angle and usable integration times, so casa-rs uses one schedule for all three.

    AW pointing can reuse the origin of an earlier convolution buffer¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 bug. casa-rs uses the current buffer's origin (#1070).
    • Affected code: TransformMachines2/VB2CFBMap.cc, PhaseGrad.cc (needsNewPhaseGrad, ComputeFieldPointingGrad) and the resampler's accumulateToGrid.inc and accumulateFromGrid.inc.

    The gradient array uses the selected PA buffer's maximum CF width. Its origin is width/2 for an even width and width/2+1 for an odd width. The resampler reads the gradient about width/2 in either case. A wider array can survive a switch to a smaller CF buffer on the same field. PSF formation visits the weight buffer first, and the residual can use its origin when the two buffers have opposite parity.

    On the offset field of refim_oneshiftpoint.mosaic.ms, making the imaging or weight buffer even produces a 0.0325 NRMS change in .residual.tt0 between fresh PSF formation and calcpsf=False,calcres=True with that same PSF. The PSF, weights, inputs and cache are identical between each pair. The uniformly odd control produces identical CASA residuals. Casa-rs uses the maximum width of each selected imaging or weight buffer independently. Its fresh and saved-PSF runs agree with all 21 CASA products from the saved-PSF control, including masks and both PB-corrected Taylor images. Prediction uses the conjugate of the imaging phase.

    AW MT-MFS restart multiplies saved higher Taylor weights again¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 bug. casa-rs recomputes the weights with the PSF (#1070).
    • Affected code: TransformMachines2/CFCache.cc (loadWtImage), AWProjectFT.cc (getWeightImage) and AWProjectWBFT.cc (initializeToSky).

    With gridder='awproject', deconvolver='mtmfs' and the default calcpsf=True, CASA can read each saved .weight.ttN when a run continues at the same image prefix. CFCache::loadWtImage multiplies each saved plane by .sumwt.tt0. The higher Taylor weight images were already written as raw moments (see the preceding normalization bug). AWProjectFT::getWeightImage also reapplies its sinc grid correction to the saved, already corrected planes.

    In test_mosaic_mtmfs.test_mtmfs_awproject_cbFalse_twofield on refim_oneshiftpoint.mosaic.ms, a dirty run followed by ten CLEAN iterations multiplies the central values of .weight.tt1 and .weight.tt2 by 49,971.44, the principal sum of weights. The final restored images and residuals also depend on the saved weights. Moving only the three saved weight images aside before CLEAN makes CASA agree with a fresh CLEAN on the same observation. casa-rs recomputes weights when it recomputes the PSF. All 19 products and the iteration counts agree with both CASA controls under the existing tolerances.

    The matching cbTrue_twofield case reloads the higher weights by 49,980.72 and fits the same reload and sinc correction to 7.4e-8 NRMS. Fresh CLEAN, same-prefix CLEAN after moving only the saved weights, and continuation with calcpsf=False each agree with casa-rs on all 19 products and their masks. Both implementations report ten iterations and two major cycles in each control.

    A Taylor term gives every Stokes plane the first correlation's sum of weights¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: deliberate CASA shortcut; casa-rs follows it for parity (#1065)
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines2/MultiTermFTNew.cc (finalizeToSkyNew, finalizeToWeightImage), synthesis/TransformMachines/StokesImageUtil.cc (ToStokesSumWt)

    A single-term gridder (FTMachine::finalizeToSkyNew) forms each Stokes plane's .sumwt with ToStokesSumWt's coordinate form, from the correlations the plane is made of: min(RR, LL) for I and V, min(RL, LR) for Q and U. MultiTermFTNew calls the two-argument form instead, which "picks the value from the first correlation plane, and fills it into all stokes planes", as its comment says, valid when every correlation has the same weights. So a Taylor term's .sumwt.ttN holds the first gridded correlation's sum on every Stokes plane: of parallel-hand data imaged as IQUV, Q and U have Stokes I's sum where a single term's have zero, and the residual of every plane is normalized by that sum. CASA 6.7.6.14's test_stokes_weighting (call 8, mtmfs of refim_twochan.ms) writes [143360.94] × 4 in every .sumwt.ttN. casa-rs forms a Taylor term's sums the same way.

    With no weight on the first correlation, every Taylor plane is zero¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA writes blank products; casa-rs refuses with a diagnostic (#1109)
    • Affected code: synthesis/ImagerObjects/SIImageStore.cc (divideImageByWeightVal)

    When usable data reach the grids but the first grid plane has no weight, the Taylor sum-weight rule gives zero to every Stokes plane. divideImageByWeightVal blanks a plane whose sum is at most 1e-7. casa-rs refuses a Taylor product whose principal sum is nonpositive.

    A first correlation explicitly flagged throughout is an earlier case. FTMachine::setSpectralFlag applies the documented ordinary Stokes rule: a flag on any selected correlation flags the whole channel. Flagging RR in refim_point_linRL.ms leaves RL, LR and LL unflagged, but CASA 6.7.6.14 writes a blank PSF and then aborts on invalid array bounds. casa-rs reports that RR is fully flagged before publishing images. A MeasurementSet containing just RL and LR images QU successfully on the same data. The complete-correlation control still agrees with CASA. Imaging issue #1109

    restoringbeam='common' restores every Taylor Stokes plane with the first plane's beam¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA behaviour; casa-rs's result is the same (#1065)
    • Affected code: synthesis/ImagerObjects/SDAlgorithmBase.cc (restore), SDAlgorithmMSMFS.cc (restore), SIImageStore.cc (restore), synthesis/TransformMachines/StokesImageUtil.cc (ToStokesPSF)

    SDAlgorithmBase::restore passes its restoring beam by non-const reference to SIImageStore::restore on each Stokes plane's sub-store, and the first plane's call sets it to that plane's beam (casacore's SubImage holds one beam of the set). Planes 1 to P−1 are then restored with plane 0's beam, and SDAlgorithmMSMFS::restore records that one beam (setRestoringBeam), so the image has a single beam. casa-rs restores every plane with the beam that encloses all the planes' fitted beams, as it does any image's planes under 'common', and records that one beam. The two agree because ToStokesPSF gives every Stokes plane the first correlation's PSF, so every plane fits the same beam. A beam the request gives is recorded once in the same way.

    A Gaussian beam fit to collinear PSF samples is unstable¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-11
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 instability, with a deterministic failed-fit treatment in casa-rs (#1140).
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines/StokesImageUtil.cc (FitGaussianPSF), synthesis/ImagerObjects/SIImageStore.cc (makeImageBeamSet, getPSFSidelobeLevel).

    In a 64-square, two-channel Clark restart at 8 arcsec per pixel, the saved unit-peak PSF of one channel is a one-pixel-wide line. Changing one off-peak sample by one Float step, about 5.96e-8, moves CASA's fitted position angle from 10.3232° to 0.0017° or −56.3750°, and the reported sidelobe from 0.585933 to 0.156330 or 0.900000. All three PSFs have the same collinear fit support. That support constrains one directional width, leaving the two-dimensional ellipse underdetermined.

    casa-rs checks the selected fit samples on their integer grid and retries with the existing lower cutoffs. When every attempt with enough samples is collinear, the plane uses the ordinary failed-fit beam for its domain. That is the largest fitted beam by area, or a circular 1e-6 arcsec beam when the domain has no fitted beam. Restoration, the minor-cycle patch, and the sidelobe calculation use the same beam. Two-dimensional fits and saved beam metadata retain their existing treatment.

    MT-MFS via cube can abort when the primary-beam cutoff removes the central PSF¶

    CASA 6.7.6-14 aborts for a standard-gridder MVC run on field 0 of refim_oneshiftpoint.mosaic.ms with a 512-square image, 10 arcsec cells, three output channels, and pblimit=-0.8. The reduced Taylor PSF's positive maximum is 0.035371 at (275, 168), outside the central 80-square region. Both a ten-iteration run and a dirty run with restoration enabled reproduce the abort.

    MatrixCleaner::validatePsf leaves the peak at (0, 0) when its central search sees only zeros. MultiTermMatrixCleaner::computeHessianPeak then uses that location to slice its 80-square convolution patch and throws an uncaught array exception for the negative corner (-40, -40).

    casa-rs rejects the clean request with a task error when the Taylor PSF has no positive finite peak in the central patch. Its dirty path publishes the cube and Taylor products without attempting that solve. With the same input and pblimit=-0.1, both clean runs complete ten iterations and three major cycles and stop at the iteration limit. Issue #1140.

    The analytic PB ignores a failed toPixel¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-11
    • Status: likely CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#702, PR #707)
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines/PBMath1D.cc (apply)

    PBMath1D::apply converts the pointing to a pixel with the image's direction coordinate (toPixel) and ignores the call's result, so a pointing the projection cannot place, such as one 90° or more from the image centre under SIN, is centred on whatever pixel the failed call left. casa-rs adds no beam for a pointing 90° or more from an image domain's centre and refuses any other projection failure.

    A mosaic cube restart grids at the saved alternate PSF direction¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 takes the saved PSF coordinates when it creates a temporary channel subimage for calcpsf=True in a cube restart. CubeMajorCycleAlgorithm::subImageStore constructs a SimpleSIImageStore from that subimage, and the constructor takes its coordinates from the PSF when one is present. The gridded sensitivity and PB then correspond to the saved PSF direction, though CASA writes them into the main image's coordinate system.

    On the three-channel natural refim_alma_mosaic.ms cube in #1062, change only whether tclean starts fresh or resumes a saved cube whose PSF is centred 7.5 arcsec away. When recomputing the PSF, CASA's residual, PB and weight image differ from the fresh run by 0.318, 0.167 and 0.337 NRMS. Replacing only the direction coordinate in a copy of the saved PSF makes all three products and the recomputed PSF agree exactly with the fresh run. Reusing the PSF with calcpsf=False preserves the saved PSF and its coordinates and does not trigger the defect.

    casa-rs recomputes the cube's PSF at the requested centre and records that direction in its WCS. The main products use the main image's geometry. The regression reforming_a_mosaic_cube_psf_uses_the_requested_geometry checks the PSF, beams, PB, weight, residual and sums on a cube restart.

    The cube Briggs weightor grids and looks up its density in different uvw frames¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-15
    • Status: likely CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#712 review by Fable and Astra)
    • Affected code (CASA 6.7.5.18 and 6.7.6.14):
    • casatools/src/code/synthesis/TransformMachines2/BriggsCubeWeightor.cc (weightUniform, getWeightUniform)
    • casatools/src/code/synthesis/TransformMachines2/GridFT.cc (put)

    BriggsCubeWeightor, which weighs cubes under uniform and Briggs weighting (perchanweightdensity), fills its density grid through GridFT::put, which rotates each row's uvw to the image phase centre (rotateUVW, GridFT.cc:778). It then looks each row up, and tapers it, at vb.uvw(), the unrotated MeasurementSet uvw (BriggsCubeWeightor.cc:826, 860-861). The grid and the lookup use different frames. When the phase centre is the field centre the rotation is the identity and nothing changes; for a cube with a phase centre away from the field centre, or a multi-field cube, a row reads the density of a different cell from the one its own sample was counted in, so its weight no longer follows the local density. The MFS weightor (VisImagingWeight) accumulates and looks up in one frame, the MeasurementSet uvw.

    casa-rs bins and looks up the cube rule at the rotated uvw, one frame for both (the_cube_density_bins_and_looks_up_the_rotated_uvw), and the standard (MFS) rule at the MeasurementSet uvw, as VisImagingWeight does (the_standard_density_bins_the_measurement_set_uvw). Cubes imaged at their own field centre agree with CASA either way; off-centre and multi-field cubes under a density weighting differ from CASA's weights by this mechanism.

    Measured on a mosaic (2026-11-03, #818, CASA 6.7.6.14, CPU f64). On a mosaic a row's uvw is MosaicFT's reprojection (girarUVW, in which w does not reach u and v), so casa-rs bins and looks up a mosaic cube's density there, while CASA bins at GridFT's rotation and looks up at the MeasurementSet uvw. On refim_alma_mosaic.ms (three fields 7.5″ apart, three channels, 350×280 at 0.06″, mosweight=True, where each field's density is its own, BriggsCubeWeightor's multiField):

    • one field imaged at its own centre agrees with CASA on every product, the PSF to 1e-7 and .sumwt exactly, under uniform and Briggs weighting;
    • the same field imaged at its neighbour's centre differs, uniform weighting PSF NRMS 2.6e-2 and .sumwt 4.1e-4: 4,135 of its 130,032 (row, channel) weights differ from CASA's IMAGING_WEIGHT;
    • an evidence-only build that grids the density at GridFT's rotation of the MeasurementSet uvw and looks it up at the MeasurementSet uvw, as CASA does, matches all 130,032 of CASA's weights to 4.2e-10;
    • the three fields together, at the test's centre (the middle field's): PSF NRMS 9.3e-3 under uniform weighting, 5.6e-4 under Briggs 0.5 (2.6e-2 is sparser: each field's density holds a third of the samples); with one density over all fields (mosweight=False), Briggs PSF 3.9e-4.

    So this ALMA mosaic cube's differences under a density weighting, with or without mosweight, are this entry's mechanism (#789 records the seven-pointing case). An EVLA mosaic cube (refim_oneshiftpoint.mosaic.ms, three one-channel windows) differs from CASA under uniform weighting even for one field at its own centre, with or without mosweight (PSF NRMS 3.6e-3, ten .pb mask pixels), where the standard gridder agrees to 1e-7; this mechanism does not explain it. The terminal density cell described under #789 has a separate cause.

    The EVLA refim_mawproject.ms alternate-PSF cube in #1062 also exercises per-channel Briggs density. The raw PSF NRMS is 0.01356, whether it uses the requested image centre or a separate PSF centre. Disabling per-channel density brings both PSFs below 1e-7. With per-channel density on, replaying the 13,608 CASA row weights for each centre independently brings both sets of eight image products within 2.14e-6 NRMS, with exact masks and sums of weights. The native density grid and lookup still use the same frame.

    The exact external-table Venus cube in #818 uses briggsbwtaper and mosweight=True. CASA adds two guard planes at each end of the REST density axis. The matched probe across seven fields compared 21,000 weights. With CASA's GridFT accumulation and stored-UVW lookup (using the stored frequency for the Float coordinates and bandwidth distance), the weights agree within 3.73e−8 absolute and 8.60e−8 normalized RMS. GridFT accumulation alone still differs by 1.35e−2 in field 5. Replaying the complete captured CASA weights independently matches the weighted cube's seven products within 1.26e−6 per plane, with exact masks and sum weights and matching WCS, controller counts and beams. The native single-frame weights give residual NRMS 2.10e−2 and 83 cutoff crossings against the raw CASA weights. Applying the same rotated coordinates to both binning and lookup preserves a sample's contribution to its own cell. This is the same two-frame exception as the fixed-source ALMA mosaics, now verified on the moving-source bandwidth-taper path.

    simanalyze's own cube images meet it with one pointing too (2026-11-03, the simulation round trip, #980). simobserve centres a pointing on the model's centre pixel. For core5ps.skymodel's 1873 pixels of 0.043″, that is half a pixel, 0.0215″ west and north, from the reference direction simanalyze images at. CASA's simobserve and casa-rs's put it at the same direction. Under simanalyze's Briggs call on a 4-channel cube, the PSF differs from CASA's by 3.4e-4 and .sumwt by 1.2e-5 at that phase centre. At the pointing's own direction, they agree to 1.2e-7 and 5.6e-8, and the clean matches iteration for iteration (900, model 1.7e-7). The mosaic of the same cube differs by 3.3e-3 under Briggs and agrees within 1e-6 under natural weighting.

    simanalyze TP seed and feather PSF displays¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 deletes cell_asec after SF TP gridding, then uses it to plot an interferometric PSF in the same invocation. A TP seed plus showpsf=True raises UnboundLocalError. The graphics oracle restores only that display scale from the selected image cell; native graphics use the PSF image's WCS.

    After feathering, CASA changes imagename to the feathered stem, then tries to open <stem>.feather.psf, which feather does not create. The oracle and native figures display the original synthesis PSF. Numerical imaging and feather calculations are unchanged.

    The simalma combination figure deletes existing flat and convolved model images as part of drawing its panels. Native plotting uses private copies for the summary calculation and preserves the numerical products, following the accepted simulation plan.

    simalma configuration names and zero-minute TP exposures¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14's task_simalma.py records an unknown cycle as integer -1 then compares it with string '-1'. A valid ALMA/ACA configuration whose name has no cycle number raises TypeError on Python 3. Native simalma classifies the configuration directly; the independent oracle corrects only that comparison. The compact ALMA/ACA integration case exercises these names.

    The task formats TP exposure with %dmin. A positive tptime below one minute becomes zero and cannot observe its planned pointings. Native simalma refuses that request before replacing a project. Whole-minute truncation for valid TP exposures is preserved.

    CASA omits the final row and column of its cube density grid¶

    BriggsCubeWeightor::fillImgWeightCol builds its density with GridFT. In FTMachine::findGridSector, a sector reaching the grid's right edge sets its width to nxp - x0 - 1 while x0 is zero-based. The function then makes the origin one-based for the Float kernel sectggrids, which accumulates through x0 + nxsub - 1. The final in-band column is excluded. The same calculation omits the final row. The weightor can look up those cells, but their accumulated density is zero and the visibility gets zero weight.

    On CASA 6.7.6.14, field 0 of refim_oneshiftpoint.mosaic.ms imaged at its own centre as a 512² uniform-weighted mosaic cube at 10 arcsec has two affected rows, one each in spectral windows 1 and 2. They map to the last column. CASA's captured row weights are zero for both, while casa-rs gives each 0.5. Changing only the cell size to 9.9 arcsec puts the same rows in interior columns and CASA gives both 0.5. The natural mosaic control agrees directly. The standard uniform control agrees because those rows fall outside its gridding kernel's full support.

    Casa-rs keeps the final in-band row and column and rejects cells outside the density grid. An evidence-only replay of the captured CASA weights through native gridding reproduces the EVLA mosaic to 7.32e-7 normalized RMS per plane or better, with exact masks, including the ten pixels affected by the dropped weights (#789).

    Continuum briggsabs at robust 0 and noise 0 weighs every visibility infinite¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#980)
    • Affected code (CASA 6.7.6.14):
    • casatools/src/code/synthesis/ImagerObjects/SynthesisImagerVi2.cc (weight)
    • casatools/src/code/msvis/MSVis/VisImagingWeight.cc (the VisibilityIterator2 constructor and weightUniform)

    Absolute Briggs weighting (weighting='briggsabs', rmode = "abs") divides each weight by D·f² + d² with f² = robust² and d² = 2·noise². With robust = 0 and noise = 0 both vanish. For a cube with perchanweightdensity, SynthesisImagerVi2::weight refuses the pair ("Absolute Briggs formula does not allow for robust 0 and estimated noise per visibility 0") before it builds the BriggsCubeWeightor; every other density (a continuum image, or a cube without perchanweightdensity) goes to VisImagingWeight, which has no such check, so weightUniform divides every weight by zero and the imaging weights are infinite.

    casa-rs refuses the pair in every mode, at the tclean reader (noise), the compiled weighting contract and the operator (WeightingGeneration::absolute_density).

    MultiTermFTNew forms Taylor imaging weights in single precision¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-11; metric approved 2026-10-15
    • Status: CASA rounding; casa-rs does not follow it (#690)
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines2/MultiTermFTNew.cc (modifyVisWeights, modifyModelVis)

    modifyVisWeights holds each channel frequency and the Taylor variable (ν − ν₀) / ν₀ in Float, and multiplies the Float imaging weights by its powers. The odd moments of .sumwt are sums that nearly cancel (two channels at x ≈ ±1/3 leave Σw·x ≈ −0.05 of Σ|w·x| ≈ 8·10⁴), so this rounding shows as a 1 % relative difference in .sumwt.tt1 on refim_twopoints_twochan. An emulation of both codes' arithmetic reproduces both published values to every stored digit; casa-rs, which rounds the Taylor variable to f32 once and accumulates in f64, is 0.06 % from the exact sum, CASA 0.93 %. No image reads the odd moments' sums.

    casa-rs keeps its arithmetic. The T1.5 comparison judges an odd moment k by |Δ| / √(tt_{k−1} · tt_{k+1}) and every other .sumwt by its relative difference, both against 1e-6, and leaves .sumwt out of a case's image NRMS. The narrow-band mosaic MT-MFS rows (three channels at 340 GHz) still miss that bound at about 5e-6 and are listed exceptions until an emulation confirms the same mechanism there and in the odd-moment weight images.

    Facets of an image size that does not divide¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-15
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#664)
    • Affected code: synthesis/ImagerObjects/SIImageStore.cc (makeSubImage)

    A facet of an n-pixel image split N ways is n / N pixels (integer division) from facet · (n / N), so when N does not divide n the top and right remainder strips belong to no facet: CASA grids and cleans nothing there, silently. casa-rs refuses such a layout (NonDivisibleFacetLayout).

    An unflagged non-finite sample turns the image to NaN¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (a NaN image loses the product, so casa-rs corrects it)
    • Affected code: CASA's gridding path, which accumulates unflagged samples without testing them for finiteness

    CASA grids a NaN or infinite visibility, weight or UVW in an unflagged sample like any other, and the transform spreads it, so the products come out NaN everywhere without naming the sample. casa-rs fails the run instead (FiniteValuePolicy::RejectUnflaggedInput, and PrepareError::NonFiniteUvw for UVW found while compiling), naming the row, channel and correlation so the sample can be flagged. Flagged samples and grid padding are skipped, as in CASA. The CASA behaviour is as ARCHITECTURE.md has recorded it since the review of #792; it has not been rerun against CASA for this entry.

    tclean images DATA when CORRECTED_DATA is missing, and says nothing¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows, with a warning (simulation plan §3, decision 6)
    • Affected code: synthesis/ImagerObjects/SIMapperCollection.cc (grid), synthesis/TransformMachines2/FTMachine.cc (makeImage), synthesis/ImagerObjects/SynthesisImagerVi2.cc (selectData)

    datacolumn='corrected' sets FTMachine::CORRECTED in selectData without looking at the MeasurementSet. When a VisBuffer has no corrected visibilities, SIMapperCollection::grid and FTMachine::makeImage switch to OBSERVED, so tclean images DATA (less the model in a major cycle). SynthesisImager::selectData once logged "CORRECTED_DATA column does not exist. Using DATA column instead"; that block is commented out, and CASA 6.7.6's libcasacpp_synthesis does not contain the message, so CASA logs no message for the fallback.

    casa-rs's simulator writes DATA only, so its imager follows the fallback and logs CASA's old message as a warning, since the column imaged is not the one asked for.

    VisibilityIterator2's existsColumn also counts CORRECTED_DATA as missing in an iteration chunk whose first row's cell is undefined (isDefined(0) on the chunk's attached table), so CASA can image DATA chunk by chunk from a partly written column. casa-rs decides by the column's presence alone and images an existing CORRECTED_DATA as before. That is a deliberate scope limit, not full parity: casa-tables' metadata-only probe (array_cell_is_defined_uncached) answers only for lazy, disk-backed TiledShapeStMan columns, and one probe would not reproduce the per-chunk rule.

    The Airy beam's blockage term uses the inverse length ratio¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: likely CASA bug; casa-rs follows it (#841, PR #843)
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines/PBMath1DAiry.cc (fillPBArray)

    For a dish of diameter D with a central blockage of diameter b, the voltage pattern is proportional to D²·jinc(x) − b²·jinc(x·b/D), where jinc(x) = 2J₁(x)/x and x = πDθ/λ. fillPBArray tabulates (areaRatio·jinc(x) − jinc(x·lengthRatio)) / (areaRatio − 1) with lengthRatio = D/b, so it evaluates the blockage term at x·D/b instead of x·b/D.

    For ALMA's default pattern (10.7 m, 0.75 m blockage), the power in the main lobe comes out about 1% above the physical pattern: 0.507 against 0.500 at the physical half-power radius, and 0.901 against 0.892 at 42% of that radius. Every Airy pattern CASA builds this way is affected: the simulator's setvp, imaging's primary beams and the mosaic gridder's HetArrayConvFunc dishes.

    ALMA's 10.7 m effective diameter was chosen against CASA's beam, so casa-rs tabulates the same expression (casa_numerics::AnnularApertureVoltageTable) and its predictions match CASA's to 1.6e-7. The simulator's beam test checks CASA's formula rather than the physical one.

    The simulator stores TAI as UTC¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-20
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#841, plan decision 5)
    • Affected code: ms/MSOper/NewMSSimulator.cc (observe, lines 1262-1280)

    observe converts the reference epoch to TAI and adds its seconds to the start and stop offsets, then writes the result into TIME, whose MEASINFO says UTC. The data therefore start TAI − UTC at the reference epoch after the requested epoch (37 s since 2017, 35 s in 2014). The geometry is computed with the stored number read as UT1 (line 1546), so CASA's data are self-consistent; only the schedule is shifted.

    casa-rs stores the requested UTC, and also reads the stored TIME as UT1 for the geometry, so its uvw agree with CASA's at equal stored TIME. A parity run requests a start later by TAI − UTC at the reference epoch on the casa-rs side, taken from the measures data and never written in as a number, and compares rows at equal TIME (tools/simulation/compare_sm_ms.py).

    The same offset separates the calibration tables the two simulators write (setgain and setleakage with table=): CASA's TIME is TAI under the UTC label. sm.setapply of a CASA-simulated table to a casa-rs MeasurementSet, or the reverse, therefore takes its solutions TAI − UTC away (37 s today), several solutions of 10 s integrations. The corrupt-setapply-* scenarios agree with CASA because their runs are shifted as above; tables solved from real data carry true UTC and are not affected.

    The simulator charges shadowing to the antenna in front¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: likely CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841)
    • Affected code: ms/MSOper/NewMSSimulator.cc (blockage, lines 1780-1815)

    A row's uvw is R·(X₁ − X₂), so w > 0 puts antenna 1 nearer the source. blockage zeroes antenna 2's blocked fraction when w > 0 and antenna 1's when w < 0, which leaves the blockage on the antenna in front, the one that does the blocking. CASA's own flagger (FlagAgentShadow, since casapy 4.1) flags the antenna behind. casa-rs's simulator flags the antenna behind (casa_ms::shadowing::ShadowedAntenna::Behind); the CASA convention remains available as CasaSimulatorFront.

    The compact-array scheduling cases in tools/simulation/simobserve_parity.py retain the raw task projects under casa-raw. Their independent control uses CASA's agentflagger tool to clear flags and apply its behind-antenna shadow agent, then predicts again so newly unflagged rows carry the sky signal. Calling the tool avoids adding unrelated task HISTORY to the reference.

    setspwindow relabels every spectral window's frame¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841)
    • Affected code: ms/MSOper/NewMSSimulator.cc (initSpWindows, line 750)

    Each setspwindow call ends with measFreqRef().fillColumn(freqType), which writes the new window's frame into MEAS_FREQ_REF for every row of SPECTRAL_WINDOW. A TOPO window followed by an LSRK one ends with both labelled LSRK, although their frequencies were given in different frames. casa-rs writes each window's own frame.

    setconfig accepts coordinate systems it cannot convert¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841)
    • Affected code: ms/MSOper/NewMSSimulator.cc (initAnt, lines 452-456; longlat2global, lines 566-575)

    For coordsystem='longlat', longlat2global logs SEVERE "not yet implemented"; for an unknown name, initAnt logs SEVERE "Unknown coordinate system type". In both cases the antennas are added at the Earth's centre and setconfig returns True, so the script continues with an array whose geometry is meaningless. In casa-rs, longlat raises RuntimeError and an unknown name logs CASA's message and returns False; neither adds antennas.

    The tools convert quantities whose units do not conform¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841)
    • Affected code: casatools' argument conversion (casaQuantity, casaMDirection) used by simulator_cmpt.cc and the other tools

    A tool argument such as integrationtime or freq is read as a quantity and converted to the unit the method needs without checking that the units conform. CASA 6.7.6 takes integrationtime='10Jy' as 1e-25 s, integrationtime='10m' (metres, perhaps meant as minutes) as 10 s and freq='1s' as 1 Hz. A direction string whose second or third token does not read keeps the previous value, so 'J2000 12h00m00 garbage' has a declination of 180°. casa-rs refuses both with casacore's "From/to units not consistent" or a parse error. The conversions CASA defines between dimensions are kept: a dimensionless number is the SI unit, an angle is a time at 360° a day, and a length is a frequency through c.

    Simulator calls before setconfig abort the process¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841)
    • Affected code: ms/MSOper/NewMSSimulator.cc (initFeeds, observe)

    setfeed or observe before any setconfig builds a table slice over zero antennas. casacore's array code throws std::runtime_error (an empty Slicer), which the tool binding does not catch, and the Python process aborts. casa-rs raises RuntimeError from setfeed, and observe returns False with "Antenna information not yet defined".

    A zero integration time adds rows without bound¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841)
    • Affected code: simulator_cmpt.cc (settimes, line 221); ms/MSOper/NewMSSimulator.cc (observe)

    settimes asserts only that the integration time is not negative, and settimes() with no arguments sets 0 s. observe then divides the scan's span by zero to count integrations; over a nonzero span this adds billions of rows before the disk fills. casa-rs's observe returns False for an integration time that is not positive ("observe: the integration time must be positive").

    simutil.readantenna averages a stale loop variable¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py (readantenna, lines 1783-1791)

    When a configuration file gives absolute coordinates, names no known observatory and has no COFA header, readantenna means to place the observatory at the mean antenna position. It averages x, y and z, which are the loop variables of the UTM branch, rather than the antenna lists stnx, stny and stnz. For an XYZ file the names are unbound and the call raises UnboundLocalError; for a UTM file the observatory becomes the last antenna. It then assigns xyz2long's (longitude, latitude, height) to (latitude, longitude, height), so the two angles are swapped. casa-rs converts the mean antenna position, as the comment intends (casa_simulation::readantenna, which lists its smaller divergences).

    simutil.readantenna names antennas after blank lines¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py (readantenna)

    readantenna skips a line with fewer than four values, but still appends a default A%02d pad and antenna name for it, so the names run ahead of the positions by one for every such line. CASA's own WSRT.cfg has two blank lines, so readantenna returns 16 names for 14 antennas, starting A00, A01, RT0, and a CASA simulation of WSRT names every dish wrongly. casa-rs skips such lines entirely.

    The measures tool converts with a frame at MJD 0 and the geocentre¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA behaviour; casa-rs does not follow it (#841)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/tools/measures/measures_cmpt.cc (the measures constructor)

    The measures tool starts with a frame of a default epoch (MJD 0 UTC), a default position (the zero vector) and a default direction (the J2000 pole). A conversion that needs a frame element me.doframe has not set uses these without a warning, so me.measure(me.direction('J2000', '12h00m00', '45d00m00'), 'AZEL') in a fresh tool returns an azimuth and elevation for 1858-11-17 at the centre of the Earth (casacore only notes, at INFO, that MJD 0 is outside its Earth-orientation table). After me.done() the frame is empty and the same call fails with "Cannot convert due to missing frame information". casa-rs's tool starts with the empty frame, so such a conversion always fails with that message until doframe sets the element.

    sm.predict rewrites the model image¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#841)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Simulator.cc (createSkyEquation, lines 2319-2344)

    createSkyEquation opens each model as a PagedImage<Float>, gives its coordinate system an LSRK spectral conversion and Hz world units ("FTMachine only works in Hz and LSRK") and stores it back with setCoordinateInfo, so predicting rewrites the model's table.dat. Later reads of the model, including another predict, see coordinates the user did not write; the CASA oracle (tools/simulation/casa_sm_observe_oracle.py --model-root) copies the model for every call because of it. casa-rs converts the spectral axis to Hz in memory and never writes the model.

    sm.predict accepts models the simulator cannot read¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-21
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#841)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/tools/simulator/simulator_cmpt.cc (predict, lines 620-629); synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Simulator.cc (createSkyEquation, lines 2313-2319 and 2568-2572; predict, lines 2161-2163)

    casatools' binding checks each model with ImageFactory::fromFile, which also opens FITS images and complex-valued CASA images. The simulator then reads each model as a PagedImage<Float>. A FITS file is not a table, so it logs "… is unreadable" and then "No model images found"; a complex image throws when opened as Float, which createSkyEquation catches ("Caught exception: …"). Either way sm.predict logs "Failed to create SkyEquation" and returns False, and the script continues with DATA not predicted. These outcomes are read from the source and were not probed. casa-rs's check opens each model as the simulator reads it, so a FITS or complex model raises RuntimeError "Unable to open image …" at the call.

    IAU 2000 keeps the IAU 1980 sidereal time and ecliptic obliquities¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-22
    • Status: casacore behaviour; casa-rs follows it for parity (#850)
    • Affected code: measures/Measures/MCEpoch.cc (initConvert, UT1_GAST), measures/Measures/MeasMath.cc (applyMECLIPtoJMEAN, applyTECLIPtoJTRUE), measures/Measures/MeasTable.cc (fundArg)

    With measures.iau2000.b_use set, casacore still forms GAST from the IAU 1982 GMST and the IAU 1980 equation of the equinoxes of Nutation::STANDARD, without the 1994 complementary terms, and every HADEC, AZEL and ITRF conversion takes its LAST from that GAST. TECLIPTIC rotates by the IAU 1980 true obliquity and MECLIPTIC by MeasTable::fundArg(0), under either model. That polynomial's T² term is −0.0059″, where IAU 1976 has −0.00059″: 0.37 mas in MECLIPTIC by 2026. casa-rs computes all of these as casacore does (casacore_sidereal, casacore_frames), which moved its IAU 2000A J2000→HADEC from 20.4 to 16.7 mas off casacore, the residual of the APP step alone.

    Direction latitudes come from an unnormalized vector¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-22
    • Status: casacore numerical defect; casa-rs does not follow it (#850)
    • Affected code: measures/Measures/MeasData.cc (GALtoJ2000, GALtoB1950, MToB1950, MToJ2000), measures/Measures/MCDirection.cc (doConvert), casa/Quanta/MVDirection.cc (getLat, get)

    casacore's galactic and FK4 matrices are 10-digit literals, orthogonal only to about 7e-11 (1.5e-8 for MToB1950(4)), and MCDirection::doConvert does not renormalize after applying them. MVDirection::getLat() and get() then take asin(z), assuming a unit vector, so the latitude casacore reports is off by about (|v| − 1)·tan(b) and becomes NaN once z exceeds 1. casa-rs converts with the same matrices, renormalizes, and reports the latitude of the unit vector. The two directions agree to 0.01 µas; the reported angles differ by up to 51 µas for B1950→GALACTIC on the law's grid and by 1.6 mas for J2000→B1950 at b = −89.77°, where tan(b) is 250 (measures_frames_interop).

    IAU 2000 JMEAN to J2000 applies bias and precession in the wrong order¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-22
    • Status: casacore bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#850)
    • Affected code: measures/Measures/MeasMath.cc (deapplyPrecession)

    Under IAU 2000, applyPrecession takes J2000 to JMEAN as in *= B; in *= P, v′ = Pᵀ·Bᵀ·v, with B the frame bias and P the precession. The inverse, deapplyPrecession, applies in = B·in and then in = P·in, which is P·B·v′, not B·P·v′. The two do not commute, so casacore's JMEAN→J2000 is not the inverse of its J2000→JMEAN: MECLIPTIC→J2000 and TECLIPTIC→J2000 are 0.14 mas off in 2026, and the error grows with time from J2000. casa-rs inverts exactly; the frame law compares the IAU 2000 ecliptics from the equator of date.

    IAU 2000 GMST1 adds the TT day fraction¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-22
    • Status: casacore bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#850)
    • Affected code: measures/Measures/MCEpoch.cc (UT1_GMST1, GMST1_UT1), measures/Measures/MeasTable.cc (GMST00)

    Under IAU 2000, UT1_GMST1 turns the UT1 epoch into TT and adds GMST00/(2π) and 6713 days to it. GMST00 is already the sidereal time of day (the Earth rotation angle in [0, 2π) plus precession), so the TT day fraction is counted twice: UT1 51544.5 gives GMST1 58258.280, against the IAU 1976 path's 58257.779 and a GAST of 58257.779, and LMST inherits the error. GMST1_UT1's iteration does not undo it: GMST1 58258.280 converts back to UT1 51544.999. casa-rs uses the IAU 1976 GMST1 under either model, as casacore's own GAST does, so its GMST1, LMST, GAST and LAST agree with each other and, under IAU 1976, with casacore.

    Checking all components of an empty list exits CASA¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA exits; casa-rs raises a catchable error (owner approved, #1158)
    • Affected code: componentlist::_checkIndices and componentlist::isphysical

    CASA 6.7.6-14 exits its Python process when componentlist().isphysical() checks the default [-1] selection on an empty list. The index helper expands [-1] into an empty vector and still reads its first element. An isolated Mac mini run ended with status 255 and no Python exception. casa-rs raises a RuntimeError so a caller can recover. An explicitly empty selection, [], returns True, as in CASA. The owner approved a catchable error on 2026-11-05.

    Component selection aborts for non-finite or cyclic inputs¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA crash casa-rs corrects (#1176)
    • Affected code: CASA componentlist.select Python binding

    With three point components, CASA 6.7.6.14 terminates an isolated Python process with signal 11 for cl.select(float("nan")), cl.select(float("inf")), or a list that contains itself. By contrast, cl.select([float("nan")]) selects index 0, and a 100-level acyclic nested list containing 1 selects index 1. The isolated probe and its receipt are tools/simulation/componentlist_selection_crashes_oracle.py and crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_componentlist_selection_crashes.json.

    casa-rs raises ValueError for scalar non-finite or cyclic selections and leaves the selection unchanged. It continues to accept nested acyclic lists and follows the recorded list-wrapped numeric conversions. This corrects the process crash: scripts can catch the exception and retain their session.

    Writing a limb-darkened disk without an exponent aborts CASA¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#841)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/code/components/ComponentModels/ComponentList.cc (writeTable, lines 1016-1022; readTable, lines 1155-1161)

    A component list holding a limb-darkened disk gets an Optional_Parameters column, and writeTable puts each such component's optional parameters in it; the first is the disk's limb-darkening exponent. A limb-darkened disk made through cl.fromrecord without optionalParameters has none, and writing it throws casacore::indexError from the ComponentList destructor, which terminates the process (probed with CASA 6.7.6: fromrecord and rename succeed, close aborts). Had the empty vector been stored, readTable would read its first element out of bounds. casa-rs leaves the cell undefined, which CASA reads as the shape's default exponent of 0.05, as it reads every other row the column does not describe.

    A negative component turns positive under the primary beam¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs does not follow (#841)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/code/synthesis/TransformMachines/BeamSkyJones.cc (apply(SkyComponent&, …), lines 488-511)

    With sm.setvp(dovp=True), SkyEquation::applySkyJones applies the beam to each component with fullspectral set. That path multiplies the beam-applied flux, which keeps the component's sign, by a per-channel factor of the spectrum's sample over abs(flux), which carries the sign again, so a negative flux comes out positive. Probed with CASA 6.7.6: a −0.4 Jy point 20 arcsec from the pointing centre predicts −0.4 Jy without the beam and +0.4 Jy times the beam with it. casa-rs keeps the sign; sm_components_scenarios.json keeps negative components away from the beam.

    The same defect doubles the phase of complex correlations. In a linear window, a component with nonzero Stokes U and V has XY = U+iV. The beam path multiplies each channel's sampled XY by the original XY/abs(XY), rotating it again; YX acquires the conjugate rotation. In the SIM-10 VP-table controls, both points have the same original Stokes flux [1, 0.1, 0.02, 0.03] Jy. CASA's auto-baseline crosshands show exactly this extra unit-phase factor, while I and Q agree. casa-rs preserves the requested correlation phase. sm_voltage_patterns_oracle.py retains raw CASA rows and supplies corrected rows by dividing selected correlations by that original unit-phase factor. No scientific tolerance changes; the remaining documented LSRK-label error is below the existing 1e-3 threshold in these controls.

    The interferometric simobserve control if_joint_polarized also exercises this defect in circular products and the joint-sum defect described below. It keeps the raw project, predicts its image and component separately with CASA, removes the component's extra original unit-phase factor, and adds the independent terms. The component is a single constant-spectrum source; the reference asserts that domain rather than applying one phase to an arbitrary component list.

    An uncovered first mosaic image suppresses a valid model¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 accepts sm.predict(imagename=[outside, inside]) but produces zero DATA when all pointings lie outside the first image, even though the second model alone produces signal. sm_mosaic_coverage_oracle.py retains that raw result and captures the valid model alone in a fresh simulator. Its outside-only control is zero, so linearity independently gives the expected sum. casa-rs tests coverage for each image and preserves the valid contribution. The unchanged 1e-3 threshold applies to the actual C++ control. This is a data-loss defect casa-rs corrects under SIM-10 (#1297).

    Saved Jones-image beams lose or reverse image polarization¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-14
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs does not follow (#1297, SIM-10)
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines/PBMath2DImage.cc, applyJonesFastX and apply(SkyComponent&, ...) in CASA 6.7.6.14.

    The component path reverses its input and output correlations around the Jones product. The image path constructs the same reversed Jones matrix but applies it directly to the image correlations. An asymmetric voltage matrix therefore has different polarization effects on an image and on its component representation. A one-Stokes sky also retains only one beam-applied correlation, losing induced Q/U/V. Giving CASA four Stokes planes with Q/U/V zero changes the prediction even though the sky is the same. In the named controls, the original one-Stokes image produces equal XX/YY and zero crosshands, while the equivalent four-plane sky has unequal parallel hands and nonzero crosshands.

    casa-rs applies the same coherency product to image and component signals and grids all four resulting Stokes planes. sm_image_beams_oracle.py retains the raw CASA rows. Its independent image control supplies the equivalent four-plane sky and reverses the beam correlation values to compensate for CASA's image indexing. Those actual C++ rows are the corrected reference, with the usual 1e-3 threshold unchanged. The scalar real, real/imaginary and complex beam controls require no such correction. The total-power controls in sm_total_power_oracle.py retain this correction for a nonzero I/Q/U/V sky, including an ordinary GridFT control (#1299).

    PBMath2DImage::updateJones also returns its cached beam when the sky coordinates and shape are unchanged, without testing the pointing or parallactic angle. An A/B/A observation separated by four hours therefore keeps the first visit's attenuation for every field. casa-rs refreshes the beam on each pointing/field transition and retains the visit's orientation when reusing prediction grids. The real_image_revisit control preserves CASA's raw cached output and compares with actual CASA predictions made using a fresh simulator for each visit, removing that stale cache. The same cache suppresses within-scan orientation refresh: the four-integration sm_image_beam_refresh_oracle.py control preserves the raw output and uses a fresh simulator for each integration. casa-rs refreshes when the requested angle threshold is crossed; the independent refreshed signal differs from a frozen beam by 0.56%.

    EVLA component dosquint is a no-op¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 accepts sm.setvp(dosquint=True) for EVLA, but logs "No Beam Squint". The synthetic 14.5 GHz controls in sm_squint_oracle.py produce identical DATA with squint disabled, enabled and a 0.01 degree angle-refresh threshold. The corresponding VLA controls change by 0.00760 and 0.00923 NRMS, so the workload measures both effects.

    PBMath1DEVLA declares its own private squint_p; its frequency-selection code updates that member, while the inherited PBMath1D::apply reads the base class's zero offset. casa-rs preserves the observed EVLA response for CASA equivalence. Applying a physically different EVLA correction would change existing results and is outside this comparison target. VLA band offsets and its angle-refresh behavior are implemented separately.

    A single spectral index is read past its end¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs does not follow (#841)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/code/components/ComponentModels/SpectralIndex.cc (sampleStokes, lines 138-146 and 213-237); components/ComponentModels/SkyCompRep.cc (visibility, line 314)

    cl.addcomponent(spectrumtype="spectral index", index=α) stores one index. The per-frequency sampleStokes scales only I by it and leaves Q, U and V alone; the matrix sampleStokes, which SkyCompRep::visibility and so every component prediction uses, has no one-index branch and reads indices 1 to 3 out of bounds. For an unpolarised component the out-of-bounds factors multiply zeros. Read from the source and not probed: a polarised component with a single index predicts Q, U and V scaled by whatever follows the vector. casa-rs reads a single index as [α, 0, 0, 0], so the fractional polarisation is constant, as the matrix sampler's comments describe; sm_components_scenarios.json gives its polarised components no index.

    The primary beam's component spectrum labels topocentric samples LSRK¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#841)
