    • Affected code: casatools/src/code/synthesis/TransformMachines/BeamSkyJones.cc (apply(SkyComponent&, …), lines 494-529); components/ComponentModels/SkyCompRep.cc (visibility, lines 303-314)

    The full-spectral beam path evaluates the beam and the spectrum at each channel's topocentric frequency, stores the products in a TabularSpectrum at the channels' LSRK frequencies, and labels it LSRK. SkyCompRep::visibility then looks the table up at the topocentric frequencies, which it takes to be in the component's frame, so each channel reads the table shifted by the observatory's velocity, up to about 1e-4 of the frequency. casa-rs evaluates the beam and the spectrum at each channel's own frequency. With the beam, sm_components_scenarios.json agrees with CASA to 7e-5 and 1e-4 normalized RMS, against 1.3e-5 or better without it: consistent in size with this shift, though the shift was not isolated.

    cl.addcomponent stores four flux values as Stokes¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs follows it (#841, owner decision 2026-10-23)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/tools/componentlist/componentlist_cmpt.cc (setflux, lines 831-930); components/ComponentModels/Flux.tcc (FluxRep::setValue(const Vector<T>&), lines 363-368)

    setflux, which addcomponent calls, sets the flux's representation from polarization and then stores four values with FluxRep::setValue(Vector<Double>), which resets the representation to Stokes. So cl.addcomponent(flux=[1, 0.1, 0.2, 0.9], polarization='circular') stores I = 1, Q = 0.1, U = 0.2, V = 0.9 rather than RR, RL, LR and LL (probed with CASA 6.7.6 for 'Circular', 'circular', 'CIRCULAR' and 'linear'). polarization still has effects: an unknown name raises, and one value with a representation other than Stokes raises. casa-rs does the same, so a script gets the list CASA would give it.

    cl.addcomponent stores the flux in single precision¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#841)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/tools/componentlist/componentlist_cmpt.cc (setflux, lines 858-870)

    setflux reads the values into a Vector<Complex> (single precision) before storing them as doubles, so flux=0.1 is stored as 0.10000000149011612 (probed with CASA 6.7.6). casa-rs keeps the values given; they differ from CASA's by at most one single-precision rounding, 6e-8 relative.

    The cl tool records a shape's axes in the units it was given¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows (#1187)
    • Affected code: components/ComponentModels/TwoSidedShape.cc (toRecord, lines 265-330)

    TwoSidedShape remembers the units its axes were set in, and its record gives them in those units: a Gaussian made by addcomponent with majoraxis='1arcsec' records {'unit': 'arcsec', 'value': 1.0}. A shape read from a table has the units readTable sets: arcmin for the axes and degrees for the position angle, with the errors in radians. casa-rs keeps the input units while the component remains in memory, and uses the table units after reopening. It previously always reported the table units, which have the same physical value but differ in the record. The owner approved matching CASA's in-memory units on 2026-11-05.

    cl.addcomponent keeps a point for a shape it cannot make¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows (#841)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/tools/componentlist/componentlist_cmpt.cc (addcomponent, lines 453-526; setshape, lines 1285-1386)

    addcomponent adds a point and then calls setshape, ignoring what it returns. setshape logs SEVERE and returns False for a shape name it does not know, for a limb-darkened disk ("not implemented yet"), and for an axis that is not an angle (none given, a number, or another unit), so the component stays a point at the given direction and addcomponent returns True (probed with CASA 6.7.6). casa-rs does the same and logs the message at ERROR. Scripts that misspell a shape name therefore get points, as they do in CASA.

    What setshape raises, addcomponent raises too, after removing the component it began: an axis that does not read as a quantity ('garbage', a complex number), and what the shape's constructor raises, such as a Gaussian's position angle outside ±2π (Gaussian2DParam::setPA) or axes that are not positive. A minor axis longer than the major raises as well: TwoSidedShape::fromRecord (TwoSidedShape.cc, lines 229-263) means to refuse it, but compares the minor axis with itself (near(minorAxisInRad, minorAxisInRad), line 249), so the check never fires and the constructor raises instead. casa-rs raises in each case with casacore's message, and adds nothing.

    setmosaicfield sits its grid off the field centre¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 2, plan §4 "Diverged and recorded")
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Simulator.cc (setmosaicfield, lines 849-901)

    setmosaicfield tests for an odd count with (nx/2)!=floor(nx/2) on integers. That is always false, so every grid takes the even-count offsets. Those offsets, (i − ceil(nx/2)) − 0.5 spacings in right ascension and (j − ceil(ny/2.0)) − 0.5 in declination, count i and j from zero where one was meant.

    Measured with CASA 6.7.6 at 1′ spacing: - 3 × 3: right ascension offsets −1.5, −0.5 and +0.5 spacings; declination offsets −2.5, −1.5 and −0.5. - 2 × 2: −1.5 and −0.5 spacings on both axes.

    A centred grid would be at −1, 0 and +1, and at ±0.5.

    Over a pole, CASA also sets the right ascension to |π − α|, where crossing the pole adds π.

    casa-rs centres every grid: offsets (i − (n − 1)/2) spacings, right ascension divided by the centre's cos δ. It crosses a pole as the sphere does. The fields keep CASA's names, {sourcename}_{k}, numbered down each column and then across.

    observemany pads one name with empty ones¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs follows its outcome (#841 Phase 2)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/tools/simulator/simulator_cmpt.cc (observemany, lines 318-326)

    casatools means one source name, or one direction, to stand for every pointing. It grows the list with Vector<String>::resize(nptg, true), which keeps the existing elements and default-constructs the rest, so the new entries are empty strings, not copies. With one name and several pointings, pointing 1's field is "". NewMSSimulator::observe raises "Field 1: not yet defined", and sm.observemany logs it and returns False (measured with CASA 6.7.6). With one direction, the later pointings have none, and each keeps the previous pointing's phase centre while the field stays the same.

    casars.tools.simulator pads the lists the same way, so it returns the same False, and moves only the first pointing. Unlike CASA, it adds no rows before refusing (next entry).

    observemany adds rows before it looks up later fields¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 2)
    • Affected code: ms/MSOper/NewMSSimulator.cc (observe, the vector form)

    observe adds every pointing's MAIN rows (ms_p->addRow(nNewRows)) before its pointing loop. It looks up the first pointing's field before that, but each later field only when the loop reaches it. A later field that is not defined raises with the rows already added: their FIELD_ID, TIME and UVW are unwritten, and the call returns False. casa-rs looks up every pointing's field before it plans anything, so the same call returns False with the same message and leaves no rows.

    sm.predict through WProjectFT without planes crashes the simulator¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 2)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Simulator.cc (createSkyEquation, line 2465); synthesis/TransformMachines/WPConvFunc.cc (findConvFunction, lines 164-176)

    The simulator builds its WProjectFT with no w statistics (minW, maxW and rmsW all −1). For setoptions(ftmachine='wproject', wprojplanes=…) below 1, findConvFunction takes its automatic branch, whose largest w then comes out negative and whose plane count comes out zero or negative. Measured on CASA 6.7.6: setoptions returns True, and predict with wprojplanes=0 or -1 kills the Python process with a segmentation fault (exit 139) after it logs "Fourier transforms will use specified common tangent point". casa-rs records the planes at setoptions, as CASA does, and its predict raises RuntimeError ("W projection needs at least two planes").

    What casa-rs follows of the same machine: - wprojplanes=1 returns False from predict with CASA's "Convolution function integral is not positive". - WProjectFT pads by 1, its constructor's default, whatever setoptions(padding=…) says. - Autocorrelations predict zero, since the machine is built with usezero off. - Rows beyond the largest w the planes reach (0.25/|cell|) predict zero.

    Mixed image/component prediction doubles components at flagged samples¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs corrects it (#1053, standing deviation policy)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/SkyEquation.cc (get of an image model) and synthesis/TransformMachines/GridFT.cc (get)

    CASA 6.7.6.14 first predicts components, then adds each image. SkyEquation::get saves the previous model cube, calls the image gridder in place, and adds the saved cube back. The gridder overwrites unflagged samples but leaves flagged samples and rows untouched. Those samples therefore contain the previous component twice. Independent component-only, image-only and joint predictions reproduce this for both replacing and incremental sm.predict after openfromms: a 0.25 Jy component at the field centre contributes 0.5 Jy to a flagged sample in the joint run, and 0.25 Jy on its own. At the second field, the same error doubles its complex visibility, including its phase.

    casa-rs masks the image prediction before adding components, so each component contributes once regardless of flags. Incremental prediction then adds the stored DATA once, and corruption still reaches flagged samples. This follows the intended sum of sky models without making flagged results depend on whether an image was supplied. The raw joint results and independent controls are in crates/casa-simulation/tests/data/sm_flagged_samples_casa.json.gz, generated by tools/simulation/sm_flagged_samples_oracle.py; tests/flagged_samples.rs checks both CASA's excess and the corrected independent sum.

    SDGrid predicts total power into cross-correlations with no positive uvw¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#841)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SDGrid.cc (get, the rowFlags loop)

    sm.predict after setoptions(ftmachine='sd') samples the model at each row's pointing. SDGrid::get leaves out a row whose uvw has a positive component (max(uvw) > 0), which is meant to keep the cross-correlations of a single-dish predict at zero. A cross-correlation whose u, v and w are all zero or negative passes that test and is given the pointing's total power as if it were one dish's autocorrelation. The original two-antenna probe saw only rows with a positive component. On 2026-11-11 an eight-antenna, two-pointing probe reproduced the defect in CASA 6.7.6.14: 56 of 112 cross-correlation rows contain nonzero total power. The other pointing samples zero brightness; there are also 32 autocorrelation rows. The raw DATA is retained in sm_multiple_models_casa.json.gz, generated by tools/simulation/sm_multiple_models_oracle.py. casa-rs predicts total power into autocorrelations only; the new test compares those directly with CASA and requires every cross-correlation sample to be exactly zero.

    GridBoth::get adds this same SDGrid result to its interferometric result. Its GridFT has autocorrelations disabled. casa-rs therefore uses single-dish signals on autocorrelations and interferometric signals on cross-correlations; sm_ft_modes_oracle.py retains raw joint output and uses independent GridFT cross-correlations as its corrected reference.

    The joint PB control also exposes failed-call data loss: with a 64-pixel image and gridding support 134, CASA returns false but erases selected DATA. casa-rs rejects the insufficient extent before recording the prediction and keeps the input DATA. The raw failure and the successful 256-pixel joint PB calculation are retained in the same fixture.

    The EVLA's beam table has no 11052 MHz fit¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: likely CASA bug; casa-rs follows it for parity (#841 Phase 2)
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines/PBMath1DEVLA.cc (init, line 320)

    PBMath1DEVLA::init fills its map of polynomial beam fits by frequency, in MHz, one 128 MHz step at a time. In X band the sequence runs 10948, then 10052, then 11180: the second 10052 was meant for 11052. Through std::map::operator[] it replaces the first 10052 fit, so the map has no 11052 entry. The 10052 MHz frequencies use the fit meant for 11052, and those near 11052 MHz take a neighbour's. The coefficients differ by about 1% in the linear term, which moves the beam by a few 1e-3 at its half power. casa-rs's table (casa_numerics::EvlaPowerPattern, shared by the simulator and imaging) is CASA's map as built.

    sm.close() aborts the process after a mosaic predict¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 2)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Simulator.cc (close, after createSkyEquation built MosaicFTNew with HetArrayConvFunc)

    After setoptions(ftmachine='mosaic') and a predict that returns True, CASA 6.7.6's sm.close() kills the Python process: libc++abi prints "Pure virtual function called!" and raises SIGABRT (exit 134 from a shell). simobserve's own sequence (setvp, setoptions(ftmachine='mosaic'), predict, close) aborts the same way. It was not traced to the destructor that makes the call. DATA is complete on disk when it happens: a rerun whose abort was turned into a quiet exit wrote the same DATA, UVW, FLAG and TIME bit for bit, and those MeasurementSets agree with casa-rs's to a few 1e-7 normalized RMS. casa-rs's close returns normally. The CASA oracle (tools/simulation/casa_sm_observe_oracle.py) runs such scenarios (close_aborts) in a child process, accepts the abort only when every predict returned True, and records close as aborted.

    The 2026-11-11 multi-image oracle reproduces the abort with CASA 6.7.6.14 after sm.done() as well. Predicting the same mosaic image twice also produced SIGBUS during cleanup on one run. The isolated child writes its reference DATA before cleanup; the parent accepts only these identified cleanup failures and records the process exit separately from prediction.

    Mosaic multi-image prediction depends on image order¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-11
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs corrects it under the standing CASA deviations rule (#1053)
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines/SimplePBConvFunc.cc (convIndex, storeImageParams) and HetArrayConvFunc.cc (findConvFunction), through the simulator's mosaic machine

    With CASA 6.7.6.14, sm.predict(imagename=[a, b]) through the mosaic machine gives a different result from predicting the two images separately and adding their DATA. The deterministic oracle uses a 64-square, single-plane image and a 96-square, four-plane image, at the same cell size and phase centre. Against the separately predicted sum, [a, b] differs by 0.00353 normalized RMS and [b, a] by 0.0326. Swapping the images changes DATA by 0.0307. [a, a] equals twice the separate prediction of a, so this is not a general failure to accumulate images.

    The public CASA source at 4ebd5b1508a5d31b74e7b5f6b89313368d30b9ef explains this failure: SimplePBConvFunc::convIndex keys the convolution cache by MeasurementSet and spectral window, omitting image geometry. storeImageParams updates the current image coordinates and dimensions, but does not invalidate the cached main convolution. HetArrayConvFunc then reuses it for the later model. This source inspection supports the diagnosis; the numerical reproduction above uses the installed 6.7.6.14 oracle rather than claiming that the public revision is its exact source.

    casa-rs prepares each image with its own geometry and adds the predictions. This follows SkyEquation::predict's evident intent to sum all model images and avoids silently corrupting DATA according to input order. The test retains the raw joint CASA outputs and checks that they still expose the defect. Its independent reference for these two cases is the sum of the separate CASA predictions, with the usual 0.001 normalized RMS limit; the limit is not relaxed to admit the defective joint output.

    The simulator's mosaic machine is not tclean's¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows for parity, per machine (#841 Phase 2, #875)
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines/HetArrayConvFunc.cc, SimplePBConvFunc.cc and MosaicFT.cc (the simulator's, through MosaicFTNew) against their synthesis/TransformMachines2/ namesakes (tclean's)

    sm.predict after setoptions(ftmachine='mosaic') degrids through the earlier heterogeneous-array machine, and tclean(gridder='mosaic') through the later one. They differ in: - the dish's support radius: 150″ at 100 GHz for every ALMA and ACA dish in the simulator; in tclean, max(150″, fov/5) for a 12 m dish and max(300″, fov/3) for any other; - the screen's least size: none in the simulator, a tenth of the image in tclean; - the frequency cells (findUsefulChannels): from the selected channels alone in the simulator, lround(span / 0.5 % of the top) cells matched in Float; stepping down from the window's top frequency in tclean; - the support search: in the simulator, inwards from the lattice's edge along the row and the column one cell off its centre, the radius found widened by √2; in tclean, outwards from the weight plane's peak; - the channel frequencies: the window's own in the simulator; tclean converts them to the image's frame (LSRK).

    Both grid every row of a window with the widest pair's support (MosaicFT hands sectgmosd2 and the degridders max(convSupportPlanes_p)), each pair normalised over its own; casa-rs took tclean's rows each with its own support until #980, which put mixed 12 m and 7 m tclean mosaics 2.5e-3 normalized RMS from CASA, and 1.8e-2 for the 7 m antennas of a mixed array, where they agree to 6e-7.

    On the #875 probe (six 12 m and six 7 m ALMA antennas over three fields at 230 GHz) CASA logs supports [6, 6, 6] for the simulator and [7, 6, 6] for tclean, and the two machines' predictions of one model differ by 4.5–5.6e-3 normalized RMS per dish-pair class on a TOPO MeasurementSet and 1.9–2.4e-3 on an LSRK one. casa-rs's mosaic set takes either machine's rules (casa_imaging_operator::MosaicRules): imaging follows tclean's, the simulator its own.

    Both machines reproject rows through FTMachine::girarUVW, whose new u and v take no share of w, where GridFT's rotateUVW applies the full rotation. At offsets of 10–17″ the two differ by a few 1e-4 cells, which flips the fine offset of a sample near a rounding boundary; with the mosaic's ten offsets a cell, such a sample moved by up to 10% (#841: 33 of 4,752 rows, until the simulator took girarUVW too).

    tclean's mosaic beams for a MeasurementSet know only the dishes met before it¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: casa-rs diverges (#980); one beam set over every MeasurementSet's dishes for every pass
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines2/HetArrayConvFunc.cc (findAntennaSizes, findConvFunction)

    With a list of MeasurementSets, findAntennaSizes adds each MeasurementSet's dish diameters to those of the ones the visibility iterator met before it, and findConvFunction rebuilds the beams of a MeasurementSet's window whenever the iterator moves to another MeasurementSet (msId_p != vb.msId()). In the first pass (the PSF and the weight image) the first MeasurementSet's beams therefore know only its own dishes, and in every later pass every MeasurementSet's: the screen size (the widest dish's support radius) and the gridding support (the widest pair's) of its rows change between passes, so the result depends on the order CASA visits the list in. casa-rs builds one set from every MeasurementSet's dishes and grids every pass with it.

    On CASA's own ALMA 12 m and ACA 7 m lists (test_het_mosaic_mfs_alma_listofms CAS-14255 and CAS-14350, 12 m data first, whose pair has the widest support) only the first pass's screen size differs: every product agrees to 1.3e-5 normalized RMS. With the 7 m data made earlier, so that CASA meets it first, the first pass grids the 7 m rows with the 7 m support alone, and the products differ by up to 5.4e-4; each MeasurementSet imaged alone agrees to 7e-7.

    A mosaic's weight image is transformed in single precision¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: casa-rs diverges (#980); its weight image is transformed in double precision, so a .pb pixel within single-precision rounding of pblimit can fall on the other side of CASA's mask
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines2/MosaicFT.cc (finalizeToSky: convertArray(griddedWeight, griddedWeight2), then ft_p.c2cFFT(*weightLattice) on the Complex grid)

    MosaicFT accumulates the gridded weights in DComplex but converts them to Complex before their inverse transform, so the weight image, and the .pb (sqrt(weight / max)) and pixel mask SIImageStore::makePBFromWeight form from it, carry single-precision rounding: about 1e-8 of the peak, which is a few 1e-6 of the weight where the beam meets a pblimit of 0.05. Pixels the image's symmetry makes equal show it: on refim_sim_hetarray_alma.ms (test_het_antenna_mosaic_cube_alma.3, channel 2), four mirror pixels have CASA beams 0.0500000405, 0.0500001221, 0.0499998446 and 0.0499997467, whose mean, 0.04999993, is casa-rs's 0.0499999261–0.0499999285 at all four. CASA keeps the first two and masks the others; casa-rs masks all four. At double grid precision casa-rs grids and transforms the weights in double precision, rounding each pixel to single only as it publishes it, and does not add CASA's rounding.

    Every other pixel agrees: the cube mosaics of that MeasurementSet's cross baselines (.3) and of all its antennas (.4) differ from CASA in 2 and 1 .pb mask pixels of channel 2, each within 1.3e-7 of pblimit, and in no pixel of their other channels or of the 12 m, 7 m and two-field cubes (.1, .2, .5); their products agree to 4e-5 normalized RMS. Such pixels cannot measure parity.

    The seven-pointing B10 Högbom control with mosweight=True (#818) has one affected pixel, at zero-based (603, 1020) with pblimit=0.2. The unblanked beams reconstructed from the two weight images are 0.2000000128 in casa-rs and 0.1999999561 in CASA. The same pixel crosses the cut in the PB, restored image, PB-corrected image and residual masks. A diagnostic cut at either 0.1999998 or 0.2000002 gives identical support everywhere on the original arrays. The production cut remains at 0.2. All nine products agree within 1.05e−6 normalized RMS on the common support, with exact sum weights and matching WCS and beams.

    sm.predict phases a moved observemany pointing on its field¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows for parity (#884, which corrected the session's own phasing of #861)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSOper/NewMSSimulator.cc (observe, the vector form observemany calls) and msvis/MSVis/VisBuffer.cc (phaseCenter, from FIELD)

    A pointing observemany gives a direction keeps its field's FIELD row. NewMSSimulator computes its UVW and elevation at the direction and writes that direction to POINTING, but leaves FIELD as it was. predict reads the MeasurementSet through a VisBuffer, whose phase centre is FIELD's PHASE_DIR, so it phases the pointing on the field while its UVW belong to the moved direction. Measured on CASA 6.7.6 (twelve ALMA antennas, a second pointing moved 10.4″ east): a 1 Jy point at the field's direction predicts 1 Jy at zero phase in both pointings, and one at the moved direction predicts phases spread over ±180° (rms 101°) in the pointing centred on it.

    casa-rs follows. A moved pointing's UVW, elevation limit and POINTING are at the moved direction (Scan::pointing_rad), and its DATA is phased on its field (Scan::phase_center_rad). Total power samples where POINTING points (#874), as CASA's SDGrid does. The phasing is what every imager and calibrator reading the MeasurementSet assumes: FIELD is the only phase centre it records, so DATA phased on the moved direction would image shifted by the offset. The UVW at the moved direction are CASA's inconsistency. They distort the geometry only at second order, in the offset times a source's distance from the phase centre.

    Evidence on the CPU and on Metal: - components-moved-pointing in tools/simulation/sm_components_scenarios.json, an image and a component list without a beam: 8.36e-6 normalized RMS; - the openfromms-moved-pointing-* scenarios of sm_openfromms_scenarios.json: 0 and 7e-11.

    A primary beam follows POINTING independently of FIELD's phase centre (the next entry).

    The beam on a moved pointing stays where the field's first row points¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; fresh and resumed interferometric simulations centre each integration's beam and mosaic kernels on POINTING (#884, #1294, #1297). Fresh total-power beam offsets remain on #1299.
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines/BeamSkyJones.cc (changed, update) and synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Simulator.cc (makeVisSet's sort)

    VPSkyJones, the simulator's primary beam, inherits BeamSkyJones::update, which takes the beam's centre from POINTING (VisBufferUtil::getPointingDir) at the row it updates on. It updates only when BeamSkyJones::changed fires: - the MeasurementSet, the array or the field changes; - the parallactic angle moves past setvp's parangleincrement. Its default of 360° means never; - POINTING is in a local frame and the time changes.

    The comparisons of the pointing directions themselves are commented out. predict iterates FIELD_ID first, then FEED1, ARRAY_ID, DATA_DESC_ID and TIME. So every row of a field is beamed where the field's first-visited row points, whichever direction a later pointing of the field was moved to.

    Measured on CASA 6.7.6, ALMA at 230 GHz, a second pointing moved 10.4″ east: - Field first, then moved: a point at the moved direction keeps 0.652 of the beam's peak in both pointings. The beam stays on the field. - Moved first, then back at the field (Astra's probe on PR #888): 1.0 in both. The beam stays on the moved direction. - Total power: SDGrid samples POINTING's moved direction while the beam stays where the first row pointed. With one antenna, a constant model and the BOX kernel, CASA's moved samples are 0.671.

    None of these is the beam the moved pointing sees, which follows the antennas. Fresh and resumed simulations separate FIELD's visibility phase from POINTING's beam and total-power sample direction. For changing pointings, tools/simulation/sm_offset_pointings_oracle.py retains the raw CASA output and records a second independent CASA control with a distinct, equal-phase FIELD identity per integration, forcing the stale cache to refresh. Native beam and mosaic predictions agree with that control. Fresh fixed/changing observemany controls in sm_fresh_pointings_oracle.py use the same independent cache reset and capture CASA's IERS inputs; CPU and Metal image, component and mosaic DATA agree below 3e-7 NRMS with unchanged tolerances. Native total power continues to use autocorrelations only, correcting the separate SDGrid cross-correlation defect documented above. Incomplete or disagreeing antenna pointings are explicitly refused before changing the DATA plan.

    Fresh total-power observemany sampling also follows POINTING; the six fixed/changing BOX/PB/VP controls in sm_fresh_pointings_oracle.py retain raw output and use the same independent cache reset (#1299). simobserve gives each pointing its own field and does not reach this cache defect.

    predict and corrupt add scratch columns to a MeasurementSet without them¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-23
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (plan decision 6)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Simulator.cc (predict and corrupt build a VisSet) and msvis/MSVis/VisSet.cc (its constructor's addCalSet2 and initCalSet)

    CASA's simulator writes its steps' results through a VisSet, whose constructor adds MODEL_DATA (1) and CORRECTED_DATA (DATA) to a MeasurementSet without CORRECTED_DATA. predict then sets CORRECTED_DATA to the new DATA and MODEL_DATA to 1, and corrupt sets CORRECTED_DATA to the corrupted DATA. casa-rs's simulator writes DATA only (plan decision 6), and after openfromms it creates neither column: it keeps those MAIN already has in step with DATA as CASA does, and leaves a MeasurementSet without them without them.

    CASA's gain screens read past their transform¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/CalCorruptor.cc (fBM::initialize, GJonesCorruptor::initialize)

    sm.setgain(mode='fbm') draws each antenna's amplitude and phase screens with fBM::initialize on n even slots of 5 s. It fills a half spectrum F of n/2 values, all but the last, and transforms it with FFTServer::fft into a real G whose length the server chooses: the last value's imaginary part is zero and the server was sized n, which is neither 2·(n/2) − 2 nor 2·(n/2) − 1, so G has n − 2 values for an even n and n − 3 for an odd one. It then copies n values out of G, the last two or three from past its end, and GJonesCorruptor::initialize scales each screen by its root-mean-square, those values included.

    What lies past the end changes from run to run. Measured with CASA 6.7.6: - three runs of corrupt-gain (tools/simulation/sm_corrupt_scenarios.json, the same seed) gave amplitude scales of 0.969, 0.966 and 1.005 of the documented one; - in a six-antenna probe with amplitude=[0.1], the phases' scale was 0.15 to 0.22 of it in one run and 1.0 in another; - with a table written, one run's last solution was unit gain, as zeros past the end would make it; - in one run of corrupt-gain-table, antennas 0–6 and 10 of 12 had unit gain throughout, their screens normalised by a value large enough to flatten them.

    casa-rs transforms every slot, so its screens have the documented scale. The CASA comparison holds CASA's gains to closure only, reports rather than checks CASA's unit-gain antennas, and compares the two implementations' screens by their autocorrelations, which a screen's scale does not change.

    settrop(mode='individual')'s water-vapour series come from the same fBM::initialize and normalise the same way (#841 Phase 4d): in one probe, antenna 0's phases were all below 5·10⁻¹⁰ rad. casa-rs transforms every slot there too. On fewer than six slots the spectrum's bins i < N/2 − 1 hold only the mean, so whatever varies in CASA's series is what it read past the transform; casa-rs refuses individual mode on so short a track rather than add no turbulence.

    Gain screens of neighbouring antennas share a seed¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/CalCorruptor.cc (GJonesCorruptor::initialize)

    GJonesCorruptor::initialize seeds antenna a's amplitude screen for receptor r with seed + a + r, and its phase screen with 100 times that. Antenna a's second receptor and antenna a + 1's first draw the same screens: measured, their amplitudes are identical. casa-rs keys each antenna's and receptor's screens apart.

    setgain(mode='random') draws gains about zero¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (owner, 2026-10-24: follow CASA only if there is a good reason for its behaviour, which there is not; #841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/CalCorruptor.cc (GJonesCorruptor::simPar)

    The random mode, which setgain's documentation does not list but Simulator::setgain accepts, returns camp.re·n₁ + i·camp.im·n₂ per integration, antenna and receptor, without adding one: gains of a few hundredths, which all but erase the data. Measured with CASA 6.7.6, amplitude=[0.05] on a 1 Jy point: the table's gains have a mean modulus of 0.065, real parts of mean 0.0000 and standard deviation 0.048, and DATA a mean modulus of 0.0046 Jy. The fbm gains scatter about one, and the amplitude is documented as the gain variations' scale. Gain errors are multiplicative and near unity: a well-designed array's gains factor into antenna gains to within a per cent, and simulations of gain errors draw deviations of a per cent and a degree about one (Synthesis Imaging in Radio Astronomy II, pp. 122 and 354; Heywood, Calibration II, NRAO Synthesis Imaging Workshop 2024, slide 22). Nothing in CASA adds the one back for random; it appears in GJonesCorruptor::simPar and GJones::createCorruptor only. casa-rs draws 1 + camp.re·n₁ + i·camp.im·n₂.

    An autocorrelation row resets its antenna's simulated term¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc (setSimulate)

    SolvableVisCal::setSimulate fills a solution from its chunk's rows, setting ANTENNA1's value at every row, and at an autocorrelation sets it to 1 for a multiplicative term instead of a draw. An antenna's autocorrelation comes before its cross-correlations as ANTENNA1, which set it again, except for the last antenna, which is ANTENNA1 only of its autocorrelation. So with autocorrelations written, CASA's default, the last antenna has unit gain throughout (setgain) and a leakage of 1 on both receptors (setleakage); measured, antenna 42 of 43 in corrupt-gain. casa-rs simulates every antenna's term. settrop's Tf term takes the same loop (its modes are not ones whose autocorrelations CASA simulates), and casa-rs simulates every antenna's there too (#841 Phase 4d).

    setleakage simulates only the first two spectral windows¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc (setSimulate, sizeUpSim); the cause was not traced

    Measured with CASA 6.7.6, five ALMA antennas and setleakage(table=...): with three, four or eight spectral windows, observed once or twice each, the table has solutions for windows 0 and 1 only, and corrupt logs "Encountered data spw N for which there is no (simulated) calibration" for each later window and leaves its rows as they were. setgain is not affected: with four windows its table has solutions for all four, and every window is corrupted. casa-rs simulates a leakage for every window. The CASA comparison, corrupt-leakage, observes two windows.

    setleakage draws one leakage per window for the whole run¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows (#841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Simulator.cc (setleakage), synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc (setSimulate) and JonesGenLin

    Simulator::setleakage simulates with an infinite solution interval: its table has one solution per spectral window and antenna, spanning every scan and field, and corrupt applies it alike to every scan (measured: eight scans of two windows). corrupt applies it as the linearised JonesGenLin, which raises "JonesGenLin matrix apply (J::aR) incompatible with VisVector." on data with two correlations. casa-rs follows both, and likewise does not read setgain's interval, whose documentation says it is not used: CASA's gains are solved per integration.

    A simulated term's table is written when the term is set¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc (setSimulate, storeNCT) and NewCalTable::writeToDisk

    setgain(table=...) and setleakage(table=...) write the term's calibration table as soon as they simulate it, replacing a table of that name, its subtables copied from the MeasurementSet as it then is. casa-rs writes the MeasurementSet only at close, so it writes the table there, from the MeasurementSet it wrote: its rows, sorted by spectral window, time and antenna as CASA sorts them, are the same (tables in tools/simulation/sm_corrupt_scenarios.json compares them with CASA's), but the table appears at close, after reset too, and its subtables include what was observed after the call. casa-rs replaces only a table at the path; anything else there is refused.

    tsys-manual noise takes stale or borrowed system temperatures¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bugs; casa-rs does not follow them (#841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/CalCorruptor.cc (AtmosCorruptor::setCurrTime and simPar), synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc (setSimulate) and synthesis/MeasurementComponents/Jones.cc (JonesScal::invert, setMatByOk)

    setnoise(mode='tsys-manual') adds a unit-amplitude ANoise term and a T term flagged NoiseScale, whose inverse scales the noise: σ_ij = c a / sqrt(Δν t) · sqrt(Tsys(A_i) Tsys(A_j)) / (D_i D_j) (casa_simulation's corrupt::tsys has the whole formula). CASA computes the T table when setnoise is called, one solution per integration and spectral window, and applies it at corrupt by the nearest time. Measured on CASA 6.7.6 (the probe scripts and their results are on the NAS under casa-rs/issue-841-tsys-3a/probes; seven antennas of 12 m and 7 m, per-row variances over 32 to 128 channels): the formula holds to 4·10⁻⁴ (cross 0.99999 ± 0.00036, autocorrelations 1.0003 ± 0.0009, 12+12, 12+7 and 7+7 m pairs alike, and to 6·10⁻⁴ with every parameter away from its default), and CASA's T table, written with integrations of 120 s so that the cache below refreshes at each, gives every row's σ within 1.9·10⁻⁶ of casa-rs's (5,040 rows, two windows). But four inputs are not the row's own:

    • The airmass is up to 100 s stale. simPar caches each antenna's airmass and setCurrTime recomputes it only when the solution time has moved more than 100 s since (the comment's intent is a 1 % tolerance at elevations above 5°). At 10 to 14° elevation with τ₀ = 1 and 2 s integrations, CASA's per-integration variance against the formula at the integration's own time rises in a sawtooth with a 102 s period from 1.01 to 1.56, 1.176 ± 0.0014 on average. The cache is not reset at a field change either.
    • Rows observed after setnoise take another integration's solution. The table holds only the integrations observed by then; a scan observed later is scaled by the nearest one. A scan at 51.6° elevation observed after setnoise got the noise of the earlier scan's last integration at 72.7°: 0.666 ± 0.002 of its own variance (τ₀ = 0.5).
    • Every row takes the first row's EXPOSURE (exposure()(0)): after settimes changed the integration time from 10 s to 2 s, the 2 s scan's variance was 0.1999 of its own, 0.9994 of the first row's.
    • An antenna without an unflagged row applies as 1. setSimulate skips fully flagged rows, so a shadowed or low antenna gets no solution; JonesScal::invert marks it bad and setMatByOk applies it as 1. Its rows get the unit noise times the other antenna's scale (variance 1.0006 of 1 Jy² when both are bad, 1.009 of s_j² when one is).

    casa-rs computes each row's σ from its own integration: each antenna's airmass at the phase centre at the row's TIME, the row's EXPOSURE, its window and dishes, whatever the flags and whenever the row was observed. The CASA comparison scenarios (tools/simulation/sm_noise_scenarios.json) observe above 40° elevation with one integration time and no flagged rows, where the stale airmass moves Tsys by under 0.1 %. casa-rs keeps CASA's rule for a T that is not finite and positive (Tsys overflowing single precision below the horizon, airmass 1000): that antenna scales by 1.

    A tsys-manual term scales every noise term¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (owner, 2026-10-24: follow CASA only if its product is the better simulation, which it is not; #841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/VisEquation.cc (collapseForSim)

    collapseForSim first adds every ANoise term's noise to DATA, then applies every NoiseScale T term to DATA, in correct mode. So each tsys-manual term's scale multiplies the noise of every term, its own and the others'. Measured on CASA 6.7.6: two tsys-manual terms of one seed gave variance 4 σ⁴ (1.004 of it) rather than 4 σ², in Jy⁴, and a 0.5 Jy simplenoise term with a tsys-manual term gave (0.25 + 1) σ² (0.998 of it) rather than 0.25 + σ²: the 0.5 Jy noise came out scaled by the system temperature. casa-rs adds each term's noise with its own σ, as simplenoise terms add, so simplenoise's σ stays in Jy whatever else is set; terms of one seed still add coherently. simobserve sets one noise term and never reaches this.

    CASA's product has no physical counterpart. A system temperature is the sum of its contributions (receiver, atmosphere, spillover, CMB), which a single tsys-manual term already models; a baseline's noise variance scales as the product of its two antennas' system temperatures; and the powers of uncorrelated noise add (Thompson, Moran & Swenson, Interferometry and Synthesis in Radio Astronomy, 3rd ed., pp. 55, 266, 386 and 733). Multiplying one term's σ by another's gives Jy², not a noise in Jy.

    A refused tsys-manual setnoise leaves a unit noise term¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 3)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Simulator.cc (setnoise)

    setnoise creates the unit-amplitude ANoise term before it checks the Tsys parameters. A water vapour above 100 mm, or a zenith Tsys that is not positive or above 10⁶ K, then makes it return False, but the ANoise stays: the next corrupt adds 1 Jy of noise (CASA 6.7.6, pwv='200mm': variance 0.872 over cross- and autocorrelations, where 1 Jy gives 0.875). casa-rs refuses such a setnoise without adding a term. tsys-atm takes the same path in setnoise (the ANoise is created before either Tsys mode's checks; read from the source, not measured), and casa-rs refuses it the same way.

    tsys-atm noise takes its atmosphere half a band below each channel¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bugs; casa-rs does not follow them (#841 Phase 4c)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/CalCorruptor.cc (AtmosCorruptor::initAtm, tsys, opac) and synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc (setSimulate)

    setnoise(mode='tsys-atm') scales a unit ANoise by a Tf term ("TF NOISE") whose Tsys comes from ATM for each channel: Tsys(A, c) = (hν/k) e^(τA) (1/(e^(hν/k/T_ebb(A)) − 1) + T_rx/(hν/k)) (1 + g), τ the zenith opacity and T_ebb the sky's equivalent blackbody temperature at airmass A, with hν/k from the channel's frequency (casa_simulation's corrupt::tsys_atm has the whole model). Measured on CASA 6.7.6 (ALMA's site defaults, 1 mm of water, 64-channel windows of 100 MHz from 180 GHz, across the 183 GHz line, and from 321 GHz, 2 s integrations; the probe scripts and results are on the NAS under casa-rs/issue-841-tsys-4c), every channel's noise variance agrees with that formula, through casa-atmosphere, with no free parameter (ratio 1.0000 and 1.0027, rms 0.0106 and 0.0100 per channel, against a statistical 1σ of 0.0149) once the atmosphere is taken at the frequencies CASA gives ATM, which are not the channels':

    • The atmosphere is half a band below the channel. initAtm builds each window's ATM grid from REF_FREQUENCY, which the simulator sets to the first channel's frequency, with the reference at the middle channel: ATM's channel i is at REF + (i − n/2)·Δν for the first window and REF + (i − n/2 − 1)·Δν for the others, and opac and tsys read channel c of that grid. So channel c's τ and T_ebb are those of a frequency half the band below it (3.2 GHz for the probe's windows), while hν/k is the channel's own. Against the atmosphere at each channel's own frequency, CASA's variance across the 183 GHz line is off by a mean factor of 6.1 and up to 103; at 321 GHz by 0.39 on average and up to 5.5. The shift grows with the window's width and matters wherever the atmosphere changes across it: the 183 GHz line's brightness changes by tens of kelvin over a few GHz at a 5,000 m site (Thompson, Moran & Swenson, Interferometry and Synthesis in Radio Astronomy, 3rd ed., p. 746, Fig. 13.21), and an ozone line at 231.7 GHz nearly doubles ALMA's system temperature within a band (B. Svoboda, Special Topics: Spectral Lines, NRAO Synthesis Imaging Workshop 2026, slide 18).
    • A one-channel window after the first gets no Tsys. For n = 1 the later windows' channel map is read past its end, which is undefined behaviour. In CASA 6.7.6 on macOS the index it read pointed past the grid (the log reads "ch 972849296/20"); the term's T came out bad, setMatByOk applied it as 1, and the probe's 2 GHz continuum window at 230 GHz got unscaled 1 Jy noise (variance 0.996 Jy²). Another build may read something else.

    casa-rs evaluates ATM at each channel's CHAN_FREQ, for every window and a one-channel window alike, so a channel's σ follows the sky it observes. As for tsys-manual, each row takes its own integration's airmasses and EXPOSURE (stale and borrowed system temperatures); the term is also refused when its zenith Tsys at the first window's centre is not a number, which CASA's check (> 10⁶ K or ≤ 0) lets through.

    settrop turns the phase by the dispersive wet delay alone¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 4d)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/CalCorruptor.cc (AtmosCorruptor::cphase)

    sm.settrop adds a Tf Jones term whose value for antenna a in channel c is exp(i·δ_a·φ(c)): δ the fractional excess of water vapour over pwv above the antenna (fractional Brownian motion of rms deltapwv) and φ ATM's zenith wet phase delay for pwv (casa_simulation's corrupt::troposphere has the whole model). cphase takes φ from SkyStatus::getDispersiveH2OPhaseDelay: the dispersive part alone, the refraction the water lines add. Measured on CASA 6.7.6 (seven antennas, 1 mm of water, 64-channel windows of 100 MHz from 180 GHz, across the 183 GHz line, and of 15.625 MHz at 230 GHz; tools/simulation/sm_trop_scenarios.json, probes on the NAS under casa-rs/issue-841-trop-4d), each row of CASA's table is one δ times that dispersive delay (on CASA's grid, below) to 2.2·10⁻⁵ (single precision), where the whole wet delay at the channels' frequencies leaves δ spread across a row by 0.17 of itself (median).

    The path a column of water vapour adds is mostly its non-dispersive part, the refractivity of the water molecule's permanent dipole: ℒ_V ≃ 6.3w, "an excellent approximation for frequencies below 100 GHz", rising to about 8w in the windows above (Thompson, Moran & Swenson, Interferometry and Synthesis in Radio Astronomy, 3rd ed., eq. 13.19, p. 702). At settrop's site ATM gives 7.0 mm of path per mm of water at 100 GHz, and the whole wet delay is 940, 119, 36, 23 and 8.5 times the dispersive part at 43, 100, 183, 230 and 345 GHz. With settrop's defaults (3 mm of water fluctuating by 15 %), CASA's phases have an rms of 3° at 100 GHz and 39° at 230 GHz where a fluctuation that size in the water column turns the phase by about 380° and 910°.

    casa-rs takes the whole wet delay, the dispersive and non-dispersive parts together, so deltapwv means the fluctuation of the water column it names. With CASA's defaults the phases are then fully scrambled at millimetre wavelengths; a deltapwv of 1 % of 3 mm (30 µm of water) turns them by about 60° rms at 230 GHz.

    settrop takes its atmosphere half a band below each channel¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 4d)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/CalCorruptor.cc (AtmosCorruptor::initAtm, cphase)

    settrop's delays come from the same ATM grid as tsys-atm's noise, and cphase reads channel c of it through the same channel map: each channel's φ is the atmosphere's half a band below it, and a one-channel window after the first reads the map past its end (tsys-atm's atmosphere half a band away). In the Phase 4d probe's CASA table, δ (a row's phase over the dispersive delay) is one value across the row to 2.8·10⁻⁶ of itself with the delay on that grid, and spreads by 2.5 of itself across the 183 GHz line with the delay at the channels' own frequencies. casa-rs evaluates ATM at each channel's CHAN_FREQ.

    settrop's screen has an anisotropic spectrum¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bugs; casa-rs does not follow them (#841 Phase 4d)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/CalCorruptor.cc (fBM::initialize, two dimensions)

    settrop(mode='screen') blows one two-dimensional screen of fractional Brownian motion across the array. fBM::initialize fills a half spectrum F2(i, j), i along the wind (the half axis) and j across it, with amplitudes |(i, j)|^−(β+½)/2 times a normal draw (its comment: "still something not quite right with exponent"; an earlier line, left commented out, had −(β+1)/2):

    • The wavenumbers are indices. The screen is long along the wind (CASA's probe screen was 1,359 by 271 pixels), so an index along it is a fifth of the wavenumber of an index across it: the spectrum is stretched along the wind, and the screen is not isotropic.
    • The full axis is not wrapped. j runs to the screen's width, where the indices past half of it are negative wavenumbers; taking them as large ones gives the negative wavenumbers the smallest amplitudes.
    • The exponent. A two-dimensional fractional Brownian motion whose cuts have the spectral exponent β (as individual mode's series, and beta's documentation, have it) has amplitudes |k|^−(β+1)/2.
    • Out of bounds. It sets the imaginary parts of F2(s0/2, 0) and F2(s0/2, s1/2), one past the end of the half axis's s0/2 entries, and a half axis of s0/2 entries transforms to s0 − 2 real pixels along the wind, of which it copies s0: the last two from past the end of the output, as the one-dimensional series do (gain screens read past their transform). CASA's own settrop test is skipped for "intermittent failures".

    Isotropic turbulence's phase spectrum depends only on the spatial frequency's size, |q|^−11/3 in two dimensions (Thompson, Moran & Swenson, 3rd ed., sec. 13.1.7, p. 725). casa-rs draws |k|^−(β+1)/2 on the wavenumbers in cycles per pixel, the full axis wrapped, with the conjugate pairs and real bins the real transform needs on the half axis's first and Nyquist columns (drawn apart, those modes come out at half the power), and transforms every pixel: on a screen five times as long as wide its structure function is the same along the wind and across it, where CASA's differ by five, and its boundary modes have their interior neighbours' power (corrupt::troposphere's unit tests).

    settrop with simint stamps a solution halfway to its interval's start¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 4d)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc (setSimulate)

    With simint longer than an integration, setSimulate iterates the MeasurementSet in intervals of simint and keeps a solution for each integration, stamped (t₀ + t)/2, t₀ the interval's first integration's time and t its own, and takes the screen's slot nearest that stamp. Measured on CASA 6.7.6 (simint=6 on 2 s integrations): the table's times are 0, 1, 2, 6, 7, 8, … s for integrations at 0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10 s, and corrupt, applying the solution nearest each row's time, gives an interval's later integrations a slot from earlier in it. casa-rs stamps each solution with its integration's time and takes the slot nearest it, as CASA does when simint is not set (each integration its own interval). The slots are simint wide either way.

    ATM's HCN catalogue misplaces a line at 106 GHz¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: ATM data error; casa-rs corrects it (#841 Phase 4, casa-atmosphere; owner decision, 2026-10-24)
    • Affected code: atmosphere/ATM/ATMRefractiveIndex.cc:796 (mkSpecificRefractivity_hcn, fre[18])

    HCN's rotational lines are spaced by about 88.6 GHz (J=1–0 at 88.631 GHz, 2–1 at 177.260, 3–2 at 265.886; Synthesis Imaging in Radio Astronomy II, printed p. 568, PDF p. 592): the table runs 88.63, 177.26, …, 974.49 GHz, then gives the J=12–11 line as 106.298069519 before continuing at 1151.45 GHz. The line belongs at 1062.98 GHz (as the JPL catalogue has it, c027001.cat); the decimal point moved. ATM therefore sums a J=12–11-strength HCN line at 106.3 GHz and none at 1063 GHz. HCN is a trace gas (a few 1e-4 ppmv), but near 106.3 GHz the misplaced line is not negligible: with the default Chajnantor profile, the compiled C++ gives a zenith opacity of 0.029368 at 106.298 GHz, of which HCN is 4.5·10⁻⁴; with the line moved to 1062.98 GHz it gives 0.028919, HCN 1.3·10⁻⁷, so the total is 1.5 % high there (measured in the #894 review). Away from 106.3 GHz the line's wings fall off (in at_tool_check.py's bands the correction moves the dry opacity by at most 3.5·10⁻⁵ and Tebb by 1.3·10⁻⁵, at 90 GHz), and at 1063 GHz the missing line is lost among water's.

    casa-rs moves the decimal point back: the line is at 1062.98069519 GHz. The correction is one of four in tools/atmosphere/atm-corrections.patch, which extract_atm_tables.py applies before it generates the port's tables and build-atm-oracle.sh before it compiles the C++ the tests compare with. at_tool_check.py shows the C++ built without the patch equals CASA's at tool (to 7.5·10⁻¹⁵) and the port equals the C++ built with it (to 8.1·10⁻¹⁵), so the port differs from the at tool by the four corrections alone.

    ATM's reference air densities drop a digit at sea level¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: ATM data error; casa-rs corrects it (#841 Phase 4; owner decision, 2026-10-24)
    • Affected code: atmosphere/ATM/ATMProfile.cc:1360, :1367, :1374, :1381, :1388 (st76, den[6][50])

    st76 converts the minor gases' mixing ratios to number densities with a tabulated air density. For the mid-latitude, subarctic and US Standard atmospheres the sea-level entry has lost its leading digit: .496E+19, .711E+19, .549E+19, .855E+19, .548E+19 cm⁻³ where the next entries (2.26e19 and so on at 1 km) and the tropical profile's 2.450e19 show about 2.5e19 was meant. The parabola st76 fits through 0, 1 and 2 km then falls far below the true density near the ground: in the committed sea-level mid-latitude summer case, restoring the digit raises the first layer's interpolated density 4.075 times (#894 review). So O3, N2O, CO, NO2, SO2, HCl and HCN are up to about four times too scarce in layers whose middle is below 1 km. Only sea-level-to-1-km sites with those atmospheres see it, and those gases' columns lie mostly higher.

    casa-rs restores the digit: 2.496e19, 2.711e19, 2.549e19, 2.855e19 and 2.548e19 cm⁻³. Air density falls with height, by about e⁻¹ in a dry-air scale height of 8 km (Interferometry and Synthesis in Radio Astronomy, 3rd ed., printed pp. 661 and 668, PDF pp. 700 and 707), and the restored values fall by 0.88 to 0.91 to their 1 km entries, as the tropical profile's untouched 2.450e19 does; the typo's values rise 3 to 5 times instead. The US Standard Atmosphere's sea-level 1013.25 hPa and 288.15 K give 2.547e19 by the ideal gas law. In at_tool_check.py's humid sea-level atmosphere (mid-latitude summer) the correction raises the O3, CO and N2O line opacities by up to 1.8 %, 7.7 % and 8.5 % and the dry opacity by up to 0.22 % (at 690 GHz); Tebb moves by at most 1.2·10⁻⁵. Sites at 1 km and above do not change.

    ATM weights H2(17)O by the (18)O abundance¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: ATM bug; casa-rs corrects it (#841 Phase 4; owner decision, 2026-10-24)
    • Affected code: atmosphere/ATM/ATMRefractiveIndex.cc:449 (mkEarthSpecificRefractivity, ratiohh17o)

    ratiohh17o = abun_18o/(1+abun_18o+abun_17o+2.0*abun_d) uses the (18)O/(16)O ratio (0.0020439) where the (17)O ratio (0.000375) belongs, so H2(17)O lines are 5.45 times too strong. ATM's own (17)O fractions for oxygen and ozone use abun_17o. casa-rs does too. Over at_tool_check.py's atmospheres and bands the correction lowers the wet opacity by up to 0.61 % (at 990 GHz) and Tebb by up to 0.087 % (at 460 GHz), and changes the dispersive wet phase by up to 4 % where it is small (at 380 GHz); at Chajnantor the zenith opacity at 230 GHz moves by 2·10⁻⁴.

    ATM's HDO weight divides by three¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: ATM typo; casa-rs corrects it (#841 Phase 4; owner decision, 2026-10-24)
    • Affected code: atmosphere/ATM/ATMRefractiveIndex.cc:450 (mkEarthSpecificRefractivity, ratiohdo)

    ratiohdo = 2.0*abun_d/(1+abun_18o+abun_17o+2-0*abun_d) reads 2-0*abun_d (that is, 2) where every sibling ratio has 2.0*abun_d. The denominator is 3.0024 instead of 1.0030, so HDO lines are about three times too weak. casa-rs uses 2.0*abun_d. Of the four corrections this one matters most: at Chajnantor the zenith opacity rises 0.32 % at 230 GHz (near HDO's 225.9 GHz line) and 0.15 % at 90 GHz (near its 80.6 GHz line), with Tebb 0.24 % and 0.10 % higher; over at_tool_check.py's atmospheres and bands the wet opacity rises by up to 1.8 % and Tebb by up to 0.47 % (both at 850 GHz).

    ATM's HCN profile stops at 100 km¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: ATM data gap; casa-rs follows it (#841 Phase 4): there are no values to restore, and the air above 90 km (below 0.002 hPa) is about 2·10⁻⁶ of the column
    • Affected code: atmosphere/ATM/ATMProfile.cc:1429 (st76, hcn[50])

    hcn[50] is initialised with 45 values, so C++ zero-fills its last five levels (100 to 120 km) and the interpolated HCN falls to zero over them. A layer whose middle is above 90 km already interpolates toward the zero at 100 km: the tropical fixture keeps, under a 95 km top, a layer with its middle at 90.8 km and its top at 94.1 km (#894 review). So profiles topped above about 90 km see the gap; tools/atmosphere/extract_atm_tables.py reports it whenever it regenerates the tables.

    Copying an AtmProfile copies its top pressures as bottom pressures¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: ATM bug; no effect on what casa-rs ports
    • Affected code: atmosphere/ATM/ATMProfile.cc:567 (copy constructor)

    The copy constructor fills v_layerPressure0_ from a.v_layerPressure1_. RefractiveIndexProfile (and so SkyStatus) copies its AtmProfile this way, so their getLayerBottomPressure returns each layer's top pressure and setAltitude would take a wrong ground pressure. No opacity, path or brightness calculation reads bottom pressures. casa-atmosphere keeps one profile and never copies it through such a path.

    ATM leaves the ozone isotopologue layers unpadded¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: latent ATM bug; does not arise with ATM's own profiles
    • Affected code: atmosphere/ATM/ATMRefractiveIndexProfile.cc:723–725 (mkRefractiveIndexProfile)

    For a layer without ozone, mkRefractiveIndexProfile pushes a zero onto the total-ozone vector only. The eight ozone state and isotopologue vectors get nothing, so they become shorter than the layer count and later layers' values shift down one place. ATM's standard profiles have ozone in every layer, so this needs a user profile with zero ozone.

    ATM omits (16)O(17)O¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: deliberate in ATM; casa-rs follows it
    • Affected code: atmosphere/ATM/ATMRefractiveIndex.cc:3252 (mkSpecificRefractivity_16o17o)

    The (16)O(17)O function returns zero before its line loop. The comment says the isotope was removed in 2024 while its catalogue is reviewed, because it gave lines that do not exist. The port returns zero too and keeps the catalogue in its tables for the record.

    RefractiveIndexProfile's averaged non-dispersive wet phase reads a channel as a water column¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: ATM bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 4b)
    • Affected code: atmosphere/ATM/ATMRefractiveIndexProfile.cc:2466 (getAverageNonDispersiveH2OPhaseDelay(const Length &, unsigned int))

    The window average calls getNonDispersiveH2OPhaseDelay(v_transfertId_[spwid] + i). RefractiveIndexProfile has no overload taking a channel alone, so the index converts to a Length in metres, and the call returns channel 0's phase for a water column of 0, 1, 2 … metres. The average is then scaled again by the requested column. For the simulator's Chajnantor atmosphere and 1.3 mm of water this gives 306,132° in a 22 GHz window, where the channels' mean is a few degrees (casa-atmosphere's oracle_absorption test reproduces ATM's number exactly). CASA's at tool does not reach this function: its averages go through SkyStatus, whose override is correct. casa-atmosphere takes the mean of the channels' phases.

    VisCalSolver2 fails a solve whose chi-square is exactly zero¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-24
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#903, #905)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/VisCalSolver2.cc:180–185 (and :353–358, the other solve overload), CASA 6.7.5.18

    Each Levenberg-Marquardt iteration recomputes chi-square and, if it is exactly 0.0, prints "CHI2 IS SPURIOUSLY ZERO!" and returns False, so Calibrater::genericGatherAndSolve records no solution for the interval (and writes no table when no interval solves). Chi-square is exactly zero only when the current gains reproduce every unflagged sample to the last bit, which in practice means noise-free synthetic data whose first guess is already the answer. That is an exact fit, not a failure; CASA's oracle runs on the casa-rs gain fixtures had to add noise to get a table at all. casa-rs's solve_graphs keeps an exact fit as a solution. A chi-square that is small but not zero follows CASA's convergence rule unchanged, so noise-free data that converge only linearly can still reach CASA's 50-iteration limit and fail as CASA's would.

    calc_pointings2's ALMA tiling reads an undefined pbcoeff¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-26
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 5a)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py:611 (calc_pointings2)

    For maptype='ALMA' (and its aliases alma, ALMA-OT), when the caller gives no primary beam (beam <= 0), calc_pointings2 defaults it to spacing_asec*pbcoeff*pl.sqrt(3). pbcoeff is a local of task_simobserve.py, not defined in simutil.py, so the call raises NameError (CASA 6.7.6). simobserve reaches it with an ALMA map and no antenna list, and any script that calls calc_pointings2 directly without beam does too. casa-rs uses simobserve's value, 1.13, as the default clearly intends (casa_simulation::simutil::pointings::PBCOEFF); the pointings are then those of the beam spacing × 1.13 × √3.

    noisetemp reads the wrong receiver temperature at the edges of its tables¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-26
    • Status: CASA bugs; casa-rs does not follow them (#841 Phase 5a)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py:1124–1134 (noisetemp)

    noisetemp looks the receiver temperature up with while(f0[z]<obsfreq and z<len(t0)): z+=1 and then t_rx=t0[z-1]:

    • At the first band edge. A frequency at or above the table's lower limit but not above its first edge leaves z = 0, and t0[-1] is Python's last entry. ALMA, ACA and total power at exactly 35 GHz get 230 K (band 10's) instead of 28 K, the (E)VLA from 0.8 to 1 GHz 28 K (Q band's) instead of 10 K, and the SMA at 180 GHz 500 K instead of 67 K. casa-rs takes the first temperature there.
    • Above the last edge of an unknown telescope's table. For a telescope noisetemp does not know, the frequency edges and temperatures are lists of the same length ([10, 900], [200, 200]), so above 900 GHz the test reads f0[2] before checking z and raises IndexError (CASA 6.7.6, WSRT at 950 GHz). casa-rs keeps the last temperature, 200 K. For the telescopes it knows, the edges have one more entry than the temperatures and the test does not overrun.

    The efficiencies are unchanged. tools/simulation/simutil_oracle.py records both cases, and tests/simutil_casa.rs checks that casa-rs departs exactly there.

    simutil.xyz2long subtracts the x offset from y¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1053)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py (xyz2long, lon = pl.arctan2(y-dx, x-dx))

    xyz2long converts Earth-centred x, y, z on a datum to longitude, latitude and height, first removing the datum's offsets. Its distance from the polar axis uses (x-dx, y-dy) and its latitude z-dz, but its longitude is atan2(y-dx, x-dx): it subtracts the x offset from y. For a datum whose x and y offsets differ, the longitude is wrong: long2xyz then xyz2long on NAD27 at ALMA returns a longitude 2.2 arcseconds from the one given (CASA 6.7.6.14). casa-rs subtracts dy (readantenna::geodesy::xyz_to_geodetic), so xyz2long inverts long2xyz; for WGS 84, the datum readantenna uses, the two agree.

    simutil.xyz2long_old subtracts the x offset from z¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1053)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py (xyz2long_old, c = (z-dx)**2 / er**2)

    xyz2long_old, CASA's older iteration for the same conversion, removes the datum's offsets as (x-dx, y-dy, z-dz) everywhere but in its second term, c = (z-dx)**2 / er**2, which subtracts the x offset from z. Its latitude is then slightly wrong for a datum whose x and z offsets differ. casa-rs subtracts dz (readantenna::geodesy::xyz_to_geodetic_old); its longitude and, for WGS 84, its latitude are CASA's.

    Ten ITRF observatories' tabulated positions disagree with themselves¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: measures data defect; casa-rs reads the table as casacore does (#1106)
    • Affected data: the geodetic/Observatories table of the measures data (casarundata 2026.02.19 and 2026.10.27); casacore measures/Measures/MeasTable.cc (doInitObservatories)

    Each row of Observatories gives a position twice: Long, Lat and Height, and X, Y and Z. casacore builds every observatory from the first three alone, for an ITRF row as a spherical vector whose length is Height, and never reads X, Y, Z. For 10 of the 20 ITRF rows the two descriptions are different places:

    Observatory distance between them what is wrong
    OVRO_MMA 6,370,350 m Height is a geodetic height: casacore places it 1.2 km from the Earth's centre
    LOFAR 6,364,570 m the same: 49 m from the centre
    SAO SMA 4,983,940 m Y has the wrong sign; Long, Lat, Height are SMA's
    NRO 19,983 m unknown
    SMA 13,844 m Lat appears to be geodetic beside a geocentric radius
    e-MERLIN, MERLIN2 6,366 m unknown
    SUNRISE 743 m a placeholder: X, Y, Z are zero
    EVLA 34.5 m rounded Long, Lat and Height
    ATF 24.2 m unknown

    CASA 6.7.6.14's me.observatory returns the Long, Lat, Height position for each, so its simulations, itrf2locname and frame conversions use it. casa-rs reads the table as casacore does (ObservatoryEntry::position in casa-measures-data), so the two place every observatory alike, including the three that casacore puts inside the Earth. Choosing the other column per row would need knowing which one was meant, and would make casa-rs and CASA disagree about the same data; the correction belongs in the table.

    simutil.msg posts a verbose message twice, in colour¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: casa-rs posts it once (#1053)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py (msg)

    With verbose=True, CASA's msg posts each message to its log twice at INFO: once with ANSI colour codes around [origin] and the words WARNING and ERROR, once with those words still coloured. casa-rs logs it once, plain, at INFO on casars.<origin>, and writes the open report as CASA does. Without verbose the two log alike (CASA 6.7.6.14, tools/simulation/simutil_helpers_oracle.py).

    simutil.is4d leaves the image tool open¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA defect; casa-rs does not follow it (#1053)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py (is4d)

    is4d returns False before ia.done() for an image without CASA's four axes in order, so the module's shared image tool stays open and the next ia.open warns "Another image is already open, closing first". casa-rs opens nothing that outlives the call.

    MDirection::from_source_name trims a source name¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: casa-rs is more lenient (#1110)
    • Affected code: casacore MeasTable::Source; casa-types measures/direction.rs (parse_named_direction)

    casacore finds a source by name with MUString::minimaxNC, which keeps spaces, and so does casa-rs's measures data. CASA 6.7.6.14's me.source reads its argument as a direction string first, splitting it at spaces: it finds ' 0002-478', with leading spaces, but not '\t0002-478', with a tab. casa-rs's me.source splits the same way and agrees on both. casa-rs's Rust MDirection::from_source_name, which TaQL's measures functions and the derived MeasurementSet columns use, trims every kind of surrounding white space, a tab too, before it looks a name up, as it does for planets and ZENITH; there a name with leading white space finds a source that casacore's MeasTable::Source would not.

    direction_splitter cannot split a direction without an epoch¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-26
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs refuses the input (#841 Phase 5a)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py:876–894 (direction_splitter)

    direction_splitter keeps the epoch only when a direction has three fields, but reads the longitude from field 1 and the latitude from field 2 whatever the count. A direction without an epoch ('18h00m00 -23d00m00') therefore raises IndexError, and a latitude its regular expression ([+-]?\d+).(\d+).(\d+\.?\d*) does not match raises AttributeError (CASA 6.7.6). casa-rs refuses both with "can't interpret ... as a direction", the message isdirection gives, so a simobserve that is given such a direction stops with a clear error rather than a traceback. A direction with more than three fields is split as CASA splits it (no epoch, fields 1 and 2).

    modifymodel swaps axes into place and refuses a cyclic order¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 5b)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py:2951–2971 (modifymodel)

    modifymodel finds which of the model's axes is each of the four it writes (direction, direction, Stokes, spectral) and then moves them with for ax in range(4): if axmap[ax] != ax: arr = arr.swapaxes(ax, axmap[ax]), marking both axes done after each swap. That orders a model whose axes are a transposition of the four (the FITS order, spectral before Stokes), but not a cyclic one: a model with its spectral axis first ([spectral, RA, Dec, Stokes]) is left out of order, and the check before it raises "internal error: I'm confused about the shape if your model data cube" (CASA 6.7.6), with the new image already created and empty. casa-rs permutes the axes, so any order of the four is written as [RA, Dec, Stokes, spectral]; tests/skymodel_casa.rs checks the cyclic case pixel by pixel.

    modifymodel labels the new image's Stokes planes by their count¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 5b)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py:2838–2912 (modifymodel)

    modifymodel returns the model's Stokes labels (model_stokes), but the image it writes takes its Stokes axis from ia.fromshape, whose default coordinate system labels the planes by their count: I, IQ, IQU or IQUV (CoordinateUtil::addStokesAxis). So: - a model labelled I and V comes out labelled I and Q, the V data then read as Stokes Q; - three unlabelled planes come out IQU, though modifymodel warns "setting IQV Stokes parameters from the 4th axis of you model" and returns "IQV".

    casa-rs keeps the model's labels and labels unlabelled planes as modifymodel reports them (IQV for three). Models of Stokes I alone, the usual case, are unaffected. A model labelled in a circular or linear basis (RR and LL, or XX and YY) also keeps its labels, so flatimage (which wants a Stokes I plane) and adding components (which wants I, Q, U or V) refuse it, where CASA relabels it I and Q and goes on; simobserve's sky model is a brightness in Stokes parameters.

    modifymodel indexes the map it makes of two cells¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 5b)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py:2631–2632, 2720 (modifymodel)

    Given incell as a list of two cells, modifymodel converts them with incell = map(qa.convert, incell, ['arcsec', 'arcsec']) and later reads incell[0]. Under Python 3 map returns an iterator, and the index raises TypeError: 'map' object is not subscriptable (CASA 6.7.6). casa-rs takes the two cells, converted to arcsec and used as given (signs included), as the code did under Python 2. A single cell is unaffected: it becomes [−cell, cell].

    An unset compwidth gives the component-only model a meaningless spectral increment¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 5b)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simobserve.py:506

    With a component list and no sky image, simobserve observes a bandwidth of compwidth, or 2 GHz when compwidth is not a quantity, but sets the .compskymodel image's spectral increment with modelcsys.setincrement(type="spectral", value=compwidth) whatever it is. For compwidth="", coordsys.setincrement puts a meaningless value there: in CASA 6.7.6 the model's right ascension in radians, read as hertz (4.976 Hz for RA 19h). casa-rs uses the 2 GHz simobserve observes with. The increment is the model's only spectral information beyond its reference frequency, since the model has one channel.

    ia.modify converts a component spectrum through the image's default measures frame¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA behaviour; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 5b)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/code/imageanalysis/Images/ComponentListImage.cc (_cacheCoordinateInfo), components/ComponentModels/SpectralModel.cc:100–116 (refFreqInFrame)

    When ia.modify adds a component list to an image, each spectral model converts its reference frequency into the image's spectral frame (refFreqInFrame) through the measures frame of the image's spectral coordinate. An image made from a shape (ia.fromshape, as modifymodel and simobserve make theirs) has that frame at its defaults: the geocentre, an epoch of 0, and the J2000 pole, the same defaults as the measures tool's. So a TOPO spectrum on an LSRK image, or LSRK spectra on a TOPO image, are shifted by a velocity no observation has, about 1e-4 of the frequency in CASA 6.7.6's oracle, which a spectral index turns into a flux difference of about 1e-4.

    casa-rs converts as CASA does when the image's conversion layer carries a real frame: an epoch and an observatory position (and the direction). The oracle's LSRD image, with an LSRK conversion layer at the VLA on 2025-06-01, agrees with CASA to 1e-6 (a 1 Jy point at the reference frequency stays 1 Jy). With no conversion layer, or the placeholder (an epoch of 0 at the geocentre), casa-rs takes the frequencies as they are: a meaningful conversion would need the observation's epoch and position, which such a sky model does not carry.

    Components in the image's own frame, the usual case, are unaffected. The oracle's matched-frame cases (LSRK spectra on an LSRK cube, TOPO spectra on a TOPO cube) agree with CASA to 1e-6, where the same cube with spectra in the other frame and only the placeholder differs by 1.1e-4.

    simobserve's calibrator: the last pass, its scans and two component lists¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bugs; casa-rs does not follow them (#841 Phase 5c)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simobserve.py (CASA 6.7.6: the observing loop, sm.setdata(fieldid=range(0,nfld)), and the complist+","+cal.cclist joins); Simulator::createSkyEquation

    With a phase calibrator (caldirection and calflux), CASA 6.7.6's simobserve has three faults. Each was measured by tools/simulation/simobserve_parity.py's CASA runs.

    The last pass. The observing loop calls observemany for the map's pointings only when a pass over them completes, and observes the calibrator after it. Pointings of a pass that totaltime ends inside are collected but never observed. With totaltime='300s' over a 105-pointing map at 10 s, CASA's MeasurementSet holds the calibrator's one scan and no pointing at all. casa-rs observes the pointings the time allows, as CASA does without a calibrator. With totaltime a whole number of passes, the two schedules are the same.

    The calibrator's scans. Before predicting, the task selects the map's fields, setdata(fieldid=range(0,nfld)). The calibrator's field is nfld, so its scans keep the zeros observe wrote; noise is then added to them. casa-rs predicts every scan, the calibrator's included.

    Two component lists. With a complist and a calibrator, the task predicts from complist + "," + <stem>.cal.cclist. createSkyEquation opens that string as one table, which does not exist ("ComponentList … not readable"), so predict returns False. The task does not check it: the MeasurementSet is left unpredicted, every visibility zero, for a model image with a list or for a list alone. casa-rs predicts both lists, as one list of their components (PredictSpec::component_lists).

    The parity cases calibrator and calibrator_lists compare casa-rs with CASA's own simulator doing what the task meant: CASA's MeasurementSet predicted again over every field, from the model image and the lists merged into one. DATA agrees to an NRMS of 5.3e-9 and 5.5e-9.

    The schedule_calibrator_partial control also retains the raw project and reruns the same CASA task with its final queued observemany flushed when srces is nonempty. Only that condition changes: CASA still constructs the schedule and writes the scans. The calibrator component-list comparison allows only the already documented cl.addcomponent float32 rounding of flux; direction and frequency tolerances stay unchanged.

    simobserve ignores failed frequency-frame predictions¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-14
    • Status: CASA error-propagation defect; casa-rs corrects it under the standing deviation policy (#1301).
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simobserve.py, the unchecked sm.predict calls.

    With outframe='Undefined', CASA 6.7.6.14's simulator returns False and logs Transformations to/from frame "Undefined" are not possible.
