For a component prediction in outframe='REST', it returns False and logs No MeasFrame specified for conversion of Frequency: the task supplies no radial-velocity frame. The task ignores both failures, reports success and publishes an entirely unpredicted zero-DATA MeasurementSet. Image-only REST prediction succeeds and is retained.

    casa-rs reports the same prediction cause before publishing the invalid MS. The project can retain the model and pointing products prepared before the failure. This prevents apparent successful science from a rejected calculation. The if_*_undefined and if_components_rest controls retain CASA's raw task projects, require all raw DATA to be zero, independently verify False from CASA's simulator, and require the native error and matching pre-prediction products with no native MS. This is a corrected failure outcome, not a claim that CASA's task itself raises that error.

    simobserve leaves <stem>.noisier.T.cal¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 5c)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simobserve.py (CASA 6.7.6, setnoise(..., table=noisymsroot) and the clean-up)

    tsys-atm noise is set with table=<noisy root>, so CASA writes the T term's table beside the noisy MeasurementSet. The task's clean-up deletes <stem>.noisy.T.cal and <stem>.noisy.sd.T.cal, but not <stem>.noisier.T.cal, the one written when leakage comes first. The table is a by-product the task means to remove; casa-rs writes no Tsys table for any of the three.

    simobserve's observing loop never ends for pointings of no length¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs refuses the input (#841 Phase 5c)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simobserve.py (CASA 6.7.6, the while (sttime-scanstart) < totalsec loop)

    The loop advances the time by each pointing's scan length. When the lengths of a pass sum to zero, as they do for integration='0s' or a pointing file of zero times, the time never advances and CASA loops forever, collecting pointings. casa-rs refuses such a schedule with a message.

    simutil.ismstp takes any zero UVW value for total power¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 5c)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py (ismstp)

    When simobserve corrupts an existing MeasurementSet (obsmode=''), it asks ismstp whether the data are total power. For a MeasurementSet whose first antenna's name has no TP and which has more than one antenna, the test is uvw.all() == 0.. That is true when any UVW value is zero, not when all are. An interferometer's MeasurementSet with one zero coordinate is then corrupted as a single dish's, with the wrong file names and sensitivity constant. casa-rs tests whether every value is zero.

    simobserve deletes a sky model that is the model it writes¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs refuses the input (#841 Phase 5c)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simobserve.py (CASA 6.7.6, the newmodel set-up)

    The task writes <project>/<stem>.skymodel, or <stem>.newmodel when skymodel is the bare name <stem>.skymodel. Given the full path <project>/<stem>.skymodel, as a rerun on an earlier run's model would give it, the output is the input: with overwrite=True, the default, CASA deletes it and then fails to read it. casa-rs refuses it, and the model is left in place.

    simobserve nests a project path inside itself¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA limitation; casa-rs departs (#841 Phase 5c)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simobserve.py (CASA 6.7.6, fileroot + "/" + project)

    The task makes the directory project and names its files fileroot + "/" + project + "." + <configuration>, so a project given as a path, a/b, names its files a/b/a/b.<cfg>…, in a directory that does not exist. Only a plain name in the working directory works. casa-rs names the files by the directory's last component, a/b/b.<cfg>…. For a plain name the two are the same.

    simobserve writes through a symbolic link in the project¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA limitation; casa-rs departs (#841 Phase 5c, #920 review)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simobserve.py (CASA 6.7.6, shutil.copyfile(ptgfile, ...ptg.txt), util.write_pointings after os.path.exists, and the shutil.rmtree calls before each product is written)

    overwrite=True replaces the project's files without asking. CASA writes <stem>.ptg.txt through a symbolic link there in two ways: - a pointing file copied from ptgfile replaces the file outside the project that the link points to (shutil.copyfile follows it); - laid-out pointings over a dangling link create the file the link names outside the project. os.path.exists follows the link, finds nothing to remove, and open(..., 'w') follows it.

    A link to a table that it replaces stops the task instead, since shutil.rmtree refuses links. Its existence checks follow links, so a dangling link does not count as an existing file under overwrite=False.

    casa-rs replaces a link in the project with the new file or table and leaves what the link points to as it was. A link counts as an existing file under overwrite=False, dangling or not. The project's own files are the only ones overwrite replaces (the owner's decision of 2026-10-26, recorded with the provider contracts' overwrite exception).

    simobserve rounds a direction list of one to its mean¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA artefact; casa-rs simobserve departs (#841 Phase 5c, after the #913 review)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simobserve.py (CASA 6.7.6, dir = direction for a list) and simutil.direction_splitter

    CASA passes a direction list to calc_pointings2 as a list, even a list of one. direction_splitter centres a list at its mean, written at precision 5 and read back. So ['J2000 18h00m00.123456789s …'] lays its map out about 1e-5 s of right ascension (0.15 mas) from the same direction given as a string.

    casa-rs's simutil tool follows CASA here, since its lists must match CASA's (#913 review). casa-rs's simobserve cannot tell the two apart. The task contract resolves a string to a list of one, as it does for every parameter that takes a string or a list. So simobserve lays a map out at a single direction exactly as given, whether a string or a list of one. A list of several is the pointings themselves, as in CASA. The rounding carries no information, so the exact direction is the better answer.

    A gain table's solved window records its frequency as its resolution and total bandwidth¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows, for CASA's append (#917)
    • Affected code: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc, SolvableVisCal::setCTFrequencies, CASA 6.7.5.18

    A G or T table holds one channel per spectral window. For a window a solve reaches, setCTFrequencies writes that channel's frequency and width from FreqMetaData. It then takes const Vector<Double>& chres(chfr); // resolution same as freq and Double totbw=sum(chfr);, so RESOLUTION and TOTAL_BANDWIDTH both hold the frequency. On ngc5921 (gaincal, spw='0') the table's window reads CHAN_FREQ 1413421909.706 Hz, CHAN_WIDTH 1538085.9 Hz, and RESOLUTION and TOTAL_BANDWIDTH 1413421909.706 Hz.

    The values are plainly meant to be widths: - the same function sets EFFECTIVE_BW to the width; - the older setOrVerifyCTFrequencies sets resolution, effective bandwidth and total bandwidth to the width; - NewCalTable::setSpwFreqs sets TOTAL_BANDWIDTH to the summed width; - a window no solve reached keeps its summed resolution and the MeasurementSet's total bandwidth (createMemCalTable2).

    Nothing in CASA's apply path reads either column, so the error does not change calibrated data. But CASA's gaincal(append=True) merges the new solutions' windows into the table's (NewCalTable::mergeSpwMetaInfo). It requires NUM_CHAN, CHAN_FREQ, CHAN_WIDTH, RESOLUTION, EFFECTIVE_BW, REF_FREQUENCY and TOTAL_BANDWIDTH to equal its own exactly for any window both hold.

    A casa-rs table that wrote the widths there was refused ("Error merging spw=0's meta info"). So casa-rs writes the frequency as CASA does: the interoperability contract outranks the correction.

    The law casa_appends_to_a_casa_rs_gain_table (casa-calibration slow-tests) appends CASA's solutions to a casa-rs table. With gain_table_windows_match_casa_on_ngc5921 and its two-sideband companion, it asserts that every column of every window row equals CASA's exactly.

    The same defect is present for channelized bandpass(bandtype='B') in CASA 6.7.6.14: RESOLUTION is each channel's frequency and TOTAL_BANDWIDTH is their sum. CAL-06 preserves those values for the same append-interoperability reason. calibration_lifecycle_oracle.py exchanges B tables in both directions and compares every SPW column.

    imregrid refuses a one-channel template in another spectral frame¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#841 Phase 6a)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6's imregrid (casacore ImageRegrid through ImageRegridder)

    casacore does not regrid an axis whose input and template both have one pixel. CASA 6.7.6 keeps the input's coordinate for it in the output: a template one channel 3 MHz away, or with its one Stokes plane labelled Q, gives the input's channel and Stokes I (tools/images/image_analysis_oracle.py, cases imregrid_one_channel_moved and imregrid_one_stokes_plane_relabelled). The same template with that one channel in another frame (LSRK to TOPO) stops instead, with "Failed to set conversion layer state".

    casa-rs treats that channel as it treats the others: it is not regridded, and the output keeps the input's (casa_images::regrid). Converting the frame of an axis that is never regridded serves no purpose.

    tsdimaging's theoretical beam models the kernels, not the gridder's¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-28
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#938 part 1, #841 Phase 6c)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/sdbeamutil.py (TheoreticalBeam.get_kernel and the kernel functions it calls)

    tsdimaging gives its image the beam sdbeamutil.TheoreticalBeam computes: the antenna's Gaussian beam convolved with a cut through the gridding kernel and with a box as wide as the sampling, then fitted with a Gaussian. The kernel it convolves with is not the gridder's (SDGrid::init) but a model of it, and the models disagree with the gridder: - GAUSS. The gridder takes gwidth as a half width at half maximum; sdbeamutil passes it to a Gaussian that takes a full width. It also cuts the kernel at 1.5 gwidth where the gridder cuts at truncate (default 3 gwidth). - GJINC. sdbeamutil ignores truncate ("NOT SUPPORTED YET") and always cuts at the jinc's first null. - SF. The gridder's kernel is (1 − ν²) grdsf(ν); sdbeamutil uses SciPy's prolate spheroid pro_ang1(0, 0, c) with c = 5.356π/2, chosen to resemble it. - PB. The gridder takes the telescope's power pattern (for ALMA, the 10.7 m Airy pattern of VPSkyJones); sdbeamutil takes an Airy disk scaled to the Gaussian beam's width, for the antenna's own diameter. - BOX. sdbeamutil makes the box one pixel wide whatever convsupport is; the gridder's box reaches 2s + 1 pixels.

    The reported beam is then not the image's. Against CASA 6.7.6 on the oracle's setups (tools/singledish/tsdimaging_oracle.py), the gridder's own kernels give beams wider by 0.60% to 3.3% for GAUSS, and 0.31% narrower for GJINC with a truncate. They differ by at most 6.0e-4 for SF, PB and GJINC at its defaults. The gridder reads its table at the radius truncated to a hundredth of a pixel, which widens the kernel slightly. That accounts for all of GJINC's difference at its defaults (read at the rounded radius, it agrees with CASA to 1e-6) and for about 40% of SF's. The rest of SF's is SciPy's spheroid against grdsf.

    casa-rs cuts through the gridder's own table (casa_imaging_singledish:: theoretical_beam_arcsec), so the beam describes the image the gridder makes. BOX at its default support, which both describe alike, agrees with CASA to 2e-8.

    The cost falls downstream. simanalyze and simalma grid total power with SF of support 6 and scale its flux by this beam's area. A beam 6e-4 wider is an area about 1.2e-3 larger, so their scaled total-power and feathered products differ from CASA's by about that factor, at the edge of the 1e-3 acceptance. Phases 6g and 6h measure it.

    getPointingSamplingRaster measures the row spacing in raw longitude¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-28
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#938 part 1, #841 Phase 6c)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/code/synthesis/Utilities/SingleDishBeamUtil.cc (getPointingSamplingRaster)

    CASA estimates a raster's sampling from its pointings. Along the scan it scales each longitude step by the cosine of the map's central latitude, so the step is an angle on the sky. Across the scan it projects the steps between rows' median positions on the perpendicular without that factor. A map scanned in latitude, whose rows step in longitude, then has its row spacing overestimated by 1/cos δ, 15% at δ = −30°. The beam tsdimaging reports widens with it.

    casa-rs scales the rows' longitude steps as it scales the along-scan ones (casa_imaging_singledish::raster_sampling). A map scanned in longitude, as simobserve scans one, is unaffected: CASA and casa-rs agree on the oracle's maps to 1e-15.

    tsdimaging writes the weight mask's threshold to six decimal places¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-28
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#938 part 1, #841 Phase 6c)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_tsdimaging.py (CASA 6.7.6, do_weight_mask: ia.calcmask("'%s'>%f" % (weightimage, weight_threshold)))

    minweight masks the pixels whose weight is at most minweight times the median positive weight. The threshold goes into the mask expression through %f, which keeps six decimal places. A threshold under 5e-7 is written 0, so every pixel with a positive weight stays unmasked, and one of 1.4e-6 is written 0.000001, masking less than asked. Weights have no fixed scale, so a MeasurementSet whose weights are all small loses its minweight mask entirely. casa-rs compares with the threshold itself (casa_imaging_singledish::weight_mask_threshold; tools/singledish/tsdimaging_oracle.py, case sf_small_weights).

    SDGrid grids nothing with an SF kernel of no support¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-28
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs refuses (#938 part 1, #841 Phase 6c)
    • Affected code: casatools/src/code/synthesis/TransformMachines2/SDGrid.cc (SDGrid::init)

    SDGrid::init tabulates the SF kernel over convsupport pixels, so convsupport=0 gives a kernel of zeros. Every pixel's weight is then zero, the image is empty, and tsdimaging only warns that no weight is positive. casa-rs refuses that kernel, asking for a support of at least 1 or CASA's default of 3 (casa_imaging_singledish::GridFunction::from_casa).

    simanalyze retains missing or shadowed Python task bindings¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-15
    • Status: corrected by native Rust composition (#841 Phase 6g)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 task_simanalyze.get_concatweights and its from .simutil import * import

    A multi-MS call reaches the obsolete gentools(['tb', 'msmd']) without defining that factory and raises NameError. The simutil star import also shadows the public feather callable with a module, causing a feather call to raise TypeError: 'module' object is not callable. The independent simanalyze_multi_oracle.py recipe restores only those bindings before running the unchanged algorithms. Production computation is native Rust.

    Disabled upstream simulation test bodies¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 disables sixteen test_task_simobserve cases. Exercising them exposes undefined comp_nchan/ptgfile variables, the deliberate CAS-5095 component-list/SD refusal, nine obsolete "Parameter verification failed" assertions (the task now raises parameter-error dictionaries), and an atmospheric noise comparison tied to an older random draw. Native tests exercise the intended leakage equations, the actual SD refusal and invalid parameter checks; analytic noise comparisons retain the upstream 10% bound. The original assertions and their failures remain in the upstream runner.

    The disabled combined-IF/TP test_task_simanalyze case encounters the shadowed feather binding above. Its expected <project>.image0 path also omits .sd: task_simanalyze writes tpimage = project + '.sd.image' and temp_out = tpimage + '0'. The compact native counterpart checks the actual IF/TP products and compares the feathered image to the existing independent CASA fixture at 1e-3 NRMS. Its original 1458-pixel, 128-channel dimensions need a 13.13 GB native TP scaling/regridding reservation. With that reservation explicitly authorized on the 24 GiB Mac mini, the full case now completes including feathering, at a measured 12.50 GB peak process footprint (#1493). The simulation wrapper charges reused FFT scratch per direction plane and reserves only flat-image work when analysis and graphics are disabled; the earlier cube-wide scratch charges incorrectly requested 43.6 GB for feathering. CASA C++ reads all six full-size/intermediate products and their defined pixels are finite. The raw obsolete-path assertion remains unchanged.

    simanalyze changes a caller's seed-image unit in place¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-15
    • Status: caller data preserved (#841 Phase 6g, accepted Phase 6 decision)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 task_simanalyze, before simutil.imtclean

    CASA opens modelimage and sets its brightness unit to Jy/pixel before calling tclean. A total-power or previously restored synthesis image loses its Jy/beam label, including when it was supplied by the caller. Native simanalyze preserves CASA's numerical seed convention in a private copy and leaves the supplied image's pixels, mask and unit unchanged. The TP seed case compares the synthesis results and verifies the source label.

    Repeated simanalyze concatenation can duplicate observations¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-15
    • Status: new native simulation concatenations replace prior results (#841 Phase 6g)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 task_simanalyze, multiple interferometric MS branch

    The task invokes concat on <project>.concat.ms without clearing an earlier result; concat appends when that output exists. Default discovery can also select that old combined MS alongside its original inputs. Native simanalyze excludes the previous combination when the originals are present and stages a fresh combination before replacing its own output. Input observations remain read-only.

    simanalyze writes the fidelity's floor to six decimal places¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 6f)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simanalyze.py (CASA 6.7.6, "max(abs('%s'), %f)" % (difference, maxdiff/pl.sqrt(2.0)))

    The fidelity image divides the convolved model by the difference, floored at the difference's median absolute deviation over √2. The floor goes into the image expression through %f, which keeps six decimal places. A floor of 5.19e-7 Jy/beam is written 0.000001, nearly twice its value, and one under 5e-7 is written 0, so a pixel where the difference is zero divides by zero. Simulations of faint sources have differences that small (tools/simulation/simanalyze_analysis_oracle.py, case faint). casa-rs uses the floor itself (casa_simulation::simutil::analysis::fidelity).

    simanalyze's fidelity image is labelled Jy/beam¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA artefact; casa-rs departs (#841 Phase 6f)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/task_simanalyze.py (CASA 6.7.6, ia.imagecalc(fidelityim, "abs('%s') / '%s'" % (convolved, absdiff)))

    The fidelity is the ratio of two images in Jy/beam. CASA's image expression copies its unit from the first image it names, so the fidelity is labelled Jy/beam. casa-rs writes it without a unit.

    simanalyze deletes previous products before imaging preparation succeeds¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-15
    • Status: CASA data-loss bug; casa-rs preserves inputs and previous products (#180)
    • Affected code: casatasks/private/task_simanalyze.py (CASA 6.7.6.14, deletion of <imagename>.image and <imagename>.model before imtclean)

    A rerun with an invalid start model destroys the previous image/model before the imager can reject its units or resources. Selecting the previous <imagename>.model as modelimage deletes that selected input too. casa-rs refuses input/output collisions before mutation and uses the native imager's existing atomic publication. Failed preparation preserves prior products. simanalyze_task_casa.rs checks the colliding model and a K-unit start model, then verifies the previous image and model are unchanged.

    statim scales a Jy/beam image without a beam by the pixel area¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-28
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 6f)
    • Affected code: casatasks/src/private/simutil.py (CASA 6.7.6, statim: toJyarcsec=1. when ia.restoringbeam() is empty, then toJypix=toJyarcsec*pixarea)

    statim multiplies an image's minimum, maximum and corner rms by a flux-per-pixel factor. For Jy/pixel, and for Jy/beam with a restoring beam, the factor is 1, so the statistics stay in the image's unit. For Jy/beam without a beam it treats the image as Jy per square arcsecond, and the factor becomes the pixel area in square arcseconds: 0.04 for 0.2″ pixels. It still reports the unit as Jy/beam, so the numbers are in no unit at all. Without a beam, Jy/beam cannot be converted to anything. casa-rs reports the image's own values in its own unit (casa_simulation::simutil::analysis::statim; tools/simulation/simanalyze_analysis_oracle.py, image beamless.flat). simanalyze's reported statistics of its image do not meet this case: it reads the image's beam first, and fails without one.

    Image expressions containing "pa" are labelled as position angles¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-27
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#841 Phase 6f)
    • Affected code: casatools imageanalysis/ImageAnalysis/ImageExprCalculator.tcc (_expr.contains(casacore::Regex("pa\\(*")))

    ImageExprCalculator looks for the pa() function with the regular expression pa\(*, which matches "pa" followed by any number of parentheses, so any "pa" at all. An expression whose image paths contain "pa" (a project named pancake, or a path through scratchpad) has its output's unit set to deg and its Stokes axis replaced by Pangle. simanalyze's difference, absolute difference, fidelity and absolute model are all image expressions over <project>/<project>.<configuration>, so a project whose path contains "pa" gets a fidelity labelled deg, and difference and fidelity images whose Stokes plane is a position angle. casa-rs computes those images directly and keeps the Stokes axis.

    Simulated MeasurementSets name a processor that does not exist¶

    • Observed in CASA 6.7.6-14's public sim_multi_arrays_and_TP m51c 12 m and 7 m inputs and their concat result.
    • Evidence: scripts/concat_parity/task_oracle.py and the concat task reference fixtures.

    MAIN has PROCESSOR_ID=0 while PROCESSOR has no rows. Zero therefore names no valid processor. Native concatenation writes the MS null sentinel -1 in this case, preserving the absence of a processor without a dangling foreign key. The parity comparison explicitly compares that logical absence; a populated PROCESSOR table and its references are compared normally.

    concat does not scale its first input's sigma spectrum¶

    • Observed in CASA 6.7.6-14.
    • Affected code: casatasks/private/task_concat.py, the first MeasurementSet's weight-scaling block. Appended inputs are handled by MSConcat.
    • Evidence: scripts/concat_parity/task_oracle.py and the concat reference fixtures, with weight factor 4 on the chronologically first input.

    CASA multiplies its first input's WEIGHT and WEIGHT_SPECTRUM by 4 and divides SIGMA by 2, while leaving SIGMA_SPECTRUM unchanged. This makes the spectral uncertainties inconsistent with the same sample's weight and sigma. The native task divides both sigma representations by the square root of the weight factor for every input. The fixtures retain the actual CASA result, and the regression checks the corrected cells against that variance law.

    concat leaves a merged SOURCE row's TIME unchanged¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#937 PR B, #939)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSOper/MSConcat.cc (updateSource: sourceCol.interval().put(j, newTime) before sourceCol.interval().put(j, newInterval))

    When two SOURCE rows of one window are equivalent, updateSource keeps the first and widens its validity to span both: it computes the union's centre and length, then writes the centre into INTERVAL and overwrites it with the length. TIME is never written, so the kept row is valid over an interval centred on its old time, which misses the end of the other row's validity. casa-rs centres the record on the union (casa_observation::merge, sources.rs, widen).

    concat's source equivalence lets its last optional column decide¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#937 PR B, #939)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSOper/MSConcat.cc (sourceRowsEquivalent)

    sourceRowsEquivalent compares the required columns with &&, then tests each optional column present (POSITION, PULSAR_ID, REST_FREQUENCY, SYSVEL, TRANSITION) by assigning its result to areEquivalent, so the last optional column tested decides: two rows with different rest frequencies but equal transitions are equivalent, and their lines are merged into one. casa-rs requires every member to be equal (an optional one stated by both or by neither).

    concat renumbers merged sources so that one can split and fields dangle¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#937 PR B, #939)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSOper/MSConcat.cc (updateSource, "renumber consecutively"; copyField, newSourceIndex2_p)

    After removing redundant SOURCE rows, updateSource renumbers source IDs row by row: a row whose ID exceeds the count so far gets the next number. A source of several rows (one per window) after a removed ID therefore gets one number for its first row and another for the next, splitting it. The IDs it changes are applied to the fields copyField appends, not to the fields already in the output, which can then name a source ID no row has. casa-rs numbers merged sources in the order they first appear, every record of a source keeping its number, and maps every field (casa_observation::merge, sources.rs, regroup).

    concat leaves gaps in observation IDs after removing duplicates¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#937 PR B, #939)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSOper/MSConcat.cc (copyObservation)

    copyObservation appends the incoming OBSERVATION rows, removes each row equivalent to an earlier one, and maps it to the last earlier equivalent. It does not shift the IDs of the rows after a removed one, as copyProcessor does with removedSoFar, so an input of several observations can have its rows mapped past the end of the table, or onto another observation, once a duplicate is removed. casa-rs maps each duplicate to its first equivalent and numbers the distinct observations consecutively, for processors and observations alike (casa_observation::merge, distinct).

    concat drops every state when one input has none¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA behaviour; casa-rs does not follow it (#937 PR B, #939)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSOper/MSConcat.cc (concatenate, "merge STATE")

    When one of the two MeasurementSets has no STATE rows, concatenate writes no states at all: it deletes the first MeasurementSet's STATE rows and sets every row's STATE_ID to -1, so the intents of the input that had them are lost. casa-rs keeps them: the rows of an input without states have STATE_ID -1, and the others keep their merged states (casa_observation::merge, states).

    concat writes into the MeasurementSets it appends¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA behaviour; casa-rs does not follow it (#937 PR B, #939)
    • Affected code: casatools src/tools/ms/ms_cmpt.cc (ms::concatenate, line 6497)

    After appending a MeasurementSet, ms::concatenate writes a HISTORY row ("… appended to …", with its parameters) into the appended MeasurementSet, an input, rather than into the output; the inputs' own HISTORY is never copied. A task that reads several MeasurementSets to make another should leave them as they were. casa-rs never writes an input: the output's HISTORY holds what the creating task records of itself (CreationOptions::history), and the inputs' HISTORY is a declared loss.

    tclean reads a uvtaper with no unit as radians¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-28
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs refuses the value (#841 Phase 6b)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesisimager_cmpt.cc (setweighting) and msvis/MSVis/VisImagingWeight.cc (setFilter)

    tclean's documentation gives uvtaper=['300.0'] as a taper in wavelengths ("default units are lambda in aperture plane"). setFilter takes the uv-plane path only when the unit contains "lambda", and otherwise treats the value as an image-plane angle, here 300 radians. The filter is then exp(-π²θ²/(4 ln 2) u²), which underflows to zero on every baseline of refim_point.ms. Every imaging weight is zero there, and the image is empty with a beam of 1e-6 arcsec (CASA 6.7.6). A unitless value is always an angle in radians to CASA, never the documented wavelengths. Whether it empties the image depends on the value and the baselines, and a small enough one does not. casa-rs refuses a uvtaper entry without a unit and asks for an angle or for wavelengths (casa_imaging_application::ImagingRequest::uvtaper).

    tclean corrupts an absolute directory when numbering a restart¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-31
    • Status: CASA bug; owner approved preserving the correct directory (#944). Native numbering keeps absolute paths for both the main image and outliers.
    • Affected build/code: CASA 6.7.6.14, casatasks private/imagerhelpers/input_parameters.py, incrementImageNameList.

    With existing products and restart=False, CASA finds the next shared numbered image prefix. Its Python helper removes the first two characters of each directory with dirnames[immod][2:], assuming a ./ prefix. For an absolute imagename under /Volumes/, this instead produces olumes/ and fails to create the numbered PSF. Relative prefixes in the same probe correctly produce _2 and _3. The exact build, parameters and failure are recorded on #944 and in the lifecycle receipt linked from the generated compatibility reference. The native implementation keeps the requested absolute directory and uses the same suffix across the main and outlier prefixes.

    tclean cleans nothing with nsigma, fastnoise=False and no mask¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs cleans the whole image, as CASA does with fastnoise=True (#980)
    • Affected build/code: CASA 6.7.6.14, casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SIImageStore.cc (calcRobustRMS) and SynthesisDeconvolver.cc (initMinorCycle, setupMask)

    With usemask='user', no mask, nsigma>0 and fastnoise=False, the first initMinorCycle estimates the noise with calcRobustImageStatistics(*residual(), *mask(), ...). mask() creates the .mask image there, filled with zeros. setupMask then finds an existing mask and keeps it ("Initializing to existing mask") instead of setting the whole image to 1, as it does when no mask exists. The run stops at once with the zero-mask stop code 7, an empty .mask and an empty model (CASA 6.7.6.14 on refim_twochan.ms, imsize=100, cell='8.0arcsec', niter=300, nsigma=3). With fastnoise=True the noise estimate never touches mask() and the whole image is cleaned. casa-rs cleans the whole image in both cases: without a mask its noise estimate is Chauvenet's over every valid pixel, as CASA's would be once the full mask existed.

    A faceted tclean image carries no primary-beam mask¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs masks a faceted residual and restored image where the primary beam is under pblimit, including every Taylor coefficient (#980, #1063)
    • Affected build/code: CASA 6.7.6.14, casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SynthesisNormalizer.cc (divideResidualByWeight) and SIImageStore.cc (divideResidualByWeight, copyMask)

    SIImageStore::divideResidualByWeight copies the primary beam's mask0 onto the residual whenever the image has a primary beam and pblimit >= 0 ("A T/F mask in the residual will confuse users"), and restoration copies the residual's mask onto the image. With facets > 1, SynthesisNormalizer::divideResidualByWeight normalizes each facet's sub-image store instead, and copyMask finds no mask0 default mask on a facet's sub-image of the primary beam, so it copies nothing: the published .residual and .image have no mask, although .pb masks the same pixels. On refim_twopoints_twochan.ms (imsize=200, cell='8.0arcsec', facets=2, niter=30, CASA's test_multifield_facets_mfs) CASA's .pb masks 2,471 pixels below pblimit 0.2 and its .image and .residual mask none; casa-rs's .pb, .image and .residual mask those 2,471 pixels, and every pixel value agrees (normalized RMS 8e-7 and 3e-6).

    The named test_multifield_facets_mtmfs case on the same input with deconvolver='mtmfs' and nterms=2 has the same 2,471 PB mask pixels on each joined residual and restored Taylor plane. Stock CASA omits all four masks. casa-rs copies its PB mask to each plane. A comparison of the full pixel arrays, including the masked pixels, agrees within 5.38e-6 normalized RMS across this case's products.

    CASA's cube workers reject facet stores with no polarization representation¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, tclean fails before publishing a PSF for a 2×2 faceted, Stokes I, two-channel dirty cube on refim_point.ms, refim_eptwochan.ms or refim_twopoints_twochan.ms. The worker reports “data polarization type is not defined.” SIImageStore::init sets the representation to UNKNOWN. SynthesisImagerVi2 leaves the call that copies the parent's representation into a facet commented out, while it copies the representation into channel chunks. runCubeGridding sends the unset value to CubeMajorCycleAlgorithm, which rejects it. The unfaceted control completes.

    Casa-rs uses the observation's polarization plan for every facet and finishes natural-weighted, same-frame standard dirty and Högbom cubes with nearest-channel sampling. For refim_point.ms, independent CASA faceted MFS runs of the selected channels verify the PSFs, residuals, restored images, models, masks and facet weights. An unfaceted CASA cube on those same channels verifies the frequency axis and cube PB (#1063). When the first facet has no usable weight on a channel, casa-rs takes that channel's PSF from the first populated facet. This extends its scalar facet behavior to cubes. CASA's normalizer always uses facet zero, whose empty PSF cannot guide deconvolution in the populated facets.

    tclean seeds a Stokes I image from a Stokes Q start model¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-28
    • Status: CASA behavior retained with a warning (owner decision #980, 2026-10-31; supersedes #970's refusal)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SIImageStore.cc (setModelImageOne, regridToModelImage) and casacore images/Images/ImageRegrid.tcc

    regridToModelImage regrids a start model on its direction and spectral axes only. ImageRegrid copies the Stokes axis without comparing its coordinate, so a one-plane Stokes Q model seeds the model of a Stokes I run. Its pixels are then predicted, subtracted and restored as Stokes I. casa-rs now matches this positional copy for equal-count Stokes axes and warns when the labels differ (casa_imaging_application's StartModel). The owner requested matching CASA with a warning on 2026-10-31 (#980), replacing the earlier refusal. This is not a physical polarization conversion; published coordinates still describe the requested target image. Unequal-plane-count forms remain unassessed for equivalence.

    A degenerate spectral axis is replicated the same way: an MFS start model of one plane seeds the MFS plane at whatever frequency it is stated. That is physically sound for a continuum model, and casa-rs keeps it. A cube's start model must have the cube's spectral axis.

    flagmanager gives the version a save renames the new comment¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#931, #997 review)
    • Affected code: casatasks private/task_flagmanager.py (mode save, lines 51–83)

    Saving a version under a name that already exists first renames the old version to <name>.old.<seconds since 1970>, so both stay restorable. The task then rewrites the old entry of FLAG_VERSION_LIST as tmpname + ' : ' + comment, where comment is the comment of the new save. CASA 6.7.6.14 lists {0: {'name': 'v1.old.1793292269', 'comment': 'second'}, 1: {'name': 'v1', 'comment': 'second'}} after saving v1 with 'first' and then 'second'; the first comment is lost. casa-rs renames the version the same way and keeps its own comment (flagmanager's keep_existing_version).

    flagmanager replaces working flags for an unrecognized merge choice¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 accepts unrecognized merge strings in restore mode and silently applies replace. For example, merge="OR" removes working flags that were set after the version was saved. The tools/casa-compat/flagmanager_store_oracle.py comparison verifies this for both FLAG and FLAG_ROW. It also verifies replace, or, and and against independent flag patterns. casa-rs raises ValueError for an unrecognized choice and preserves the working flags.

    flagdata's summary aborts on a correlation it cannot name¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#931, #997 review)
    • Affected code: casatools flagging/Flagging/FlagMSHandler.cc (open) and FlagDataHandler.cc (generatePolarizationsMap)

    With a polarization whose CORR_TYPE is RX, RY, LX and LY (casacore Stokes types 13 to 16), CASA 6.7.6.14's flagdata(mode='summary') warns "Correlation product unknown found: 13" and "The 0 th correlation is unknown: 13", then aborts the Python process with an uncaught std::out_of_range from map::at; there is no summary. casa-rs names every correlation by casacore's Stokes::name, so the summary keys those correlations RX, RY, LX and LY (listobs::stokes_name).

    A subimage offsets a tabular coordinate's world values instead of moving its table¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-29
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1001, PR #1005 review)
    • Affected code: casacore coordinates/Coordinates/CoordinateSystem.cc (subImageInSitu) and TabularCoordinate.cc (toWorld, setReferencePixel)

    A TabularCoordinate with a nonlinear table converts a pixel through its channel corrector first and its linear part (crval, cdelt, crpix) second. subImageInSitu moves a tabular coordinate's reference pixel by the subimage's start, as for a linear axis, but the corrector stays in the input's pixels. A subimage's world values are therefore the input's at the same pixel, offset by cdelt × start, not the input's at the pixel they were cut from. For a table at pixels [0, 1, 3, 4] with world values [10, 11, 15, 22], coordsys.subimage(originshft=[0, 1]) gives world values [13, 14, 16] at pixels 0 to 2, where the input has [11, 13, 15] (CASA 6.7.6). A tabular spectral coordinate is not affected: subImageInSitu rebuilds its table over the subimage's channels. casa-rs moves the table's pixels with the reference pixel (casa_images imsubimage), so a subimage reads the input's world values at the pixels it was cut from. CASA's imsubimage ignores a box on an image without a direction coordinate, the only way casa-rs cuts a tabular coordinate's axis, so no task reaches this in CASA.

    A blank Stokes string can crash coordinate construction¶

    • Date noted 2026-11-19
    • Affected code is casatools src/tools/coordsys/coordsys_cmpt.cc in addcoordinate.

    CASA 6.7.6.14 exits with SIGSEGV on coordsys().addcoordinate(stokes=" "). In casa-rs that input adds no Stokes coordinate, matching the empty-string case.

    When coordsys.fromrecord receives a nonstring parentName, CASA reports a record type error after installing the decoded coordinates. casa-rs validates the parent-name field before replacing the existing system.

    CASA also terminates the process when a coordinate replacement vector has two dimensions, such as worldreplace0=[[1.0]] for a one-axis Stokes coordinate. Casa-rs reports a record type error and retains the former coordinate system.

    tb.getcol refuses a column of int64¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-30
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1025, the table tool)
    • Affected code: casatools src/code/stdcasa/StdCasa/CasacSupport.cc (fromValueHolder)

    tb.getcol of a scalar column of Int64 raises RuntimeError: Unknown casa DataType! in CASA 6.7.6.14, while tb.getcell of the same column returns the value as a Python int and getcol of an Int (32-bit) column returns an int64 array. fromValueHolder, which turns the column's ValueHolder into the Python value, has a TpInt64 case for a scalar but none for TpArrayInt64 (the converter for records in the same file has both). The recording is crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_table_tool.json (cells, getcol('id'); the fixture's id column is Int64). casa-rs returns the int64 array, as it does for every other integer column. This also affects the _COUNT_ column of a result from TaQL COUNT: getcol("_COUNT_") raises the same error; getcell("_COUNT_", row) returns the count. See taql_count_int64 in that recording (#1028).

    tb.putkeyword stores an int beyond 32 bits as 0¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-30
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1025, the table tool)
    • Affected code: casatools ValueHolder conversion for table keywords

    tb.putkeyword('name', 2**40) returns True and tb.getkeyword('name') then gives 0, both for a new keyword and for an existing 32-bit Int one (CASA 6.7.6.14; the casa_bugs scenario of crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_table_tool.json). The Python int is narrowed to casacore's 32-bit Int without a range check. casa-rs stores a new keyword whose value does not fit 32 bits as a 64-bit integer, and refuses such a value for an existing 32-bit keyword with RuntimeError: ... does not fit the integer keyword NAME, which holds fewer bits, rather than store a wrong number.

    cl.putkeyword uses the same CASA conversion: in CASA 6.7.6.14, writing 2**40 to either a new keyword or an existing 32-bit keyword stores 0. casa-rs keeps a new value as a 64-bit integer and refuses an overflowing replacement of a 32-bit keyword, so neither call silently loses the value (#1053).

    Some tb slice calls with invalid coordinates abort the process¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-30
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1025, the table tool)
    • Affected code: casatools slice calls that pass coordinates directly to casacore's Slicer

    On a 3×4 CASA image opened as a table, CASA 6.7.6.14 aborts on tb.getcolslice("map", [0, 0], [2, 3], [1]) with an uncaught casacore::ArraySlicerError and exit status 134. The one-element step has a different rank from the cell. casa-rs takes that step on every axis and raises a RuntimeError for invalid coordinates. The slices and ms_slices oracle recordings contain calls for which CASA returns or raises a Python exception.

    getcellslice on the same image expands the default step [1] to every axis and raises Python exceptions for invalid coordinates. It accepts either -1 or [-1] to select the first or last index of every axis for the respective corner. CASA rejects negative indices inside a multidimensional corner for getcellslice. For getcolslice, a -1 top-right index selects the last element of that axis. casa-rs follows both conventions.

    tb.putcell and putcol wrap an int beyond 32 bits in an int cell¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-30
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1025, the table tool)
    • Affected code: casatools ValueHolder conversion to a column's type

    tb.putcell('ANTENNA1', 0, 2147483648) returns True and the cell then holds -2147483648; -2147483649 becomes 2147483647 and 4294967297 becomes 1 (CASA 6.7.6.14; the int_overflow scenario of crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_table_tool.json, which the replay leaves out). putcol wraps the same way. A Python int is narrowed to the 32-bit column type without a range check, as for putkeyword above. casa-rs raises RuntimeError: a value is out of range for a column of Int and leaves the cell, rather than store a wrong number. Astra's review of #1034 noted the departure.

    tb.putcell with several rows writes those before a missing one¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-30
    • Status: CASA behaviour that casa-rs does not follow (#1025, the table tool)
    • Affected code: casacore TableProxy::putCell

    tb.putcell('id', [0, 3], 22) on a table of three rows writes row 0, then fails on row 3 with no such row: TableProxy::putCell checks and writes one row at a time, so a call that raises has still changed the table (rows_vector scenario). casa-rs checks every row first and raises with the table as it was. The recording follows the failed call with a write that puts row 0 back, so that its later reads agree.

    tb.addrows with a negative count removes rows¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-31
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1025, the table tool)
    • Affected code: casacore TableProxy::addRow (the count is an Int handed to Table::addRow(rownr_t))

    tb.addrows(-1) on a table of three rows returns True and leaves two; addrows(-2) leaves none, and the table reopens with no rows (addrows_negative scenario). The count converts to a huge unsigned number and the table's row count wraps. casa-rs raises RuntimeError ("the number of rows must not be negative") and changes nothing.

    tb.removerows and tb.addcols do part of a call that fails¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-31
    • Status: CASA behaviour that casa-rs does not follow (#1025, the table tool; Astra's review of #1036)
    • Affected code: casacore BaseTable::removeRow(Vector) and TableProxy::addColumns

    tb.removerows([2, 2]) on a table of three rows removes the first row 2, then raises "rownr 2 too high ... (#rows=2)", leaving two rows: the row numbers are taken from the highest down and each is checked against the table as it then is. (A number beyond the table, even among valid ones, fails before any row goes: it is the highest.) tb.addcols of aaa and id on a table that has id adds aaa and then raises "column already exists": addColumns builds the whole description first (a malformed field fails the call with nothing added) and then adds the columns one by one, in the record's sorted order (structure_partial scenario). casa-rs checks the whole call before it changes anything: both raise CASA's message and leave the table as it was. The successful calls, repeats included (removerows([0, 0]) removes two rows), are CASA's.

    importfits reads every FITS date as UTC¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-30
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1017)
    • Affected code: casacore coordinates/Coordinates/FITSCoordinateUtil.cc (getObsInfo, which reads TIMESYS from the header record, and the WCS date wcslib holds)

    exportfits writes an image's observation date in the epoch's own time scale, DATE-OBS with TIMESYS naming the scale (ObsInfo::toFITS). importfits gives the date back with the same calendar value but labelled UTC, whatever TIMESYS says. An image observed at 2020-05-31 12:00 TAI exported by CASA 6.7.6 with TIMESYS = 'TAI' imports at 2020-05-31 12:00 UTC, 37 seconds later. Where the scale is lost was not pinned down: getObsInfo reads TIMESYS from the header record and the date from wcslib's mjdobs, so the scale is gone by then. casa-rs reads MJD-OBS or DATE-OBS in the scale TIMESYS names, UTC when it names none (casa_coordinates::fits::from_fits_header), so the round trip keeps the date.

    feather fails on an image whose direction is not on its first two axes¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-30
    • Status: CASA limitation; casa-rs does not follow it (#1002, PR #1022 review)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/MeasurementEquations/Feather.cc (its FFT, cursor and weight-image paths)

    feather finds an image's direction axes for part of its setup, but its lattice cursors and FFTs assume the direction plane is the first two axes. CASA 6.7.6.14's feather of a pair of 16 by 16 pixel images, one Stokes and two channels, with 2″ and 6″ beams, succeeds over [RA, Dec, Stokes, Freq]. The same pair over [Stokes, RA, Dec, Freq] or [Freq, RA, Dec, Stokes], as imtrans makes it, fails with LatticeStepper::setCursorShape: cursorShape <=0 or > latticeShape. casa-rs feathers each direction plane wherever its axes are. The transposed pair's result is the transpose of the direction-first pair's (casa-images casa_axis_maps test feather_takes_the_direction_wherever_its_axes_are), and the direction-first result is checked against CASA's in image_analysis_casa.

    tb.copyrows adds the rows before it finds a cell it cannot convert, and wraps integers¶

    • Date noted: 2026-10-31
    • Status: CASA behaviour that casa-rs does not follow (#1025, the table tool; Astra's review of #1039)
    • Affected code: casacore TableCopy::copyRows and ROTableRow/TableRow::put (RecordInterface::as...)

    copyRows adds an output row and then puts its cells one by one. When a shared column has a type the input's cell cannot be converted to (a double output column for a dcomplex input, RecordInterface::asDouble - invalid data type; an int output column for a float array input, TableRecordRep::get_pointer - incorrect data type used for field ...), the call raises with the rows already added and their cells never set: reading them gives whatever the heap held (copyrows_types scenario of crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_table_tool.json, which does not compare the table after a refusal). casa-rs checks the types before it changes anything and raises with the output table as it was.

    Which conversions CASA accepts was probed pair by pair and is what casa-rs accepts (tools/table/cell_convert.rs), with one exception: an integer that does not fit the output column's type is wrapped as a C cast does (a Short -5 becomes the uChar 251, a uInt 4294967291), the same narrowing as putcell above. casa-rs raises RuntimeError: a value is out of range for a column of ..., before the output is touched, rather than store a wrong number.

    Later timerange terms take their defaults from row 0¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA quirk casa-rs follows for parity (#980, casa-ms selection::time_expr)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSSel/MSTimeParse.cc (getDefaults)

    A timerange time with missing fields (09:00:00, *, 2017/03/01) takes them, and a single time's tolerance and a bracketed range's edge width take the EXPOSURE, from the "first logical row". getDefaults sets firstLogicalRow = 0 and searches for the first logical row only while the static defaultTimeComputed is false, which its first call makes true. Every comma-separated term calls it once, so the first term reads the first logical row and every later term reads row 0. With field='1' on an MS whose FIELD 1 rows start a day after row 0, CASA 6.7.6.14 selects 10:30:00.5 on FIELD 1's day in 10:30:00.5,10:40:00.5 but looks for 10:40:00.5 on row 0's day (fields cases of resources/casa-compat/evidence/msselection-time-casa-20261102.json). casa-rs resolves the terms the same way (TimeSelectorDefaults).

    The timerange defaults ignore FLAG_ROW when other selections are set¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA quirk casa-rs follows for parity (#980)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSSel/MSTimeParse.cc (getDefaults)

    MSSelection builds its time parser with honourRowFlags true. With no other selection expression, the first logical row is the first row whose FLAG_ROW is false. With one, getDefaults tests FLAG_ROW but both branches of the test read the other expressions' condition the same way, so the first logical row is the first row they select, flagged or not. casa-rs follows both (msselection_first_logical_row).

    A refused bracketed timerange makes later ranges edge-inclusive¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs does not follow (#980)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSSel/MSTimeGram.yy (brangetimeexpr)

    The grammar keeps a bracketed range's state in the process globals MSTimeEdgeInclusiveRange and MSTimeEdgeBuffer, set when [ (or N[) is read and cleared only when a range completes. A bracketed range that is refused ([09:00:00], 10.25[09:00:00~08:00:00]) leaves them set, so the next unbracketed range anywhere in the process is edge-inclusive with that width until a range completes. In CASA 6.7.6.14, 09:00:00~09:01:00 after 10.25[09:00:00~08:00:00] selects 35 rows of a 2 s MS instead of 30, and 09:00:00.5+00:00:30 after 0.0[09:00:10~09:00:00] loses both end rows (bracket_state_leaks in resources/casa-compat/evidence/msselection-time-casa-20261102.json, whose other cases each run after a completed range clears the state). casa-rs keeps no state between parses.

    MSIter keeps the previous window's receptor angles for a window without its own FEED row¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: casacore behaviour that casa-rs does not follow (MSv2 adapter, Feeds::receptors)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MeasurementSets/MSIter.cc (setFeedInfo, lines 779-814 on master)

    The MeasurementSet definition makes a FEED row with SPECTRAL_WINDOW_ID -1 valid for every spectral window. setFeedInfo instead decides once per table: if every row is -1 it uses them for every window, and otherwise (spwDepFeed_p) it copies into its cache only the rows whose window is the iterator's current one. A window with no row of its own for an antenna and feed (a -1 row beside rows for other windows, or no row at all, as for ALMA's WVR window) leaves that cache entry untouched: receptorAngles_p is resized to the same shape, which casacore's Array::resize does not reinitialise, so receptorAngles() gives the previous window's angles (or, for the first window iterated, zeros: the new array's elements are value-initialised). XRADIO 1.3.1's converter makes the same per-dataset choice and writes such a dataset with no receptor angles. casa-rs reads an antenna and feed's rows in the window, else its rows for every window, and states no receptors where it has neither. Where FEED's rows are all -1 or all name windows, which is how CASA's simulator, ALMA and casa-rs's MSv2 writer write it, the receptors are the same.

    spw "no match" warnings refuse the expression¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows for parity (#980, casa-ms selection::spw_expr)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSSel/MSSSpwErrorHandler.h, MSSelection.cc (initErrorHandler, setErrorHandler)

    MSSSpwErrorHandler only logs its reports (handleError posts a WARN), so a spw term matching nothing (0,7 on a 4-window MeasurementSet, an unknown name) looks meant to be a warning. But it does not override clone(), and MSSelection installs mssEH->clone(), a base MSSelectionErrorHandler, which throws: CASA 6.7.6.14 refuses 0,7 with "Spw Expression: No match found for 7," (resources/casa-compat/evidence/msselection-spw-casa-20261102.json). casa-rs refuses it too.

    A spw frequency range with a negative step can loop forever¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs does not follow (#980)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSSel/MSSpwIndex.cc (matchFrequencyRange)

    A stepped window range f0~f1^s with s < 0 steps by each window's least CHAN_WIDTH, in single precision. A window whose widths are negative then steps away from f1 and never leaves the loop; a step too small to move a single-precision frequency stalls it. CASA 6.7.6.14's ms.msselect does not return for 4GHz~5GHz^-1MHz on a MeasurementSet with a descending window (not_returning in the evidence file). casa-rs refuses such expressions.

    spw channel quirks casa-rs follows¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-02
    • Status: CASA quirks casa-rs follows for parity (#980)
    • Affected code: casacore ms/MSSel/MSSpwIndex.cc, MSSpwParse.cc, MSSpwGram.yy

    A single window frequency (1.4GHz) matches a window within its largest signed CHAN_WIDTH of the window's midpoint, so never a window whose widths are negative. A channel range in hertz reads the channel order from the sign of the first CHAN_WIDTH, not from the frequencies. When a window's first frequency channel range finds no channel, selectChannelsFromIDList skips one range instead of the window's ranges, so the next window takes the skipped window's later ranges (0~2:4.5GHz~4.55GHz;0~1 gives window 2 the ranges 0~1 and -1~63). In a window list, a frequency replaces the list but not its write position (100GHz~101GHz;0 lists the matched windows, then window 0). ( ... ) skips endOfCeremony, so the expression keeps every MAIN row. All are in the evidence file; casa-ms reproduces them, and tclean refuses the parenthesized form and a window kept without channels, whose imaging in CASA is not established.

    A tuned cube selection compares outframe frequencies with LSRK ones¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs does not follow (#980, casa-imaging-application compile::frequency_bands)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SynthesisImagerVi2.cc (selectData, andFreqSelection), SynthesisImager.cc (tuneSelectData), CubeMajorCycleAlgorithm.cc (task)

    A cube is gridded by CubeMajorCycleAlgorithm's own SynthesisImagerVi2. Its selectData states each channel range spw names as a frequency band in tclean's outframe (MSUtil::getFreqRangeInSpw over the range's channel centres at every time of the window's rows) and stores it in freqBegs_p and freqEnds_p. Where tuneSelectData tunes the selection (the first visibility buffer's window has 30 or more channels, or the model is saved to MODEL_DATA), it calls selectData again with the image's band, pixel −1.5 to nchan + 0.5, in LSRK, and andFreqSelection narrows each stored band that band overlaps to their intersection, without converting either: a band in TOPO or BARY numbers is intersected with one in LSRK numbers, and the result is read as LSRK (FrequencySelectionUsingFrame(LSRK)). The two frames differ by up to tens of km/s, so with channels narrower than that, an image whose band overlaps the stored one in those numbers loses the image's edge channels (or gains channels CASA never meant to read) at every time. The same andFreqSelection narrows two of a window's own channel ranges to their intersection when a Doppler shift across the track makes their bands overlap, which keeps almost nothing of either. casa-rs states a tuned selection's bands in LSRK before intersecting them with the image's, and keeps each named range's band whole; with outframe='LSRK', the default, and ranges further apart than the shift, the selections agree. No case of CASA's test suite reaches either difference: the one tuned cube with channels narrower than the shift, test_cube_D2, images in LSRK, where casa-rs and CASA select the same channels at every time (its edge plane keeps 2460.56 of 5020.51 in both).

    The tuned bounds also pass through SynthesisImager::doubleToString, which uses std::to_string. Its six fractional digits are part of the channel selection. On the LSRK input cubic AW fixture with BARY output, CASA converts the upper tuned edge to 1524999999.9999998 Hz and passes 1525000000.000000 Hz to the iterator. A stored channel starting at that rounded edge is included. The same TOPO request already has an upper edge 2.4 μHz above it. casa-rs applies the observed rounding before the inclusive channel intersection. A channel whose edge remains beyond the rounded bound stays outside.

    A TOPO cube can gain or lose an endpoint with CASA's aberration cache history¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, the image's TOPO spectral coordinate has an LSRK conversion layer. FTMachine::initMaps evaluates image-plane frequencies through that layer and FTMachine::interpolateFrequencyTogrid applies strict interpolation bounds to the resulting frequencies and the row's LSRK channels.

    The GEO_BARY hop evaluates Aberration with coefficients from the shared MeasTable::theirMulAber cache. MeasTableMul::getArray may reuse the coefficients of a nearby epoch. Pre-populating that cache before an identical tclean run moves the converted grid by a few floating-point steps. In the original out-of-order-row VLA fixture, the last plane's sum of weights changed from 16,697.6055 to zero. Two nearby frequency grids also flipped a whole plane, in opposite directions. A separate fresh-coefficients probe reproduced the flips. Reproduction and measurements in #1198.

    For a channel-index TOPO cube in the standard gridder that spans one LSRK spectral window at unit width with natural weighting, casa-rs can compare in the source coordinate when the rounded terminal centre coincides with the stored edge. It compares the exact requested span (n - 1) * d to the exact span between the stored endpoint centres. This distinguishes an in-band terminal centre from a genuinely out-of-band one even when both round to the stored edge. Saved controls fall 7/32 of a representable step inside and 3/8 of a step outside. The source proof retains the former and excludes the latter from gridding.

    The ordinary strict row bounds and flag rules still apply. The full TOPO image coordinate, primary-beam calculation and prediction basis retain all requested channels.

    A tracked BARY cube can lose the same physical endpoint through a different round trip. On the three-channel refim_point_linRL.ms TOPO input with start=0, width=1, CASA's TOPO and BARY image centres describe the same frequencies. The first LSRK row frequency is 999988784.6184165 Hz. CASA's TOPO grid evaluates the first centre one representable step above it, while its BARY grid evaluates that centre one step below. The following 13 buffers are at least 83 Hz above the first image centre and legitimately exclude it. Both the standard and AW CASA gridders drop the whole first plane in the BARY request. The release AW comparison has 94,625 differing mask pixels on that plane and takes four clean iterations instead of three.

    casa-rs retains the first buffer's contribution in both coordinates. CASA's TOPO AW run on the same rows and cache supplies the reference for the BARY products. CASA Measures verifies the corresponding image centres in LSRK within 0.5 microhertz. The products, masks and clean counts agree against that reference. A separate explicit BARY start nearly 99 kHz below the first channel gives a blank first plane in both standard gridders.

    The same cache dependence affects the first plane of an LSRK image of TOPO data. In CASA 6.7.6.14, refim_eptwochan.ms imaged with the standard gridder, natural weighting, two channels, start=0, width=1 and linear interpolation can give first-plane sumwt of either 0 or 552. Evaluating one TOPO-to-LSRK frequency at 300 s before the earliest integration with the stock Measures tool changes the result from 0 to 552. Both runs report the same first centre, 999988750.3872572 Hz, and second-plane sumwt of 321786.

    VisibilityIteratorImpl2::getFrequencies converts the visibility frequencies at each buffer's epoch. FTMachine::interpolateFrequencyTogrid samples the image's LSRK grid within those row bounds. On the native first buffer the lower visibility frequency is 999988750.3872573 Hz. The one-point fine grid derived from the image edges puts its first sample exactly there. At the next integration even the lowest visibility frequency is about 2 Hz higher, so only the first buffer contributes to the first plane. The same bound rejects a fine point below the first visibility frequency by one representable step.

    With CASA's first plane retained and the direction mask supplied on the output grid, the CASA and native Högbom runs each keep 156 mask pixels per plane. Sumwt and controller totals agree exactly. All seven images agree within 1.21e-7 normalized RMS. The original cold-CASA zero plane is cache-dependent. Measurements and regression in #823.

    A cube's fine frequency grid lets rounding choose one or two samples per plane¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA rounding casa-rs does not follow (#980; test_cube.test_cube_5 differs by it)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/TransformMachines2/FTMachine.cc (interpolateFrequencyTogrid)

    For linear interpolation onto planes wider than a channel, CASA places floor(width) points per plane, with width the ratio of the plane spacing (imageFreq_p, from the image's spectral coordinate and its LSRK conversion layer) to the visibility buffer's first channel spacing (its channels converted to LSRK by the visibility iterator). Where planes are a whole number of channels wide, the ratio is that number exactly at the image's epoch, and after it the Doppler shift moves it by about 1e-6, below or above. test_cube_5 (refim_point.ms, 50 MHz TOPO channels, width='100MHz', outframe='TOPO') has a ratio of 2 at its first integration: the image's epoch is that integration's time and its direction the field's to the last bit, and the shift then lowers the ratio below 2 at every later integration, where both implementations place one point per plane. At the first integration CASA's two conversions round apart and its ratio falls below 2, so it places one point there too; casa-rs's ratio is 2 to rounding and it places two. Every plane of casa-rs's cube therefore holds the first integration's weight twice: its sum of weights exceeds CASA's by exactly that integration's share of the track, 0.0017154 (the rows of one of its 480 integrations, unflagged, over all), and its PSFs differ by 7e-4 to 1.3e-3 normalized RMS, the returned maxpsfsidelobe by 3e-4. Which side of 2 a ratio that is 2 exactly falls on is rounding, which casa-rs does not reproduce (AGENTS.md: a near-tie broken the other way is equally valid).

    For the channel-index TOPO cube on LSRK data in #1256, that ratio can differ for the whole track. In the five-channel VLA fixture, stock CASA uses one sample per plane at width=2. With fresh aberration coefficients in both runs, changing only the cached UT1−UTC value to the value at the image epoch changes the ratio from just below two to exactly two, doubling the sample count.

    For a standard, natural-weighted, linear channel-index TOPO cube on LSRK data, casa-rs evaluates the grid in LSRK and takes the lower sample count at an exact integral ratio above one. Ratios on either side still use CASA's floor. Strict interpolation bounds continue to apply to every gridded sample. The width-two control agrees with stock CASA, and the width-one control still agrees. Trace and controls in #1256.

    A cube without a per-channel density leaves <imagename>.gridwt behind¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs does not follow (#980; test_onefield.test_onefield_pcwdT_and_pcwdF.2's inventory differs by it)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SynthesisImagerVi2.cc (runCubeGridding), SynthesisImager.cc (getWeightDensity, setWeightDensity), SIImageStore.cc (gridwt), CubeMajorCycleAlgorithm.cc (task)

    A cube weighted by Briggs or uniform weighting with perchanweightdensity=False forms one VisImagingWeight density in the imager defining the cube. To hand it to the CubeMajorCycleAlgorithm imagers that grid the cube, runCubeGridding asks getWeightDensity, which writes the density grid to a new paged image <imagename>.gridwt and passes its name in the control record (weightdensity); each gridding imager reads it back with setWeightDensity. Nothing removes the file. A later run into the same name forms its density again and overwrites it, and reads the file only for the shape of an image store that has no other product (SIImageStore's constructor, whose comment there asks "How can this be right ?"). Its pixels are a uv-plane density, but its coordinates are the image's sky coordinates, with a FIELD_ORDER axis added for several fields. casa-rs forms the density once and grids with it in the same process, so it writes no such file; CASA 6.7.6.14 writes tst2.gridwt in test_onefield_pcwdT_and_pcwdF's second call, whose other products agree.

    W projection grids a support the grid's edge cuts, and never its last row and column¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA behaviour casa-rs follows for parity (#980, casa-imaging-operator GridEdge::Sectioned)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/TransformMachines2/WProjectFT.cc (put), FTMachine.cc (findGridSector, tweakGridSector), synthesis/fortran/wprojgrid.f (sectgwgridd, sectgwgrids, onmywgrid, sectdwgrid, owp)

    WProjectFT::put grids through the sectioned sectgwgridd: each sector findGridSector cuts grids the taps of a sample that fall on it and adds weight · Σ Re(cwt) over those taps to sumwt, wherever the sample's anchor lies (onmywgrid asks only that the support meet the sector). A support that overruns the grid is therefore gridded in part and weighed by the part, where the unsectioned gwgrid and the degridder (sectdwgrid through owp) drop it. The sectors start at the grid's first column and row (both findGridSector and tweakGridSector add one to the 0-based start, giving the 1-based x0 = 1, and onmywgrid clips a support's low end to it), and findGridSector cuts the last one short (nxsub = nxp - x0 - 1 before that increment), so they cover every column and row but the grid's last: no tap lands before the grid, and the last column and row are never gridded, though owp predicts a support that reaches them. casa-rs grids the taps on the sectors and weighs them, as CASA does. Dropping the whole sample, as casa-rs did, left test_cube_wproj_onefield's 1.5 and 1.8 GHz planes (refim_oneshiftpoint.mosaic.ms, four planes, whose supports overrun the grid at those frequencies) short of CASA's sumwt by 3.06 and 14.57 and their PSFs 3.6% and 5.3% apart in normalized RMS; they now agree with CASA 6.7.6.14 to 8e-7. (An earlier reading of the sectors as starting at the 0-based column 0, so that a tap one column below the grid would be written before it and weighed, mistook findGridSector's start before its final increment for the start it returns.)

    The standard gridder drops the final Fourier-grid row and column¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 bug. Casa-rs keeps the full support of samples that fit the standard grid. MVC comparison
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines2/GridFT.cc (put), FTMachine.cc (findGridSector) and synthesis/fortran/fgridft.f (sectggridd, onmygrid).

    onmygrid accepts a standard-kernel support that fits the entire grid. findGridSector shortens the final sector by one extra cell before it converts the sector start to one-based indices. For one sector on a 320-square grid, CASA passes (x0, y0, nxsub, nysub) = (1, 1, 319, 319). The sectioned Fortran kernel consequently drops taps on the final row or column and omits their fraction of the weight. The omitted taps shift the cube PSF and residual upstream of spectral-index masking.

    In the frozen 256-square, 0.3-arcsec, three-channel dirty MVC comparison, CASA's unmodified replay reproduces the reference exactly. Its .alpha and .alpha.error validity masks differ from casa-rs at the same two pixels near the strict residual-peak cutoff. Replaying the captured CASA gridding inputs with its short sector reconstructs its Fourier grid to 6.1e-16 normalized RMS. Extending only those two sector lengths to 320 in a diagnostic CASA process yields agreement on all 19 products under the existing limits, with identical masks and maximum NRMS 7.27e-6. The standard operator's edge regression keeps nonzero taps and full weight on both boundaries.

    A CRTF spectral range rounds each end to a channel, toward zero¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA quirk casa-rs follows for parity (#980)
    • Affected code: casatools imageanalysis/Annotations/AnnRegion.cc (_extend), casacore images/Regions/WCBox.cc (doToLCRegion), lattices/LRegions/LCBox.cc

    A region's range=[f1, f2] masks the channels from Int(p1 + 0.5) to Int(p2 + 0.5), where p1 ≤ p2 are the ends' pixels on the image's spectral axis. Int truncates toward zero, then the low end is clamped to 0 and the high end to the last channel. A region whose ends cross is left out, and the other regions are kept. Because of the truncation, a range ending up to 1.5 channels below the cube masks channel 0, while one starting half a channel above it masks nothing. An MFS image's one channel is its whole bandwidth wide. casa-rs follows this, measured with CASA 6.7.6.14 on refim_point.ms's cube and MFS image: rg.fromtext and tclean's .mask for 20 ranges, in the unit test compile::domains::tests::a_spectral_range_masks_the_channels_casa_masks.

    A mask string of several lines is an empty mask in tclean¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs corrects (#980)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SDMaskHandler.cc (reading mask text)

    CASA 6.7.6.14's tclean given mask='circle[[50pix,50pix],10pix]\nbox[[10pix,10pix],[12pix,12pix]]' makes an empty mask and stops at code 7 without cleaning. The same regions as a list, or as lines of a region file, mask both regions. casa-rs reads every line of the text as a region of the mask, as the list and the file read.

    tclean checks a vis list for repeats of its first entry only¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs corrects (#980)
    • Affected code: casatasks private/mslisthelper.py (check_mslist), called by ImagerParameters.checkAndFixSelectionPars

    CASA 6.7.6.14's tclean refuses vis=['a.ms', 'a.ms'] with "a.ms is contained in the list more than once." (ValueError, before any imaging). The check compares each later entry with the first one only, after the list is sorted by time, and as text. So ['a.ms', 'b.ms', 'b.ms'] and ['a.ms', './a.ms'] pass it, and their samples are read twice. The message states the evident intent: no MeasurementSet twice. casa-rs refuses any repeat, comparing canonical paths, with CASA's message. Evidence: CASA's refusal of [refim_point.ms, refim_point.ms] on the oracle host, 2026-11-03, and the T1 law vis_list::a_measurement_set_named_twice_is_refused.

    A common beam's circular case is the reciprocal of its axis¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs corrects (#980)
    • Affected code: casatools imageanalysis/ImageAnalysis/CasaImageBeamSet.cc (_transformEllipseByScaling, used by getCommonBeam)

    _transformEllipseByScaling computes a scaled ellipse's axes as c1 = sqrt(1/k1) and c2 = sqrt(1/k2). When they are exactly equal, a circle, it returns sqrt(k1) for both axes instead: the reciprocal of the axis. That contradicts the general branch and the function's evident intent. casa-rs returns the axis. Both take the circle branch only for exactly equal axes, which scaling rarely produces. An earlier casa-rs absolute tolerance of 1e-12 took sub-arcsecond beams in radians for circles of the reciprocal axis, about 3e6, and the common-beam search then never ended. casa-rs works in radians where CASA works in arcseconds; the common beams agree within one of the algorithm's 0.1% growth steps (casa-numerics law the_common_beam_of_sub_arcsecond_beams_is_found_whatever_the_unit). The two searches can stop at different enclosing beams. For four nearly equal channel beams (axis ratio 1.17, the simulation round trip's cube, 2026-11-03), the axes differ by 2.2e-4 and the position angle by 0.036°.

    tclean refuses a projection name with spaces round it¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: a casa-rs choice (#1064)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SynthesisUtilMethods.cc (SynthesisParamsImage::fromRecord, reading projection)

    CASA 6.7.6.14's tclean refuses projection=' TAN' with "Invalid projection code : TAN", as it refuses 'tan' and 'XYZ': casacore's Projection::type reads the name exactly. casa-rs's parameter catalog trims every string parameter before tclean reads it (projection's normalization is trim), so casa-rs images ' TAN' in TAN. A name of the wrong case or one casacore does not know is refused as CASA refuses it. No result of CASA's depends on the refusal, and following it would make projection the one string parameter the catalog does not trim. Evidence: CASA's refusals in crates/casa-imaging-application/tests/data/projections-casa-6.7.6.14.json, and the tclean_tests rows casa_values_become_the_request_casa_rs_runs and values_casa_rs_cannot_honour_are_refused.

    NCP on the celestial equator places every pixel at NaN¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA defect casa-rs corrects by refusing (#1064)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SynthesisUtilMethods.cc (SynthesisParamsImage::buildCoordinateSystem)

    CASA writes projection='NCP' as SIN slanted by [0, cot δ₀], with no guard for an image centred on the celestial equator, where cot δ₀ is infinite: the image records [0, inf] and every pixel's world direction is NaN, so no pointing, beam or mask can be placed on it. casa-rs refuses such a run before imaging, with an error that says why (PrepareError::NcpAtEquator, the law ncp_on_the_celestial_equator_is_refused). Any other declination, however close to 0, is imaged as CASA images it.

    Cubic spectral interpolation reads past a short axis and fits flagged values¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs corrects (#1066)
    • Affected code: casacore scimath/Mathematics/InterpolateArray1D.tcc (interpolatePtr's cubic case and polynomialInterpolation), called by casatools synthesis/TransformMachines2/FTMachine.cc (interpolateFrequencyTogrid, interpolateFrequencyFromgrid)

    tclean's interpolation='cubic' fits casacore's cubic polynomial through the four points about each frequency. polynomialInterpolation takes four points whatever the axis holds (a DebugAssert only), so a row of two or three selected channels, or an image of two output channels, reads past the end of its arrays. casa-rs fits the polynomial through every point such an axis has. The cubic case also notes "TODO: implement flags properly - ie don't use flagged points": a sample takes the flags of the two channels it lies between, but its value fits all four, flagged ones included, so a flagged channel stored as NaN makes an unflagged sample NaN and the image NaN with it. casa-rs fits a flagged channel's stored value as CASA does and counts a non-finite one as zero.
