The model half follows CASA too: a residual sample is Σ f·(V − M) over its four channels, and the model M is zero at a flagged one, since interpolateFrequencyFromgrid writes the prediction through swapyz(flipgrid, copyOfFlag, flipdata), which skips every entry vb.flagCube() flags. Weights are interpolated the same way; VisImagingWeight has already given a flagged channel zero weight, which casa-rs follows. Evidence: the operator laws in casa-imaging-operator/tests/cubic.rs and the cubic cases of resources/casa-compat/tclean-cube-axes-cases.json.

    A one-channel start model is regridded onto a cube by reading past its axis¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs corrects, by refusing (#1067)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SIImageStore.cc (regridToModelImage), casacore images/Images/ImageRegrid.tcc (regrid1D) and scimath/Mathematics/InterpolateArray1D.tcc (linear)

    SIImageStore::regridToModelImage regrids a start model over its direction and spectral axes with ImageRegrid and Interpolate2D::LINEAR. Along frequency, regrid1D hands casacore's linear interpolation the input's channel pixels, and for a model of one channel onto a cube of several the interpolation steps its bracketing index to -1 and reads before the start of its arrays. casa-rs refuses a one-channel start model on a cube's spectral axis ("a spectral regrid interpolates between two channels"). A one-plane model of an MFS image keeps its plane at any frequency, as CASA's regrid leaves a degenerate axis. Beyond the model's channels CASA's interpolation takes the end channel's value and only masks it; the model image has no mask, so those planes start from the end channel's model. casa-rs follows that, and also CASA's restoration of such a plane where no sample reaches it: SIImageStore::restore restores every plane, and makeImageBeamSet gives a blank PSF the largest beam, so the restored image there is the model convolved with that beam. Evidence: casa-images regrid::spectral laws, the start-model axis laws, and CASA's test_modelvis_7 and test_startmodel_regrid_cube.

    A restarted AW cube's weight image grows with the other channels' sums¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: CASA bug casa-rs does not follow (#719)
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/TransformMachines2/CFCache.cc (CFCache::loadWtImage), synthesis/ImagerObjects/SIImageStore.cc (divideWeightBySumwt, divideResidualByWeight)

    SynthesisImagerVi2 names the run's image as the weight prefix (setWtImagePrefix), so AWProjectWBFT::initializeToSky reads an existing .weight back as its average PB (CFCache::loadAvgPB), and loadWtImage multiplies it by max(sumWtTmp.get()): the largest sum of weights over all channels. .weight was divided by each channel's own sum (divideWeightBySumwt), so a second tclean call on the same image name scales channel c's weight by max(sumwt)/sumwt[c]. divideResidualByWeight, which divides by the PB peak times the weight's root, then moves its residual and every product after it. The result depends on whether an earlier call made the image and on how other channels were flagged, and no sky could change it. Each reload also applies AWProjectFT::getWeightImage's sinc correction again, a smaller effect of about 1e-5 NRMS that a channel with the largest sum keeps.

    refim_oneshiftpoint.mosaic.ms, three channels, niter=10: a fresh call's .weight peaks are 1.0000, 1.0041 and 1.0233 and its channel 2 residual peak is 0.1019; after a niter=0 call on that name they are 1.0000, 1.0081 and 1.0472, and 0.1139. casa-rs computes the weight image afresh, and agrees with CASA's fresh call within 5.7e-7 NRMS on every product; on one window, where max(sumwt) is the channel's own, CASA's restart also stays consistent and agrees within 5e-5. test_mosaic_cube.test_cube_awproject_* make that second call, so they differ from casa-rs on the channels the reload scales. Evidence: resources/casa-compat/evidence/tclean-aw-cube-20261103.json.

    A one-channel AW cube's restart writes its images with no default mask¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-03
    • Status: a CASA inconsistency casa-rs does not follow (#719)
    • Affected code: not traced

    A second tclean call on the same image name, on a cube of one channel with gridder='awproject', leaves CASA's .image and .residual with no default mask, where a fresh call and a restart of a cube of several channels have mask0. casa-rs writes mask0, the PB cut, each time. The pixels agree within 5e-5 NRMS. aw_restart_one_window in resources/casa-compat/tclean-aw-cube-cases.json shows it and fails on the mask alone.

    A one-channel mosaic cube can lose its residual mask during a major cycle¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-14
    • Status: CASA cache-dependent defect. The corresponding one-channel restart check remains on #944.
    • Affected code: SynthesisImagerVi2::runCubeGridding, SIImageStore::copyMask.

    On CASA 6.7.6.14, image one channel of refim_alma_mosaic.ms with gridder='mosaic', specmode='cube', natural weighting, Högbom, 480 pixels, 0.06 arcsec cells, pblimit=0.2 and pbcor=True. CLEAN eight iterations, edit one pixel of the residual mask, then resume for twelve iterations with calcpsf=False, calcres=False. CASA logs that residual/mask0 is already open in the table cache during both calls. Its mask copy removes the old default before trying to create the replacement. The error is caught as a T/F mask warning. The residual and restored image are left without masks even though the saved PB has mask0. In the corresponding two-channel runs the copy succeeds and both products receive the saved PB mask.

    casa-rs keeps the generated PB mask on the positive-cutoff cube path. In the initial one-channel CLEAN, the raw CASA masks differ by 45,662 pixels on each of these two products. All nine products have normalized RMS at most 5.20e-7 and the controller counts agree. See #1424 for the separate verified two-channel saved-product behavior.

    A cube's beam for a plane without one depends on how memory splits the cube¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-04
    • Status: CASA behaviour; casa-rs does not follow it (#1130)
    • Affected code: CubeMajorCycleAlgorithm.cc (dividePSFByWeight, makeImageBeamSet per chunk)

    A blank plane, or one whose PSF peaks off-centre, takes the largest beam of the image store that makeImageBeamSet runs on. In a cube each chunk runs it on its own sub-store, and the number of chunks follows the memory available. Forcing two sub-cubes through system.resources.memory, on refim_point_linRL.ms at 4 channels:

    Run Blank channel 3's beam The store's largest
    1 sub-cube 130.47" 130.47"
    2 sub-cubes 98.12" 130.47"

    With 2 sub-cubes channel 0 also stops being off-centre, peaking at the centre with a beam of 196.38". So a large cube's beams and Clark patches can depend on the machine. casa-rs takes the largest of the whole image domain, which is CASA's answer for one sub-cube, and doesn't depend on the machine.

    Multiscale misplaces a component on a plane whose PSF peaks off-centre¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-04
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1130)
    • Affected code: MatrixCleaner::clean, MatrixCleaner.cc (modelSub, scaleSub)

    MatrixCleaner updates the model and the residual through a pair of boxes, one about the position it found and one about the PSF's peak (blcPsf = blc + itsPositionPeakPsf - positionOptimum). It reads the model's update from the scale image through the second box, though the scale's delta sits at the image's centre. LCBox::verify clips the two boxes separately and makeBoxesSameSize trims them to one size, which needn't keep them paired.

    So a component is exact only when the PSF peaks at nx/2, ny/2. Otherwise the model takes it at about position + centre - psf_peak, and where a box clips, the PSF is subtracted away from the position too, and a scale-0 component can miss the model altogether. This applies to any itsPositionPeakPsf off the centre, which findPSFMaxAbs looks for only in the central box, and not just to a plane the beam fit calls off-centre (outside the inner quarter).

    refim_point_linRL.ms, stokes='I', specmode='cube', nchan=2, imsize=100, cell='8.0arcsec', gain=0.17, niter=20, deconvolver='multiscale', scales=[0,3]. Channel 0's PSF peaks at (87,36), with 0.99982 at the centre, and CASA logs that peak. A component found there lands at (50,50), where the first one appears in .model. Later ones are further off: after 20 iterations CASA's channel 0 model holds negative components such as (20,61) at -0.527, and its peak stays at 0.843.

    Channel 0 CASA 6.7.6.14 casa-rs
    First component in .model (50,50) (87,36)
    Iterations in the first cycle 20 6
    Peak after that cycle 0.843 0.733

    off_centre_multiscale in tclean-psf-off-centre-cases.json differs for this reason, by 1.2 NRMS on .model. casa-rs stores a component where it subtracted it, at its position. A plane whose PSF peaks at its centre is unchanged. Hogbom and Clark place a component at its position, and MultiTermMatrixCleaner cuts its scales at the image's centre, so a one-term mtmfs placed it correctly when tried (two terms weren't verified).

    A flat-noise cube predicts its start model without dividing it by the PB¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-04
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1127)
    • Affected code: CubeMajorCycleAlgorithm::subImageStore (by reading; not instrumented)

    .model of a flat-noise image is the physical model times the PB, so a run must divide a start model by the PB before it predicts, as imager_base.runMajorCycle does through dividemodelbyweight for an MFS image. The cube path divides the sub-image's model, then, in subImageStore, setModelImage copies the raw start model over it, so the prediction uses apparent units.

    Two calls on refim_oneshiftpoint.mosaic.ms with gridder='mosaic', specmode='cube', imsize=256, cell='10.0arcsec', spw='0', phasecenter=0, gain=0.2, pblimit=0.1, the first with niter=20, the second continuing with niter=10, startmodel=<first>.model:

    Peak residual CASA 6.7.6.14 casa-rs
    The first call's last 0.0139643 0.0139643
    The second call's first 0.0196765 0.0139643
    The second call's first, the same two calls as MFS 0.0139643 0.0139643

    A run that continues from its own model should start from the residual it ended with. CASA's cube doesn't: the gap, 0.0057, is about the 1.1 Jy component times 1/PB - 1 at its pixel, where the PB is 0.9947. casa-rs divides the model, as CASA's MFS does. tools/casa-compat/tclean_summary_matrix.py --case mosaic-seed-cube --case mosaic-seed-cube-no-cut shows it, and those two cases differ for this reason.

    Automatic-mask convolution requires square pixels in CASA¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 limitation; casa-rs keeps supporting rectangular pixels (#1140)
    • Affected code: SDMaskHandler::autoMaskByMultiThreshold and Image2DConvolver::convolve

    With usemask='auto-multithresh', imsize=64, cell=['8arcsec', '16arcsec'], a residual with a 5-by-5-pixel island and a stored 72-arcsec circular beam, CASA fails when it reaches convolution: “Pixels must be square, please regrid your image so that they are.” Image2DConvolver::convolve checks the two direction increments and throws before it convolves. casa-rs keeps its existing rectangular-cell support: pruning divides the beam solid angle by the product of the two pixel increments, and smoothing evaluates the Gaussian at both angular pixel offsets. The T1 restart law tclean_automatic_restart_prefers_the_residual_beam checks the resulting mask; rectangular_pixel_smoothing_uses_both_cell_sizes checks a rotated Gaussian. The CASA failure was observed directly; there is no CASA mask to compare.

    CASA misreads big-endian integer quantity arrays¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA bug; casa-rs does not follow it (#1183).
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/quantity_record_endian_oracle.py, CASA 6.7.6.14 on a little-endian Mac.

    A quantity record containing np.array([1], dtype=">i4") returns 16777216 m in CASA; the native-endian array returns 1 m. Big-endian 16-bit arrays return 256 m and 64-bit arrays return 0 m after integer narrowing. Signed and unsigned integer arrays show the same byte-order corruption, and arithmetic uses the corrupted values. casa-rs reads the numerical array value and returns 1 m for each. It still applies CASA's signed 32-bit narrowing to that value.

    CASA drops NumPy scalars from mixed quantity-record sequences¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-19
    • Status: CASA loses input values; casa-rs rejects the record (#1170).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA data loss under the standing rule in AGENTS.md.
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/quantity_record_sequence_edges_oracle.py and its CASA 6.7.6.14 fixture.

    For {"unit": "m", "value": [1, np.uint64(2)]}, CASA returns a scalar 1 m. For [1.5, np.uint32(2), 3.5], it returns the vector [1.5, 3.5]. A NumPy boolean in a mixed sequence is dropped the same way. The Python binding leaves these scalar types out as it constructs its variant. casa-rs raises the malformed-quantity-record error for those sequences, matching CASA's existing error for a sequence containing only those scalar types. Callers see an error before computing on a shortened quantity. Unsigned NumPy arrays retain their existing integer-array behavior.

    When a sequence mixes Python booleans and numbers, CASA also drops np.timedelta64 and np.longlong values. casa-rs refuses these mixed records as well, so a timedelta's storage unit cannot turn it into a measurement.

    CASA masks quantity-record integer overflow with a wrapper error¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-19
    • Status: The CASA Python wrapper returns with a pending exception; casa-rs reports the underlying overflow (#1170).
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/quantity_record_sequence_edges_oracle.py and its CASA 6.7.6.14 fixture.

    Native Python integers outside the signed 64-bit range, such as 2**63 or [2**63] in a quantity record's value, cause CASA's Python conversion to return a result with a pending OverflowError. CPython reports a SystemError with the overflow as its cause. casa-rs reports OverflowError directly so callers can handle the range failure. In-range native integers still narrow to the same signed 32-bit value as CASA, including the singleton list, tuple, and rectangular nested forms. Mixed boolean/floating sequences also report overflow before promoting a native integer outside that range to a floating value.

    An empty floating epoch vector can terminate CASA¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, a float64 NumPy array of shape (0,) or (1, 0) inside the m0.value field of an epoch record can terminate the process during coordsys.setepoch. casa-rs returns False and preserves the current observation date (#1626).

    CASA can also misread a byte-swapped float64 epoch vector. For example, a one-element vector containing 20.0 days can be stored as a subnormal value. casa-rs converts the byte order and stores 20.0 days.

    CASA discards the values of bare complex quantity arrays¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA silently loses input values; casa-rs raises an error (#1170).
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/quantity_record_complex_array_oracle.py, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini.

    qa.quantity(np.array([1+2j])) returns a dimensionless zero in CASA; the same happens for zero-dimensional and multidimensional complex64 and complex128 arrays. A complex number cannot be converted to a real quantity without losing information, so casa-rs raises Wrong QuantumHolder to convert asQuantumDouble instead. Complex arrays used as ordinary record data retain their values and shape.

    CASA discards bare NumPy complex64 quantity values¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA silently loses input values; casa-rs raises an error (#1170).
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/quantity_bare_complex_scalar_oracle.py, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini.

    qa.quantity(np.complex64(1+2j)) returns a dimensionless zero in CASA, while an equivalent Python complex or NumPy complex128 value raises an error. CASA also treats the complex64 value as zero in other quantity operations. casa-rs preserves both parts and raises Wrong QuantumHolder to convert asQuantumDouble rather than silently discarding them.

    A non-frequency component unit corrupts CASA's spectrum¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA changes the spectrum before raising and later aborts; casa-rs refuses the unit without changing the spectrum (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/componentlist_convertfrequnit_oracle.py and tools/casa-compat/componentlist_setfreq_oracle.py, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini, with failing cases isolated and core dumps disabled.

    After adding a component with frequency 1.4GHz, cl.convertfrequnit(0, 'm') raises a RuntimeError in CASA, but it has already installed the incompatible unit. A subsequent cl.getspectrum(0) also raises, and closing the component list aborts the process. casa-rs checks the unit first, raises a catchable error and leaves the frequency, its record unit and the component list unchanged.

    cl.setfreq(0, 1, 'm') also changes the spectrum before raising; the next read raises and closing aborts. casa-rs rejects this unit before changing either the reference frequency or its record unit.

    The same failure occurs if a stored spectrum uses MHz and a later qa.define('Hz', '1m') makes the current frequency definition incompatible. casa-rs uses coherent Hz for the stored physical frequency in that case, retaining a readable record and allowing an attached table to be saved.

    Component flux conversion can relabel incompatible units or crash¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA silently changes the dimensions of a flux and crashes on an empty list; casa-rs refuses both without changing components (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/componentlist_convertfluxunit_oracle.py, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini; the empty-list call runs in an isolated process with core dumps disabled.

    A component-list table may hold a flux in Jy.km/s. Given such a component, CASA's cl.convertfluxunit(-1, 'mJy') reports success, scales the flux by unit factors and labels it mJy, despite the incompatible dimensions. It also modifies earlier components when the incompatible one is later in the list. casa-rs checks every selected source before changing any component and raises a catchable error if one does not conform. On an empty list, CASA's cl.convertfluxunit(-1, 'mJy') terminates with SIGSEGV; casa-rs raises a catchable error. A target with dimensions unlike Jy still returns False, as CASA does, before it examines the indices.

    Component removal can lose data or crash¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA loses a component on duplicate indices and crashes on an empty list; casa-rs preserves values and reports a catchable error (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/componentlist_removal_oracle.py, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini; empty-list calls run in isolated processes with core dumps disabled.

    With four components labeled 0, 1, 2, 3, CASA's cl.remove([1, 1]) leaves 0, 3; cl.recover() appends two copies of 1, so component 2 is lost. casa-rs treats repeated indices as one: it leaves 0, 2, 3 and recovers 1 once. On an empty list, CASA's cl.remove() and cl.remove([-1]) both terminate the process with SIGSEGV; casa-rs raises a catchable RuntimeError without changing the list.

    CASA accepts component labels it cannot return¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA accepts an unreadable label; casa-rs rejects the input and preserves the previous label (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA data corruption as a class; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/componentlist_setlabel_oracle.py, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini.

    After adding a component, cl.setlabel(0, b'\xff') returns True in CASA, but cl.getlabel(0) then raises UnicodeDecodeError: the previous readable label has been replaced by bytes the Python binding cannot return. casa-rs raises UnicodeDecodeError before changing the label. Valid UTF-8 byte strings and text are accepted, and both implementations stop at the first NUL byte.

    Cyclic component-list type checks crash CASA¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA crashes; casa-rs returns the placeholder result (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes as a class; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_iscomponentlist_oracle.py, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini, in an isolated process with core dumps disabled.

    Passing a list that contains itself to componentlist.iscomponentlist terminates CASA's Python process with a segmentation fault before the placeholder can return. The method otherwise logs a warning and returns False. casa-rs detects cycles while inspecting the ignored argument and returns that same placeholder value without crashing.

    The same cyclic argument also crashes CASA's componentlist.sample placeholder. tools/casa-compat/componentlist_sample_oracle.py records the isolated crash. casa-rs returns the placeholder's empty float array instead.

    componentlist.replace crashes while converting ignored arguments¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA crashes; casa-rs handles the inputs safely (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes as a class; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_replace_crashes_oracle.py and crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_componentlist_replace_crashes.json, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini, with isolated processes and core dumps disabled.

    cl.replace(0, record) with a record containing itself, a cyclic list of whichones, and scalar NaN or infinity as whichones each terminate CASA's Python process with signal 11 before the placeholder can return. The method otherwise warns and returns False. casa-rs detects record cycles and returns the placeholder result; for cyclic or non-finite index arguments it raises a catchable ValueError, as for component selection (#1176). No component is changed.

    componentlist.asciitocomponentlist('a', 'b', direction=record) likewise crashes while converting a self-referential record, even though CASA's placeholder ignores that record. tools/simulation/componentlist_ascii_oracle.py records the crash in an isolated process. casa-rs returns the placeholder's zero and warning without reading a file or changing the list.

    Invalid component direction frames use an uninitialized reference¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA uses an uninitialized frame after a parse failure; casa-rs rejects it before mutation (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and corruption as a class; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_setrefdirframe_invalid_oracle.py and crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_componentlist_setrefdirframe_invalid.json, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini.

    componentlist.setrefdirframe(0, "bad") logs that the direction frame was not changed, but the C++ code continues with newFrame uninitialized after MDirection::getType fails. On the tested CASA build, four invalid names and an invalid byte string each raise an MDirection assertion and leave the direction unchanged; an uninitialized value can behave differently in another build. casa-rs logs the parse failure and raises a predictable RuntimeError before changing the component. The error text differs from the observed CASA assertion.

    AW projection chooses frequency cells from averaged channel widths¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 divergence; casa-rs uses the actual channel frequency (#1121).
    • Affected code: MSSelection::getChanFreqList, CFBuffer::initMaps and AWVisResampler.

    CASA constructs its per-channel AW convolution-cell map from the first selected channel frequency and the average channel width, rather than each selected channel's CHAN_FREQ. For irregular channel centres this can pick a cell on the other side of a frequency boundary. In a controlled three-channel VLA fixture with centres 1349.88, 1350.01 and 1350.15 MHz, widths of 100 kHz, and cache cells at 1200 and 1500 MHz, CASA maps the second channel using 1349.98 MHz. That selects the 1200 MHz cell even though its actual 1350.01 MHz centre is closer to the 1500 MHz cell.

    A CASA control using 125 kHz widths maps that channel to the latter cell. Its start was adjusted to keep the output coordinates identical; the visibilities, weights, flags, selected channels and output grid were otherwise the same. The two CASA results differ by 0.104 normalized RMS in the PSF and 0.158 in the primary beam. On that control, casa-rs agrees with CASA on all compared products, masks and metadata, with worst normalized RMS 5.50e-7. The width metadata really differs between the controls; the comparison isolates the cell-selection effect on this natural-weighted fixture rather than asserting that channel widths are generally interchangeable. The per-row frame conversion can itself move a channel across a cell boundary, so casa-rs recovers the stored channel frequency for AW cell selection while retaining the converted frequency for UV placement. Rounding near an exact cell tie remains permitted.

    AW cube interpolation restores the native channel map¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-11
    • Status: verified CASA 6.7.6.14 bug (#1121).
    • Affected code: FTMachine::interpolateFrequencyTogrid, AWProjectFT::put and setupVBStore, and AWVisResampler::DataToGridImpl_p in synthesis/TransformMachines2.

    AWProjectFT::put calls matchChannel again after interpolating the data, weights and flags. The second call restores the map for native channels. setupVBStore supplies native reporting frequencies. The AW loop uses the length of the interpolated flag axis to index both native vectors.

    On refim_point_linRL.ms with start=1, width=1, nchan=2 and linear interpolation, a trace of CASA's running sampler showed two interpolated entries and the native map [-2, 0, 1]. Only ordinals 0 and 1 were visited. Plane 1 had zero sum weight. CASA's standard gridder populated both planes on the same input. With start=0, width=2, nchan=2, the trace instead showed four entries. The AW loop reached ordinal 3 with positive weight and used the CF selection path even though the map and frequency vectors had length 3.

    casa-rs keeps each interpolated sample's output plane and frequency together through gridding and convolution-cell selection. This is the mapping used by the shared spectral resampler.

    For converted TOPO-to-LSRK sampling, the offset and width-two comparisons use the executing standard CASA gridder's interpolated samples. Putting the captured samples on a copy of the measurement set reproduces the standard linear products. CASA AW nearest then grids those samples with the original channel count and convolution cache. In the width-two control, the channel-width metadata is set so CASA selects the cell for the actual sample frequency, accounting for its averaged-width CF lookup described above. Both comparisons retain the image grid and check the output masks and predictions.

    For BARY input and a TOPO image, the release AW gridder can segfault in AWVisResampler::DataToGridImpl_p. A trace of the three-channel refim_point_linRL.ms fixture with its test copy tagged BARY shows five interpolation frequencies before the native AW map is restored. The standard gridder visits the same five frequencies without the stale native map. Across 14 buffers it flags all but the middle sample. Materializing those samples reproduces the standard products. CASA AW nearest on those samples and the original cache agrees with casa-rs AW linear on products and masks. Both clean runs report three iterations, four major cycles and stop code 1. The saved MODEL_DATA agrees within 7.6e-7 normalized RMS.

    For Briggs and uniform weighting on the same BARY-to-TOPO axis, the control also captures the density-weighted samples at CASA's weight interpolation call. The PSF and residual passes give identical weights. The materialized data reproduce the standard products within 1.64e-4 normalized RMS. Natural-weighted CASA AW nearest on the materialized weights reproduces the weighted casa-rs AW products within 8.1e-7, with identical masks and iteration counts. The saved MODEL_DATA agrees within 7.6e-7 normalized RMS.

    The stale map also affects cubic AW cubes. On six buffers of refim_point_withline.ms using LSRK channel frequencies, the release AW gridder drops planes on aligned and half-channel-offset axes. With eight selected channels on an aligned grid it can segfault. Capturing the standard CASA gridder's cubic samples reproduces its dirty products exactly when the samples are put on a nearest-neighbor grid. CASA AW nearest on those samples and the original cache reproduces the native AW dirty products and beam fits. For the offset clean, the control cubic-interpolates the CASA AW model back to the stored channels, subtracts it before standard CASA's cubic interpolation, grids the resulting samples with CASA AW nearest and restores with CASA. The dirty and final clean products agree within 9e-7 normalized RMS, the masks and controller counts agree exactly, and MODEL_DATA agrees within 1.03e-6 normalized RMS. The independent prediction uses the exact requested 1.275 GHz start and 50 MHz spacing to apply the strict halo bounds. The 1.65 GHz stored channel equals the lower bound of the upper halo and remains excluded. CASA's image coordinate evaluates that boundary one floating-point step lower, which can admit the equality case.

    For native-LSRK cubic cubes with Briggs and uniform weighting, CASA's standard gridder applies the per-channel density weights by nearest neighbor while interpolating the data cubically. Capturing both gives identical standard dirty products when replayed with the weights in WEIGHT_SPECTRUM. CASA AW nearest applies those captured weights once, with the original cache. The clean comparison again predicts on stored channels before residual resampling. The two dirty and clean pairs agree with casa-rs on their masks, beams, controller counts and MODEL_DATA.

    For TOPO input to the same LSRK cubic cube, the measured input spacing after conversion is slightly smaller than the 50 MHz image spacing. CASA GridFT builds ten fine-grid samples for the eight image channels. The control keeps the ten samples on the materialized MeasurementSet and assigns them to the original eight-channel output axis. This reproduces all standard dirty products exactly for natural, Briggs and uniform weighting. The clean control evaluates predictions at each buffer's converted frequencies before sampling the residuals.

    For LSRK input into a tracked BARY cubic cube, CASA's BriggsCubeWeightor::getWeightUniform narrows the visibility buffer's reporting LSRK frequency divided by c to Float before forming the density coordinates. Applying the equivalent BARY frequency and scaled baseline before narrowing moved one sample from v = 619.5 to v = 619.49994. The adjacent cells gave uniform weights of 1/6 and 1/4. The AW catalog route now undoes the wavelength rescaling for this lookup. The Briggs and uniform controls each agree across 17 dirty and clean products and 48 fitted beams. The worst normalized RMS is 9.38e-7 for products and 1.11e-6 for MODEL_DATA (#1121).

    With native TOPO rows and a natural-weighted tracked TOPO cubic image, CASA converts each visibility buffer to LSRK. At 50 MHz spacing the first buffer's interpolation includes two lower halo frequencies outside the eight image planes. The later buffers interpolate directly on the eight centres. Replaying the in-image samples on their LSRK grid through CASA AW nearest with the original cache gives the same dirty and clean products as casa-rs. The 17 products and 48 beams agree, the masks and controller counts match exactly, and the largest product and MODEL_DATA normalized RMS are 9.16e-7 and 9.41e-7 (#1121).

    In the Briggs and uniform variants of this TOPO cube, the density lookup takes the nearest native weight in LSRK. CASA's unit conversion and per-hop Doppler arithmetic place plane 5 of the first buffer one Float64 step above the midpoint. The upper channel changes 55 uniform weights and the resulting PSF. Retaining the LSRK interpolation grid and applying the per-hop conversion to the native centres reproduces the CASA weights. When the image and native channel widths coincide, the spacing of the first two selected native channels determines whether CASA uses a finer grid or converts each reporting centre directly. The density grid moves the reporting reference pixel before evaluating its first spacing. Its lookup converts each LSRK channel back to the reporting frame before rounding the pixel. A tuned model-column run can select different first channels, so the clean control uses the tuned CASA iterator. The three weighted dirty and clean pairs agree across 51 products and 132 beams with exact masks and controller counts. The largest normalized RMS is 8.80e-7 for products and 8.98e-7 for MODEL_DATA (#1121).

    For TOPO input on a tracked BARY axis, CASA refuses a 50 MHz output width because the selected native width converts to 50.0024 MHz. At 50.01 MHz, all six buffers have two cubic halo samples before the eight in-band LSRK frequencies. CASA AW nearest with those in-band samples and the original cache reproduces the dirty and clean natural-weighted products. The reported BARY WCS comes from direct CASA, and the clean control predicts on the original channels before resampling the residual. All 17 products and 48 beam comparisons pass, with exact masks and controller counts. Maximum normalized RMS is 9.72e-7 for products and 9.26e-7 for MODEL_DATA (#1121). Briggs and uniform on that grid use the captured CASA density weights. All 34 weighted products and 96 beams pass, with exact masks and controller counts and maxima of 9.00e-7 product NRMS and 1.23e-6 MODEL_DATA NRMS.

    For BARY input on a tracked TOPO axis, each of the six buffers has the same native LSRK frequencies. On the eight-plane, 50.01 MHz grid, the two lower cubic halo samples are outside the image. The same-cache CASA AW control uses the eight in-band frequencies. The three weightings pass all 51 dirty and clean products and 144 beams, with exact masks and controller counts. Maximum product normalized RMS is 9.32e-7 and MODEL_DATA normalized RMS is 1.32e-6 (#1121).

    The BARY-to-tracked-BARY cubic case uses the same BARY data and 50.01 MHz width. Each buffer again places eight LSRK samples inside the image after two lower halo samples. The matching-cache AW replay agrees for natural, Briggs and uniform weighting across 51 products and 144 beams, with exact masks and controller counts. The largest normalized RMS values are 9.62e-7 for products and 1.08e-6 for MODEL_DATA (#1121).

    AW pointing can read an uninitialized frame on an ephemeris field¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14's PointingOffsets owns a default-constructed VisBufferUtil. With usepointing=False, its first field direction lookup falls through to getEphemBasedPhaseDir. That function resets the epoch on the empty frame, raising Attempt to reset non-existent frame member Epoch. Initializing only the epoch exposes a missing observatory position. A local comparison control initializes both from the epoch already passed to the call and the VLA observatory used by the normal buffer-based constructor. The remaining CASA imaging code is unchanged.

    On the three-channel VLA AW fixture with an attached test ephemeris, specmode='cubesource', interpolation='nearest' maps channels with the source-shifted frequencies from FTMachine::matchChannel. The executing AWProjectFT::setupVBStore supplies the stored 1.5 and 2 GHz frequencies to the UVW and phase calculation. casa-rs follows both rules. The dirty and clean pairs with the frame-initialized CASA control agree on all 17 products, masks and clean counts, with maximum product normalized RMS 6.16e-7 and saved MODEL_DATA normalized RMS 5.09e-7. The unscaled native comparison had PSF normalized RMS 2.24e-3.

    For linear interpolation on this fixture, CASA shifts the input channel centres and widths before regridding. A frequency-valued start and width already describe the REST grid. The 500 MHz width is below CASA's converted 500.028 MHz minimum. At 500.1 MHz, direct CASA reports the requested 1.30 GHz start and 500.1 MHz spacing. Its AW resampler restores the native channel map after interpolation, which associates in-band values with the wrong native ordinals. The unmodified AW path with the frame initialized gives PSF NRMS 0.844 against the shared resampler's output.

    The comparison takes the two in-band samples from CASA GridFT, replays them with the original AW cache and preserves the wavelength products at each epoch. The clean control predicts on the stored channels, resamples the native residual, and restores with CASA. All 17 products and 12 beams agree with exact masks and controller counts. Maximum product normalized RMS is 3.29e-7 and MODEL_DATA is 3.54e-7 (#1121).

    The same native-ordinal problem affects an eight-plane REST cube with cubic interpolation. The 20-channel VLA fixture selects channels 3–14 and asks for a 1.275 GHz start and 50.01 MHz spacing. The frame-initialized CASA AW run differs by 0.191 in PSF normalized RMS and 0.549 in residual normalized RMS. The CASA GridFT capture and wavelength-preserving same-cache replay agree on all 17 dirty and cleaned products and 48 beams. Masks and controller counts match exactly. Maximum product normalized RMS is 4.30e-5 and stored-channel MODEL_DATA is 1.82e-6 (#1121).

    For Briggs and uniform weighting of this moving-source AW cube, BriggsCubeWeightor accumulates its density through GridFT at the rotated baselines and REST wavelengths. Its lookup maps the native channel to a REST plane and uses the stored baseline and frequency for the density cell. CASA pads this one-field selection by one plane on each side. Applying the imaging AW wavelength scale to the density grid would move its samples to different cells. The native path keeps the density grid and lookup coordinates of this CASA mode. CASA's captured weights and original cache give 34 passing dirty and clean products, 96 beam checks, exact masks and controller counts, maximum product normalized RMS 9.09e-7, and MODEL_DATA below 1.81e-6 (#1121).

    Offset data-frame AW cubes reuse the native channel map¶

    With specmode='cubedata', CASA publishes the native spectral grid as Undefined and leaves frequencies unconverted. The aligned natural nearest control agrees directly. On the 20-channel TOPO fixture with spw='0:3~14', start='1.275GHz', width='50MHz', nchan=8, raw CASA AW uses native ordinals after interpolation. Its PSF normalized RMS is 0.557 for both linear and cubic. Capturing CASA GridFT's interpolated samples and replaying them through the matching AW cache preserves their frequencies and plane assignment.

    The CASA quantity conversion divides by the unit-scale ratio, so the first centre is 1274999999.9999998 Hz. Rounding it to 1275000000 Hz moves the strict prediction halo boundary onto the 1.65 GHz stored channel and incorrectly excludes that channel. The exact conversion matches CASA without expanding the halo. Together the aligned nearest and offset linear/cubic comparisons pass 51 products and 132 beams, exact masks and controller counts, maximum product normalized RMS 9.67e-7 and MODEL_DATA below 1.73e-6 (#1121).

    CASA discards zero-dimensional floating quantity-record values¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-05
    • Status: CASA silently loses input values; casa-rs preserves them (#1170).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA data corruption as a class; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/casa-compat/quantity_record_zero_dim_oracle.py, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini.

    For qa.quantity({'value': np.array(1.25, dtype='float64'), 'unit': 'm'}), CASA returns a one-element array containing 0.0 m. qa.convert likewise converts zero. CASA handles the same array correctly when passed without a record. casa-rs preserves 1.25 m and converts it to 125 cm, using the same one-element output shape. Its getunit, getvalue and scalar arithmetic still report CASA's wrong-holder error for the record.

    Unsafe component reference-direction inputs¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA crashes or corrupts component directions; casa-rs rejects the inputs before mutation (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_setrefdir_oracle.py, tools/simulation/componentlist_setrefdir_crashes_oracle.py, and their recorded CASA 6.7.6-14 fixtures. Crash cases run in isolated processes with core dumps disabled.

    On an empty list, cl.setrefdir(-1, '1rad', '0.5rad') terminates CASA with SIGSEGV, as does a cyclic quantity record. A non-finite coordinate returns True but replaces the direction with NaN. Passing None or an integer too large for the binding as the component index raises SystemError only after changing every component's direction. casa-rs rejects these inputs without changing any components, and reports the crashes as catchable errors.

    Component-list concatenation copies the wrong components¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA copies unintended rows; casa-rs copies the requested rows (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA data loss and contradictions of evident intent as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_concatenate_oracle.py and crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_componentlist_concatenate.json, CASA 6.7.6-14 on the Mac mini.

    For a source list labeled source0, source1, source2, CASA's concatenate(source, [2, 0]) appends source0, source1; the source loop checks each requested index but copies the component at the index's position in the request. Duplicated or reordered requests can therefore silently replace the components the user asked for. casa-rs appends source2, source0. A request longer than the source list can also raise an assertion after appending some wrong rows; casa-rs appends every valid requested row. CASA also appends components before raising SystemError when the index-vector conversion leaves a Python exception pending (for example which=None); casa-rs reports the error without modifying the destination.

    Unsafe component flux-polarisation conversion¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA crashes on an empty list and mutates values before reporting failed index conversion; casa-rs rejects both inputs without mutation (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_convertfluxpol_oracle.py and its CASA 6.7.6-14 fixture; the crash probe runs in an isolated process with core dumps disabled.

    cl.convertfluxpol(-1, 'Linear') on an empty list terminates CASA with SIGSEGV. With components present, passing None or an integer too large for the Python binding as the index raises SystemError after changing every component's flux representation. casa-rs raises catchable errors and leaves all component values unchanged.

    Unsafe component flux updates¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA aborts, crashes, or loses input values; casa-rs preserves the input or rejects it before mutation (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_setflux_oracle.py and its CASA 6.7.6-14 fixture. Crash cases run in isolated processes with core dumps disabled.

    For a component, cl.setflux(0, 2, error=0.2) aborts CASA because its four-element error vector is assigned a one-element array. casa-rs stores [0.2, 0, 0, 0], preserving the supplied error. On an empty list, cl.setflux(-1, 2) terminates CASA with SIGSEGV. An index of None or an integer too large for the binding causes CASA to change every component and then raise SystemError; casa-rs reports a catchable error without mutation.

    CASA accepts complex flux values and four-element errors, silently discarding the imaginary parts; casa-rs rejects nonzero imaginary parts before mutation, because the Stokes record interface cannot return them. CASA also accepts NaN and infinity, and narrows a finite 1e200 to infinity when it temporarily stores the flux in single precision. casa-rs rejects non-finite inputs and retains finite double-precision values, consistent with the existing addcomponent precision decision above.

    Component shape changes discard direction errors¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA loses direction errors and can modify a component before reporting an argument error; casa-rs preserves them (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA data corruption as a class; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_setshape_oracle.py and its CASA 6.7.6-14 fixture.

    Given a component with nonzero direction errors, setshape(0, ...) copies only its direction and replaces both errors with zero. casa-rs preserves the original direction and both errors when changing the shape. Passing optionalparms=None can also update the shape before CASA raises SystemError; casa-rs reports the error without changing the component. Passing a cyclic optionalparms list terminates CASA with SIGSEGV; casa-rs rejects the cycle before changing the component. CASA accepts NaN as a Gaussian position angle or shape error and writes NaN; casa-rs rejects a non-finite shape or error before mutation.

    Measures spectral conversions reject or truncate valid vectors¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 me.tofrequency and me.toradialvelocity return only the first result for a Doppler record whose m0 contains two velocity values. me.torestfrequency likewise drops values when either input is a vector. Both elements convert successfully in separate scalar calls. This silently loses requested scientific results; the C++ helpers contain element loops, which also show the intended vector behavior.

    With no explicit frame, me.todoppler('BETA', frequency, '1.42GHz') rejects a BARY frequency vector [1.3e9, 2.6e9] Hz with No MeasFrame direction specified for conversion of Frequency. The same scalar inputs return valid Doppler values without a frame. This algebraic conversion does not need a direction; the vector path introduces a frame-dependent conversion.

    casa-rs preserves every input element and broadcasts a scalar against a vector. Its expected values come from independent CASA scalar calls for each element, including the approaching source at 2.6 GHz. Original CASA vector outputs and failures remain in the fixture alongside those controls. Input offsets follow each helper's semantics: todoppler reads the raw frequency/velocity, while conversions from Doppler resolve its reference offset. The correction follows the standing rule to correct scientific data loss and behavior that contradicts CASA's evident intent (#1252).

    Evidence: tools/simulation/measures_spectral_oracle.py, crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_measures_spectral.json, and test_measures_spectral.py (the scalar_controls cases).

    Cyclic spectral arguments terminate the process¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 terminates with SIGSEGV for a cyclic dictionary or list passed to me.doppler(v0=...), and for a cyclic record passed to me.todoppler. The first native spectral candidate exposed the same recursion failure in shared Python value capture. casa-rs now bounds recursive record and sequence capture at 128 levels and raises ValueError naming #1252. Capture state is restored on failure, so repeated failures and a subsequent valid call work. The isolated subprocess regressions run with core files disabled.

    Measures geometry constructors lose values and mislabel units¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 baseline and uvw accept three metre quantities as Cartesian coordinates or longitude, latitude and length as spherical coordinates. Passing the length first, for example baseline('ITRF', '3m', '10deg', '20deg'), causes the binding to reorder the quantities into an invalid sequence and return a zero baseline. The angle-first form produces the intended 3 m vector. casa-rs accepts either order and refuses incompatible dimensions such as three Hz values, which CASA likewise turns into a zero vector. CASA's quantity conformance check and angle-first records supply the independent controls.

    For uvw('ITRF', '1m', '2m', '3m', off) with a valid UVW offset, CASA's constructor drops the offset, although measure(absolute, 'ITRF', off) computes and returns the correct relative value and reference origin. casa-rs gives the constructor the same behavior as the generic conversion. The fixture retains both CASA results.

    CASA's spherical earthmagnetic('ITRF','3nT','10deg','20deg') constructor stores a vector of magnitude 3e-9 with units labelled nT. casa-rs returns the magnitude 3 in nT, checked against CASA's quantity conversions and an explicit Cartesian magnetic record. CASA also emits metres as the unit for magnetic addxvalue outputs. casa-rs returns those magnetic components in nT.

    Failed measures geometry calls can expose earlier outputs¶

    After a successful touvw or expand call, CASA 6.7.6.14 can return an empty primary record alongside auxiliary coordinates and rates from an earlier call, even on a fresh tool. casa-rs returns empty records for all outputs on failure, preserving the tuple layout. For non-geometric records such as earthmagnetic, CASA's expand can temporarily change the type to UVW and return zero coordinates in metres labelled with the original type. casa-rs refuses the incompatible input.

    The inputs, raw outputs and independent controls are in tools/simulation/measures_geometry_oracle.py and crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_measures_geometry.json. These corrections follow the standing rule on data loss, unit corruption, and behavior contradicting evident intent (#1252).

    Magnetic reference changes can corrupt field strength¶

    With a frame of 2020-01-01 UTC at ALMA, CASA 6.7.6.14 converts the Cartesian field [10000, 20000, 30000] nT from B1950 to APP with a magnitude of 22289.454 nT, down from 37416.574 nT. Supplying that same Cartesian field in APP and requesting B1950 produces three NaNs. In MCEarthMagnetic the direct FK4 E-term correction can act on an unnormalized magnetic vector, and MVEarthMagnetic::adjust() leaves the norm unchanged during corrections. The same normalization omission is visible in diurnal aberration: converting this field from J2000 to ITRF increases its magnitude by 0.009120 nT. An IGRF output uses the ITRF coordinates and inherits the same error.

    Casa-rs applies the casacore direction corrections to a unit vector, then restores the input field strength. The geometry fixture retains the raw CASA magnetic outputs and separately captures CASA's direction conversion at the same angles. The normalized FK4 controls return finite vectors in both directions and preserve the input strength (#1252).

    Comet numeric queries can terminate without an epoch¶

    After loading Ganymede_55437-56293dUTC.tab with an empty observing frame, CASA 6.7.6.14 terminates with SIGSEGV in both cometdist() and cometangdiam(). The binding dereferences the frame's epoch without checking whether one was set. This also applies when done() empties the frame while retaining the active table for the comet queries.

    Casa-rs reports the missing epoch and returns the same -1 AU or -1 rad sentinel that CASA uses when the table has no sample at a requested epoch. Setting an epoch enables the queries on the retained table again. The oracle runs each missing-epoch probe in its own process with core files disabled (#1252).

    Direction shifts can use the wrong longitude quadrant¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 derives the longitude increment for me.shift from an inverse sine. This confines that increment to ±90° even when the requested offset points across either pole or into the other longitude quadrant. From longitude 0° on the equator, a 120° eastward shift returns longitude 60°. From longitude 0° and latitude 80°, a 20° northward shift returns the original direction.

    Casa-rs uses both east and meridian components to determine the longitude quadrant. For a direction starting at a pole, the local north axis is undefined and casa-rs uses CASA's longitude convention. Independent CASA direction records at longitude 120° on the equator and longitude 180° at latitude 80° yield separations of 120° and 20° from the respective starting directions. The fixture retains those controls alongside the raw shift results. This follows the standing rule for results that contradict the documented operation (#1252). CASA measures documentation

    Batch coordinate conversion can reshape ragged inputs or misread byte order¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 can pad or truncate a ragged sequence while converting it to the coordinate matrix used by toworldmany and topixelmany. NumPy arrays with non-native byte order can produce different numeric values from the same array in native byte order.

    casa-rs requires rectangular input and converts the array's byte order before interpreting its values (#1626).

    Frame display can discard the coordinate conversion context¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, showframe() fails to display many radial-velocity references even after the epoch, observatory, and direction have all been set. For a TOPO velocity of 15.123 km/s at ALMA on 2011-01-03 17:00 UTC with a J2000 direction of (10°, 20°), it reports that the frame epoch is missing. On that same tool, measure(velocity, "LSRK") returns approximately -12.87952549 km/s.

    Casa-rs evaluates the displayed LSRK velocity with the explicit frame, using the same native conversion as measure. The fixture retains CASA's raw display errors and separately records the direct conversion for all seven affected references. The missing-frame errors still apply when the required epoch or direction is absent.

    At the same observatory, CASA's display also reports a missing position for LAST and LMST epochs. Casa-rs uses the explicit position to display TDB, UT1, and TT. The fixture retains the display errors and verifies the derived time scales against CASA's direct conversions (#1252).

    Replacing a comet table can leave the previous frame direction cached¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, set an epoch and observatory, attach a COMET direction, and then call showframe() or project a radial velocity. Replacing the attached ephemeris updates direct COMET conversion immediately. The frame display and velocity projection can still use the previous table's direction until doframe(direction("COMET")) is called again. The supplied B1950 and TOPO controls at ALMA differ by more than 230 m/s in the GEO to TOPO velocity projection.

    Casa-rs refreshes the computed direction when a new table is attached. The recorded fixture retains CASA's stale results and verifies the refresh against its results after reattaching the direction (#1252).

    Frame display can report UT1 outside the Earth-orientation table¶

    With the bundled measures snapshot and an explicit UTC epoch in 2030 or 2100, CASA 6.7.6.14 displays a UT1 value despite missing Earth-orientation coverage. Casa-rs displays the raw epoch and the available TDB and TT values, and writes UT1 = unavailable. This keeps the frame visible after framenow() when the Earth-orientation table does not cover the current date. The fixture retains CASA's exact display and uses direct TDB and TT conversions as independent controls (#1252).

    Component spectrum changes can discard data¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA clears a frequency error or leaves a partial update after an error; casa-rs preserves existing data (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_setspectrum_oracle.py and its CASA 6.7.6-14 fixture; the cyclic-input probe runs in an isolated process with core dumps disabled.

    Changing a component's spectral model with setspectrum resets its existing frequency error to zero. casa-rs keeps the original frequency error and unit. A tabulation in TOPO for a component whose reference frequency is LSRK raises Cannot transform frequency only after CASA replaces the old spectrum; casa-rs validates the frame conversion before mutation. An unconvertible numeric string in a tabular vector is silently stored as zero in CASA; casa-rs rejects it without changing the component. CASA also stores NaN spectral indices, polynomial coefficients and tabular fluxes, and terminates with SIGSEGV for a cyclic tabular vector. casa-rs rejects these inputs before mutation. A failed Python conversion can likewise cause CASA to change the spectrum before raising SystemError; casa-rs preserves the original.

    Simulating more than the signed loop range¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA's component counter overflows for requests above INT_MAX; casa-rs rejects them (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: componentlist::simulate in CASA's componentlist_cmpt.cc uses an int loop counter against a long request; test_componentlist_simulate.py checks refusal without allocating billions of components.

    componentlist.simulate(2147483648) increments a signed 32-bit counter past its maximum if it reaches the requested count. Signed overflow is undefined, and the request is liable to exhaust memory first. casa-rs refuses counts outside the loop's valid range before changing the component list.

    Stokes spectrum errors can alter existing components¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA can discard an error or leave a partial spectrum update; casa-rs preserves existing data (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes, out-of-bounds reads and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_setstokesspectrum_oracle.py and casa_componentlist_setstokesspectrum.json; CASA's componentlist::setstokesspectrum reads index[0] without checking its length.

    setstokesspectrum can change a reference frequency before returning false for a power-log polynomial, or before raising SystemError for a failed vector conversion. A tabulation in TOPO for an LSRK reference can replace the spectrum before raising a frame-conversion error. CASA also accepts a length as a reference frequency and silently treats its numeric value as hertz; and an empty index is read out of bounds. casa-rs validates inputs and preserves the old component on these failures. Like setspectrum, replacing the model preserves an existing frequency error instead of resetting it to zero.

    Measure-offset inspection can corrupt integers or crash¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA truncates large integers in offset metadata and crashes on a cyclic Python record; casa-rs preserves the value or rejects the cycle (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes and data corruption as classes; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/measures_getoffset_oracle.py and its CASA 6.7.6-14 fixture; the cyclic-input probe runs in an isolated process with core dumps disabled.

    When an otherwise valid direction offset contains an extra integer value of 2**53 + 1, CASA's getoffset returns 1. casa-rs returns the supplied integer exactly. A self-referential input record terminates CASA with SIGSEGV; casa-rs raises a catchable ValueError before conversion. Array-valued and nested measure offsets remain explicitly unimplemented in casa-rs (#1053).

    Extra-measure value inspection can crash on cyclic input¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-06
    • Status: CASA crashes; casa-rs rejects the cyclic record (#1053).
    • Owner decision: Correct CASA crashes as a class; see docs/simulation/owner-decisions-20261105.md.
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/measures_addxvalue_oracle.py and its CASA 6.7.6-14 fixture; the cyclic-input probe runs in an isolated process with core dumps disabled.

    Passing a self-referential dictionary to measures.addxvalue terminates CASA with SIGSEGV while converting the input record. casa-rs raises a catchable ValueError before conversion. Excessively nested records are also refused, and offset, multivalued and unsupported measure kinds are explicitly unimplemented (#1053).

    An unused ALMA antenna can choose the standard primary beam¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-11
    • Status: casa-rs chooses the one dish class used by the selected cross-correlations for this single-MeasurementSet standard continuum case (#1274).
    • Affected code: casatools synthesis/ImagerObjects/SynthesisImagerVi2.cc (makePB) and SynthesisImager.cc (makePBImage).

    CASA 6.7.6.14 reads ANTENNA row zero from the first MeasurementSet for its analytic primary beam. It warns when diameters differ. An ALMA-labelled observation then uses the ACA beam if that first diameter is below 12 m, even when that antenna does not appear in a selected cross-correlation.

    In public M100-X220-shortened.ms, the 264 selected field-6 rows use only 12 m dishes. ANTENNA row zero describes a 7 m dish. Changing only that unused row's diameter to 12 m in a scratch copy leaves CASA's dirty PSF, residual, restored image and sum weight numerically identical. The .pb.tt0 changes by 0.7893 normalized RMS and the PB-corrected restored image by 0.6723. The number of pixels above PB 0.2 falls from 16,384 to 12,149. The 12 m reference uses the original visibility rows.

    For a single ALMA MeasurementSet with both dish classes in its ANTENNA table, standard continuum imaging chooses the analytic primary beam from the selected cross-correlations. It refuses a selection that requires both classes.

    Invalid leakage solutions become unit leakage¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: CASA defect corrected under the standing data-corruption rule (#1053)
    • Affected code: JonesGenLin::setMatByOk in code/synthesis/MeasurementComponents/Jones.cc, called by VisJones::syncJones while Simulator::corrupt applies a D table.
    • Reference: CASA 6.7.6.14; historical public C++ revision 4ebd5b1508a5d31b74e7b5f6b89313368d30b9ef reproduces the mechanism.

    With either receptor flagged, JonesGenLin::setMatByOk replaces both leakage coefficients with complex one. A missing antenna solution has the same effect. For diagonal gains, one is the neutral coefficient; for linear leakage, zero is neutral. JonesGenLin::applyRight/applyLeft therefore add the parallel hands into the cross hands. With all correlations initially 3+4j, an autocorrelation with one flagged D receptor becomes 9+12j in both cross hands. The simulator does not persist the temporary calibration flags, so those large artificial signals remain unflagged.

    casa-rs uses zero for both D coefficients when either interpolated receptor is invalid, and for an antenna with no solution. Other antennas retain their requested time interpolation. G tables retain CASA's per-receptor unit substitution. Both paths preserve the input data flags, weights and sigma. This corrects fabricated leakage without changing valid D or G solutions.

    tools/simulation/sm_apply_flags_oracle.py preserves unmodified CASA outputs and independent CASA controls with explicit zero leakage for the affected antenna. The native comparison uses those neutral controls only at times whose interpolated solution is invalid; valid times use the original valid CASA table control. C++ consumption of native invalid-solution tables is compared with the unmodified defective CASA outputs separately. The reference fixture retains both results so this exception cannot silently become a relaxed numerical tolerance.

    Natural-spline interpolation skips the first interior back-substitution¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: corrected under the standing evident-intent rule (#1053)
    • Affected code: InterpolateArray1D<Double,Float>::interpolatePtr, natural spline back-substitution, called by CTPatchedInterp::resampleInFreq.
    • Reference: installed CASA 6.7.6.14. Historical casacore source 083f0509fd76a03d6ec738c60e9dcfaf3e6c56a5 has the same mechanism: its backward loop stops at i > 1, leaving knot 1 partially eliminated.

    A scalar Tf table with four equally spaced, real amplitudes [1,2,1/2,5/4] should have zero endpoint second derivatives and interior second derivatives [-49/10,23/5] in unit-spacing coordinates. The defining equations are 4*m1+m2=-15 and m1+4*m2=27/2. At the first three half knots, the natural spline is exactly [289/160,203/160,47/80].

    CASA leaves the first interior derivative at -15/4, rather than -49/10, and evaluates the first half knot as 1.734375, rather than 1.80625. The first derivative is then discontinuous at the first interior knot, contradicting the stated natural-spline interpolation. With input DATA 3+4j and identical antenna gains, CASA writes approximately 9.02417+12.03223j instead of 9.78762+13.05016j. The complete four-channel visibility result differs by normalized RMS 0.0780.

    casa-rs completes back-substitution at every interior knot. The fixture sm_apply_frequency_casa.json.gz keeps the raw CASA output and an independent CASA control: a nearest-interpolated table explicitly containing the exact half-knot amplitudes. That control is also checked against the closed-form receptor equation; the native spline is compared against it. C++ consumption of the original native spline table is checked separately against the raw CASA result. This exception changes no numerical tolerance.

    Simulator time-spline setup uses an uninitialized interpolator¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: corrected under the standing crash/evident-intent rule (#1053)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 CTTimeInterp1 constructor and interpolate, with casacore Interpolate1D<Float,Array<Float>>.

    For G/D/T/B/Tf/Df calibration tables, sm.setapply(interp='spline') selects the spline method before supplying the interpolator's data. Interpolate1D::setMethod accesses the uninitialized sample count and knot arrays. All eight independent four-time curve/analytic probes terminate with signal 11 during setapply; corrupt is never called. The later time registration supplies data for linear and cubic methods, but has no spline branch. The same interpolator also skips the first interior back-substitution identified in the natural-spline defect above.

    casa-rs initializes a natural amplitude/continuous-phase spline from the loaded time series, completes every interior back-substitution and retains its coefficients. Exact times and queries outside the range retain the registered knot or endpoint; one knot is constant and two knots form a line. Flags combine only the adjacent time samples, without removing flagged knots, as CASA's other time interpolators do.

    sm_apply_time_casa.json.gz retains each raw CASA termination and separate nearest-CASA controls. A dense NumPy linear-system solution, independent of the native tridiagonal solver, samples the corrected amplitude/phase curves at the requested times and supplies them to CASA's nearest interpolation. The four real amplitudes [1,2,1/2,5/4] additionally give exact half-knot amplitudes [289/160,203/160,47/80]. C++ consumption of the original native knot tables uses the separate original-table nearest controls, because the CASA spline setup itself crashes. No numerical tolerance is relaxed.

    The same setup crash is confirmed through public applycal(interp=['spline']) on the shifted twelve-time G fixture in tools/casa-compat/applycal_oracle.py (#1518): the isolated CASA process terminates with signal 11. Native task application additionally checks the independent middle half-knot value 203/160 for the four-amplitude natural spline above. This is separate from the same-input CASA comparisons; it does not relax their tolerance.

    Applycal per-scan setup crashes with scans in multiple observations¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-17
    • Status: corrected under the standing crash/evident-intent rule (#1518)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 CTPatchedInterp MS constructor and scan lookup.

    A two-observation MS/calibration table with scan 1 in observation 0 and scan 2 in observation 1 terminates with signal 11 for nearestperscan, linearperscan and linearperobsperscan. Each public-task probe runs in an isolated process. The source sizes the MS scan axis using getScanNumbers(0,0), although subsequent lookup uses scans from all observations. Native groups the selected scalar headers by their actual scan numbers and gives per-scan precedence over per-observation partitioning. All three native results agree with CASA's working single-observation controls for the same samples, calibration values and scan numbers. The original native observation IDs are preserved. These controls are recorded separately from same-input comparisons.

    Nearest-field ambiguity guard is disabled by sort options¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-17
    • Status: corrected under the standing evident-intent rule (#1518)
    • Affected code: CTPatchedInterp::setFldMap, casacore Sort/genSort.

    CASA's nearest-field mapper explicitly intends to reject duplicate minimum angular separations. Its genSort options instead OR Sort::QuickSort with Sort::Ascending, whose enum value is -1. This enables every option, including NoDuplicates, so the intended duplicate-minimum check cannot observe a duplicate. With two calibration FIELD rows having identical PHASE_DIR/REFERENCE_DIR/DELAY_DIR and different gains, CASA succeeds and applies an arbitrary tied gain. The public oracle and a fresh CASA process both reproduce this behavior.

    Native restores the explicit ambiguity check, including MS fields outside the requested selection, and raises a runtime error during setup. DATA, flags and weights remain unchanged; a newly required CORRECTED_DATA is initialized from DATA, and no successful-task HISTORY is written. The oracle records this as an intentional CASA-bug correction, separately from its matching MAIN/API/state cases. No numerical tolerance changes.

    Applycal nearest-field mapping fails for legacy BPOLY¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-17
    • Status: corrected under the standing crash rule (#1518)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 BJonesPoly::loadMemCalTable and the subsequent nearest-field mapping of the converted B table.

    BPOLY conversion ignores the supplied field selector and assigns FIELD_ID=-1 to all converted rows. With gainfield=['nearest'], CASA reports that the converted table has no available calibration and the isolated public task aborts. Casa-rs applies the field-independent BPOLY solutions for this input, as it does for explicit field selectors. The public oracle compares the result against an independent CASA run with the same polynomial table and default gainfield.

    BPOLY writes nonfinite coefficients when every channel is masked¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-18
    • Status: corrected in the native polynomial solve (#31)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 BJonesPoly::selfSolveOne and the CLIC POLYANT normal equations.

    In a 12-channel window, maskcenter=12 excludes the entire spectrum. CASA attempts the singular fit and reports the resulting table in solve_tables with nonfinite coefficients. The native task returns an empty solve_tables entry when no interval can be fitted. A singular interval is omitted when later intervals still produce valid solutions. Output creation and replacement use the shared calibration table lifecycle. The pinned BPOLY oracle checks the CASA product, the native return, and retained intervals.

    BPOLY reads a second channel from a singleton selection¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-18
    • Status: corrected in the native polynomial grid (#31)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 BJonesPoly::selfSolveOne.

    The grid spacing calculation indexes the second selected frequency even when a window contains only one selected channel. With one selected channel in each of two windows and combine='spw', the tested CASA binary collapses the fit domain to the first frequency. The native grid uses the underlying window spacing, keeps both physical frequencies, and stores occupied grid points only. A degree-zero regression checks the fit against the normal equations for the two selected channels.

    Simulator short cubic time axes can alter DATA before failing¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: corrected under the standing data-loss rule (#1053, #1054)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 CTTimeInterp1::interpolate and simulator corruption writes.

    With two calibration times coincident with the first and last data times, G and scalar-T cubic application first writes the exact-time solution. The following interior query then asks for a four-time stencil and raises RuntimeError; the already changed DATA remains. The reference fixture retains both partial-write outcomes. With two or three knots and an initial interior query, CASA raises before changing DATA. Single-time, exact and outside-range queries do not need a cubic stencil.

    casa-rs preserves those mathematical success/refusal rules, but evaluates the applied term before staging any DATA writes. A short-axis interior failure therefore leaves the entire input DATA intact. The regression test checks both exact-first partial-write cases as well as the initially interior failures. This preserves the simulator's existing fail-closed write contract.

    Simulator per-scan calibration does not synchronize its scan number¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-12
    • Status: corrected under the standing crash rule (#1291)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 VisCal::syncMeta(const VisBuffer&), called by simulator corruption, and CTPatchedInterp per-scan lookup.

    The old VisBuffer synchronization copies the observation ID but never the scan number, leaving currScan at its initialized value of -1. The newer VisBuffer2 synchronization copies both. A calibration table with valid scan numbers activates per-scan lookup; the simulator supplies -1 as the segment index. All five independent active-per-scan probes terminate with signal11. Tables whose SCAN_NUMBER is -1 disable the modifier and still work.

    casa-rs uses each simulated or opened scan's actual SCAN_NUMBER and retains OBSERVATION_ID as well. Independent CASA controls explicitly partition the input table and select the corresponding one-field-per-scan DATA, using the ordinary nearest/linear/cubic method within each partition. These controls cover nearest/linear behavior, missing segments, scan-over-observation precedence and a short cubic-axis refusal. The ordinary per-observation cases run directly in CASA. No tolerance is relaxed.

    Simulator test troposphere initializes before its times exist¶

    sm.settrop(mode="test", pwv=0.01) after four 10-second integrations on an eight-antenna observation returns False in CASA 6.7.6.14, logging logic error in AtmCorr::init() - slot times not initialized. SolvableVisCal::setSimulate calls TJones::createCorruptor before filling its corruptor's slot times; the test initializer checks that flag immediately. The source's explicit deterministic ramp is consequently unreachable.

    casa-rs implements the source-defined ramp for default integration sampling: for integration i of N and antenna a of A, its excess is (float(i)/N + float(a)/A) * float(pwv) * 10. It uses the existing corrected whole wet phase delay at each channel's CHAN_FREQ, then applies the resulting unit phasors through the ordinary Tf path. Seed, beta and deltapwv do not enter this debugging formula. Positive custom simint uses CASA's uncombined iterator chunks: a new chunk starts when an integration reaches the requested interval from the chunk's first integration, with scan/field/window boundaries preserved. Saved solutions use the midpoint of each chunk's first and last integration. tools/simulation/sm_trop_test_interval_oracle.py gets those chunks from the installed CASA iterator and evaluates the source formula through CASA ATM.

    This corrects an initialization-order failure that contradicts the defined mode, under AGENTS.md's standing CASA-bug rule. Evidence: tools/simulation/sm_trop_test_oracle.py records the actual failure and evaluates the formula through independent CASA ATM; crates/casa-simulation/tests/trop_test_ramp.rs compares native Tf parameters and DATA to that reference, including repeated corruption and reopened data. The existing wet-delay and channel-frequency corrections still apply.

    Simulator on-the-fly simple noise crashes on a newly defined window¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, observe four integrations, call setnoise(mode="simplenoise", OTF=True), define another spectral window, observe it and call corrupt: the process exits with signal 11. The same control succeeds on the original window. The other five combinations of simple/manual/atmospheric noise and OTF on/off safely skip the added window; all six combinations apply their original solutions to later integrations on an existing window.

    casa-rs gates the noise term to the windows present when it was created, preserving the new window's DATA and predictions. This corrects the crash under the standing crash rule. tools/simulation/sm_later_noise_oracle.py records each control in a separate CASA process; the native regression is crates/casa-simulation/tests/later_noise.rs, with actual Metal coverage in tests/backends/offline_noise.rs.

    Multi-feed observations accumulate the previous feed rotation¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14's NewMSSimulator::observe loads a feed's fixed BEAM_OFFSET before its integration loop, then rotates that same vector in place by each integration's parallactic angle. Later integrations therefore accumulate previous rotations instead of rotating the fixed antenna-frame offset once at their current epoch. Four integrations with two/three feeds and offsets of a few 1e-4 radians differ from otherwise identical single-integration calls by up to 0.045/0.092 m in UVW. Feed zero, whose offset is zero, agrees exactly; equatorial mounts require no rotation.

    casa-rs rotates the fixed offset at the current integration's epoch. This corrects a result that depends on how the same observation is split into calls and contradicts the fixed BEAM_OFFSET metadata. The independent reference consists of actual CASA single-integration controls, matched by TIME, antennas and FEED1/FEED2.
