FEED/POINTING metadata, row order and scan numbers still match the combined observation. Evidence is captured in tools/simulation/sm_feeds_oracle.py and tests/feeds.rs (#1293).

    Reopened simulator labels appended rows with the physical window ID¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 accepts an existing MS whose DATA_DESCRIPTION rows map to spectral windows in a different order. However, sm.observe writes the requested physical SPECTRAL_WINDOW row number into MAIN.DATA_DESC_ID. With DD window IDs [1, 0], appending window 0 writes DD 0, which labels the new rows as window 1. With DD window IDs [1], appending window 1 writes DD 1, which is outside the DATA_DESCRIPTION table. NewMSSimulator::observe writes spWin to dataDescId, without resolving it through DATA_DESCRIPTION.

    casa-rs resolves the requested window to its first existing description, preserving the window's meaning and producing an in-range foreign key. It refuses append when the requested window has no description. This corrects data corruption under the standing CASA-bug rule. The raw CASA results remain in sm_data_descriptions_casa.json.gz; the native and C++ readback checks explicitly validate the corrected appended label against the input mapping. All other row cells and observation metadata still compare to CASA. Evidence: tools/simulation/sm_data_descriptions_oracle.py and crates/casa-simulation/tests/data_descriptions.rs.

    Simulator gain application ignores a reversed correlation order¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14's supplied G-table corruption produces the same four ordered products for [XX, XY, YX, YY] and [YY, YX, XY, XX], even when an aliased DATA_DESCRIPTION row selects a POLARIZATION row declaring the latter CORR_TYPE and CORR_PRODUCT. With constant input DATA 3+4i and receptor gains 2+0.1i and 3-0.2i, its first product remains the XX result (3+4i)*|2+0.1i|² instead of the declared YY result.

    casa-rs applies g[a,p] * conj(g[b,q]) according to each description's actual receptor pair, correcting mislabeled science under the standing CASA-bug rule. The oracle retains the raw CASA result; the native and C++ readback checks reverse each channel's four G products explicitly for that case. Other descriptions, unselected rows and metadata still match CASA directly. Evidence: the alias_polarization_gain case in tools/simulation/sm_data_descriptions_oracle.py and tests/data_descriptions.rs.

    Simulator image direction conversion changes the physical sky¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, a J2000 image with only its direction display conversion changed to B1950 produces different simulated DATA. The pixels, native direction coordinate, spectral coordinate and observation remain identical. DirectionCoordinate::toWorld(MDirection&) calls the world-value overload, which applies the conversion layer, then tags those converted angles with the native type_p. FTMachine::initMaps uses that mislabeled direction for its image tangent and UVW rotation.

    A display conversion changes coordinate presentation rather than physical pixel directions. casa-rs predicts from the native direction coordinate, ignoring that presentation layer. The oracle retains the raw CASA result and separately predicts an independent CASA control that clears only the display conversion. Native and C++ readback comparisons require that control's science while retaining the raw case's metadata and quality checks. Evidence: frame_conversion_b1950 in tools/simulation/sm_image_layouts_oracle.py and crates/casa-simulation/tests/image_layouts.rs.

    Simulator field selection drops the configured focus distance¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 simulator.setdata(fieldid=[0], msselect=...) changes the simulator's nField to the length of the field selection without updating its configured distance_p vector. Simulator::createSkyEquation then skips the focus distance when distance_p.nelements() > nField. The same rows selected using only msselect="FIELD_ID == 0 && ..." retain the configured focus and produce different simulated DATA. A row selection must not change the physical sky's finite distance.

    casa-rs retains the last configured nonzero field distance for image prediction, independently of the selected fields. tools/simulation/sm_field_distance_oracle.py records the raw CASA field-selection result and an independent CASA control selecting exactly the same rows through TaQL alone. The regression compares corrected DATA to that control and keeps raw quality and geometry checks. The ordinary zero-distance selection behavior is unchanged.

    AWP2 includes an MJD-zero angle for a fully flagged integration¶

    AWPLPG::init surveys every selected visibility buffer before the major cycle checks row validity. Utils::getCurrentTimeStamp returns zero when a buffer contains no unflagged cross-correlation. CASA then computes a feed angle at MJD zero and uses it when sizing the parallactic-angle lattice. This can change the kernels used to grid the unflagged data.

    On CASA 6.7.6-14, a three-integration field-1 selection from vla_wideband_2ptg_w_squint.ms gives a maximum PA of 1.11158 rad. Setting FLAG_ROW on the first integration's 351 rows changes that maximum to 1.60999 rad. The native Measures model rejects the MJD-zero conversion. casa-rs refuses this combination with a diagnostic before allocating its convolution kernels. Issue #1071.

    AWP2 can shift a PA plane when the convolution holder grows¶

    AWConvFuncHolder::appendConvFuncs saves the old holder shape in newshp. When a new PA needs more support, it grows and centres the old data but copies the incoming PA using that old shape. This can place the new plane away from the centre used by gridding and degridding.

    On the three-integration unflagged field-1 selection described above, the holder grows from 306 to 360 fine pixels. The second PA's W=0 tap peak stays at (153, 153) instead of the grid origin (180, 180). Before the repair the raw CASA and casa-rs residual images differ by 0.99856 NRMS. An independent CASA control that grows the holder before appendConvFuncs takes its working shape places the peak at (180, 180). All eight products then agree with casa-rs within 2.50e-7 NRMS, with exact masks and sum of weights. casa-rs always centres the PA planes. Issue #1071.

    AW projection can use another antenna group's pointing¶

    BaselineType::findAntennaGroups writes group means into an array indexed by the antennas' positions in the sorted selection. makeVBRow2BLGMap passes the two baseline group ordinals to PhaseGrad::ComputeFieldPointingGrad, which indexes that antenna array with them. A one-group selection works because every antenna holds the same mean. With several groups, a group's ordinal can select an antenna from another group.

    The CASA 6.7.6-14 four-corner MT-MFS test on refim_heterogeneous_pointings.ms selects even-even and odd-odd baselines at two times, with usepointing=True and pointingoffsetsigdev=[20,20]. In the first time's image the even antennas point at (924,1124), but CASA sends (924,924) to PointingOffsets::gradPerPixel for their baseline. Odd-antenna baselines receive (924,1124). Both groups' y coordinates are likewise exchanged at the second time, at x=1124. Casa-rs takes the group mean of each baseline's actual antenna pair.

    A local CASA debugger control substitutes only the correct per-baseline pointing pixels at gradPerPixel. Using the original saved CASA convolution functions, the principal dirty PSF goes from 1.33 NRMS against casa-rs to 1.08e-7. Seventeen dirty products pass, with exact masks and sums of weights. The near-zero odd Taylor weight still fails its existing pointwise comparison. When both runs use the corrected CASA dirty inputs and the saved standard-gridder PSFs, all 19 CLEAN products agree to 5.02e-7 NRMS or better, with exact masks, ten iterations and one major cycle. Issue #1070.

    AW Float tap products perturb cancelling Taylor sensitivities¶

    AWVisResampler::DataToGridImpl_p multiplies the saved weight convolution tap by a Float complex pointing phasor, then by a Float imaging weight. Both products round before the double-complex grid accumulation. PhaseGrad::ComputeFieldPointingGrad also stores the product of the rounded axis phasors as Float complex. The higher Taylor orders can expose this rounding where frequency contributions nearly cancel, and the principal sensitivity can round across a sharp PB cut.

    In CASA 6.7.6-14, the original one-group MT-MFS case on refim_heterogeneous_pointings.ms has 40 Taylor moment matrices outside PB support that fail the PSD bound. One pixel crosses the original abs(pblimit)=0.01 when compared with casa-rs. In the four-corner case with CASA's baseline group assignment corrected, the odd weight misses its existing pointwise limit by a factor of 88. Both differences are already present in the grids before the FFT.

    An independent replay using the saved CASA kernels, measured Taylor frequencies and source Float arithmetic reproduces the three captured grids to at most 5.8e-8 NRMS. A second replay retains the Taylor coefficients and widens the pointing and weighted tap products to double. Feeding only its weight grids back through CASA's FFT and product pipeline clears both failures at the original frequency and cutoff. Against this control, casa-rs agrees on all 18 dirty products for each selection, with exact masks, sums of weights and controller counts. Worst product NRMS is 2.25e-6. The odd weight passes the same pointwise and PSD checks. The native operator retains its double arithmetic. Issue #1070.

    The same four-corner selection can move one PB cutoff pixel when both imagers generate their own convolution functions. The 512-square CASA kernel transforms use SIMD FFTW, while the native comparison used the scalar build. The six imaging and six weight cells differ by at most 2.37e-7 NRMS. Their PBs at (662,1443) are 0.0099999797 and 0.0100000488 after controlling the tap rounding described above. Setting only CASA's FFTW thread count to one reproduces its original kernels exactly. Setting FFTW_NO_SIMD only for the 24 kernel transforms makes all 12 CASA kernel arrays equal to the independently generated native arrays. With those CASA kernels and the widened sensitivity control, all 18 products pass at the original cutoff, with exact masks and controller counts and worst NRMS 2.53e-6. This is a rounding difference between FFT plans. Native retains its strict cutoff and its configured FFT backend.

    AWP2's Float sensitivity FFT can flip the primary-beam cutoff¶

    AWPLPG accumulates the sensitivity grid in double precision. MosaicFT::finalizeToSky converts that grid to Float for its inverse FFT. SIImageStore::makePBFromWeight and divideResidualByWeight then apply the strict abs(pblimit) cut. CASA's single-precision FFT can move a value across that cutoff by rounding alone.

    The named CASA 6.7.6-14 test_widefield_mvc_squint_awp2 clean uses two fields of vla_wideband_2ptg_w_squint.ms, a 2000-square Stokes I image, uniform weighting, four W planes, a 15° PA step, and pblimit=-0.1. Four of 20 million cube PB mask pixels differ between the release and native runs. The uncut beams at those pixels are within 1.3e-7 of 0.1. In output channel 3 (zero-based), one cutoff pixel raises the clean residual error to 1.132e-3 NRMS. Its error away from the cutoff crossings is 1.84e-4.

    A local CASA C++ control replaces only the Float inverse sensitivity FFT with CASA's own FFT2D::c2cFFTInDouble, preserving Float image storage and the strict cutoff. It reproduces all four PB mask flips within CASA, including the residual difference in channel 3. Native keeps its existing double-precision transform and the same cutoff. It agrees with this CASA control on all 25 clean products, with exact masks and cube sum of weights, the same 20 iterations, two major cycles and stop code 1, and a maximum per-plane NRMS of 1.60e-5. The channel-3 residual agrees within 7.58e-6. Issue #1071.

    Simulator image prediction retains the first window's polarization map¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 image prediction over windows with different CORR_TYPE arrays can route later windows using the first window's map. For example, an IQUV image predicted into XX/YY followed by YY/XX retains XX/YY values in the second window's slots; circular and two/four-correlation combinations also misroute or lose image values. FTMachine::initMaps builds polMap from the initial buffer, while GridFT::get refreshes channel mappings at window changes without refreshing that polarization mapping.

    casa-rs prepares the existing forward operator for each selected correlation setup and uses only the matching setup for a scan. The mixed-correlation oracle retains the raw combined-window CASA result and independently predicts each window through a fresh CASA simulator to obtain the correct per-window map. Seeded flags, quality and untouched-window cells still use their raw references. Combined image/component controls additionally reuse the independent image-only plus component-only correction for the flagged-component duplication documented above. No CASA tolerance is relaxed.

    Zero observer radius creates spurious diurnal aberration¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-13
    • Status: CASA defect; casa-rs uses the zero-radius limit (#1295).
    • Affected code: casacore MeasMath::applyTOPOtoHADEC and MVPosition::readjust; CASA FTMachine's default reference position.

    The simulator's unset reference position is the geocentre. Its diurnal aberration factor is zero, but MVPosition::readjust(0) scales the unit correction direction by 1e-6. The resulting spurious aberration changes HADEC, horizon and terrestrial image prediction. The same C++ calculation at an ITRF radius of one micrometre has negligible physical aberration and removes the discontinuity. For the image-frame control's first baseline, C++ gives path delays of -14.597005554443669 m at exactly zero radius and -14.597027123114067 m at the one-micrometre limit. Direct C++ calls reproduce this without the Python position-record conversion.

    casa-rs retains zero aberration at zero radius. The horizon-frame oracle in tools/simulation/sm_image_layouts_oracle.py prepares and predicts images in the same limit frame; it records this correction in the fixture. Separate nonzero-delay C++ UVWMachine controls at two visibility epochs check both the rotation refresh and the retained construction-time phase centre. The end-to-end controls keep the existing 1e-3 tolerance.

    GridFT's visibility phase uses a single-precision pi constant¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-13
    • Status: CASA defect; casa-rs retains double-precision phase arithmetic (#1295).
    • Affected code: synthesis/fortran/fgridft.f, locuvw.

    locuvw declares pi double precision but initializes it with the default-real literal data pi/3.14159265358979323846/. The literal is rounded to single precision before conversion. Calling the shipped CASA 6.7.6.14 Fortran routine directly with a 100 m delay and 100 GHz frequency returns (-0.8458330035, -0.5334478021); double-precision pi gives (-0.8427074474, -0.5383717657). The returned phasor agrees with pi first rounded to float32. Large absolute delays can therefore exceed the usual DATA tolerance even when the C++ and Rust UVW and physical path delays agree.

    casa-rs keeps the double-precision constant. The image-frame DATA controls use matching observer frames and a small initial phase offset; an independent C++ projection control also checks a nonzero delay. This avoids assigning CASA's phase-constant error to the direction conversion.

    The simulator mosaic beam axis mislabels irregular frequencies¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-13
    • Status: CASA defect; casa-rs evaluates beams at the actual frequencies (#1295).
    • Affected code: synthesis/TransformMachines/HetArrayConvFunc.cc, findConvFunction.

    findUsefulChannels preserves an irregular selected frequency vector, but findConvFunction constructs the beam image's spectral coordinate with only its first frequency and first spacing. For [100, 150, 210, 300] GHz the beam planes are consequently evaluated at [100, 150, 200, 250] GHz. A four-plane image with a central source and an offset source reproduces the error even in J2000, independently of direction-frame conversion.

    casa-rs builds each beam at its actual frequency. The oracle retains CASA's raw DATA and obtains the 210 and 300 GHz DATA and CORRECTED_DATA columns from independent constant-frequency CASA predictions of the same sky. The first two channels, model column, unselected rows and quality columns retain their ordinary CASA references. These controls agree with native prediction at 5.4e-7 NRMS in the J2000 case, without changing the 1e-3 threshold. Reproduce a frequency control with sm_image_layouts_oracle.py --case frame_j2000_mosaic --constant-frequency 210000000000 --out OUT --fixture FILE.

    MVC restoration can drop a newly generated higher-term residual mask¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, seed standard, two-term MVC on field 0 of refim_oneshiftpoint.mosaic.ms with 512 pixels per axis, 10 arcsec cells, three channels and pblimit=0.1. Remove both saved Taylor residual default masks, scale the residual planes independently, and set a 50 Jy off-axis pixel in term zero. Resume with calcpsf=False, calcres=False, five iterations and a 2 Jy threshold.

    The first major cycle replaces both Taylor residual tables and their masks in PyMtmfsViaCubeSynthesisImager.cube2tt. Tracing table identity and mask support shows 62,955 valid pixels on both terms immediately before SDAlgorithmMSMFS::restore. After restoration, term one's table header has been replaced again and its default mask is absent. Term zero retains all 62,955 valid pixels. The restored term one likewise has no mask. Restoration is meant to copy each residual's mask to its image and preserve the underlying residual. The observed mask loss occurs during restoration after the Python helper has recreated tables that the minor cycle also accesses.

    casa-rs retains the freshly generated residual mask and copies its support to the corresponding restored image. The masked and unmasked initial convergence controls follow CASA's saved term-zero mask, including all valid pixels when that initial mask is absent. The additional both-unmasked probe agrees on the controller and numeric products. Only the two term-one masks differ, by 199,189 pixels. Reinstating just those masks in CASA after restoration gives all 24 products, exact masks and a maximum normalized RMS of 8.309e-6. See #944 and #1414.

    Simulator component-list copies lose the limb-darkening exponent¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 accepts a limb-darkened disk through a component record/table. ComponentList::readTable applies Optional_Parameters to its shape, but SimpleComponentFTMachine::get then copies each partition through a record. SkyCompRep::fromRecord saves optionalParameters without applying them to the shape. Prediction therefore uses the default exponent 0.05 even when the table requests 0, 1, 2.5 or 8. This contradicts the persisted component model.

    casa-rs retains the requested exponent, rounded to the shape's stored float32, and evaluates Γ(η+1) (2/r)^η Jη(r), with η=1+n/2. The simulation oracle retains raw CASA DATA. Its corrected reference combines actual CASA point-source DATA with the requested shape evaluated by CASA's bundled GSL, using independent Cartesian tangent-plane geometry. That same GSL/geometry control first agrees with the actual default-exponent CASA prediction at 3.7e-7 NRMS. Native CPU W1/W3 predictions agree with these controls below 1e-6 NRMS, including polarized spectra, selected replacement/increments and unchanged scratch/quality cells. The uniform-disk and projected-sphere analytic limits are checked separately.

    Simulation numerical diagnostics fail before returning their calculations¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-14
    • Status: CASA defects; casa-rs corrects these simulation diagnostics (#1303).
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 casatasks/private/simutil.py, sfBeam1d and sensitivity.

    sfBeam1d passes the length-one arrays returned by qa.getvalue to Python scalar operations. Under NumPy 2 this raises TypeError: only 0-dimensional arrays can be converted to Python scalars. The reference control explicitly extracts just the three scalar quantities (beam, cell and sampling); its SciPy spheroid, convolutions and spline calculation remain unchanged. Native widths agree with seven such controls to 1e-9 relatively. Units, invalid quantity messages, zero support, support below -1 and absent sampling are covered too. CASA's literal default support -1 has negative c; SciPy returns a constant angular profile there, so native preserves that box-kernel result.

    sensitivity without an antenna list leaves padnames undefined and raises UnboundLocalError, even with a valid telescope, dish diameter and antenna count. Native returns the identical-dish radiometer estimate at the requested elevation: the existing Tsys baseline sigma divided by sqrt((time/integration) * nant*(nant-1)/2 * 2) for two polarizations. It uses the existing receiver and manual/ATM models without inventing antenna positions. Nonfinite inputs and invalid Tsys are refused before the noise model can neutralize a bad gain into a plausible estimate. Independent controls use the previously validated radiometer equations, CASA receiver efficiencies and CASA's ATM tool. Image noise needs a configuration, since diameter/count alone do not define an image's UV coverage.

    With a configuration, native follows the task's synthetic-track and middle unflagged T-solution calculation through the existing simulator. Its accepted UTC, per-row noise and physical-channel atmosphere corrections still apply. Six ALMA/ACA/VLA references, including manual/atmospheric noise and the ALMA high-frequency double-sideband case, agree within the ordinary 1e-3 tolerance. An additional equivalent-unit/time/bandwidth case also agrees. With manual noise and omitted tau0, CASA instead fails inside setnoise with a negative Tsys and a Python SystemError; native requires an explicit manual opacity and reports that cause. This does not choose a new scientific default.

    doimnoise=True calls imclean without its psfmode argument, raising TypeError for missing stokes. Inserting that argument exposes the removed legacy clean function (NameError). Native carries out the declared small 128-square, natural-weighted Stokes-I Clark calculation with the existing imaging application and returns (theoretical_noise, image_rms) in Jy. The reference uses CASA tclean on exactly the same native-written noisy MS; the native image never constructs its reference. The two 128x128x1x1 Jy/beam images agree at 1.905e-7 NRMS, and their measured RMS agrees at about 1e-9 relatively. This completes the numerical diagnostic without completing the general imclean wrapper or introducing a new task.

    The raw errors, controlled calculations and reproduction commands are kept with the simulation diagnostic evidence and tools/simulation/simutil_diagnostics_oracle.py. These apply the standing policy to correct CASA failures that contradict the calculation's evident intent; ordinary successful configurations and scientific tolerances are retained.

    Public simutil ephemeris compares a quantity record with an integer¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-11
    • Status: CASA 6.7.6.14 raises with its default duration; casa-rs accepts the quantity (#1304).
    • Owner decision: Correct behavior that contradicts documented or evident intent under the standing AGENTS.md CASA deviations rule (owner #1054).
    • Evidence: tools/simulation/simutil_graphics_oracle.py, casa_simutil_graphics.json.gz and test_simutil_graphics.py.

    Without an ms, simutil.ephemeris tests self.totaltime > 0 before converting it to hours. The constructor's own default is a quantity record, {'value': 0.0, 'unit': 'h'}, so Python 3 raises TypeError: '>' not supported between instances of 'dict' and 'int'. A supplied quantity such as {'value': 40.0, 'unit': 's'} fails the same way, after drawing only part of the figure.

    casa-rs checks the quantity's value and converts a positive duration to hours before drawing its observation bar. The default zero duration draws no bar. The fixture retains CASA's raw failures separately from successful numeric-zero controls. The positive-duration regression checks the full curve and transit against the independent CASA control, and the bar's width against the analytic 40 seconds. This corrects the default call and the intended quantity input without changing the elevation calculation.

    tsdimaging crashes after gridding an empty POINTING table¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-15
    • Status: corrected in the native task (#938 part 2)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 task_tsdimaging.set_beam_size

    With the POINTING rows removed from the total-power oracle's MS, CASA grids from the FIELD direction and writes both image products. Its subsequent raster-sampling step raises ValueError: not enough values to unpack (expected 2, got 0), leaving the image without the normal weight mask. The native task completes the FIELD fallback, writes the weight-median mask, and leaves the restoring beam unset when there is no measurable raster. tsdimaging_pointing_oracle.py retains the raw exception and raw image, then calls CASA's unchanged do_weight_mask as a completion control. Native image and weight agree with that control to 2.1e-7 normalized RMS.

    tsdimaging's empty antenna selector can include cross-correlations¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-15
    • Status: the accepted simulation core selects autocorrelations (#938)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 task_tsdimaging data selection

    CASA forwards an empty antenna selection to the imager without the &&& autocorrelation restriction; a nonempty antenna receives that restriction. Later beam estimation explicitly selects autocorrelations. The native single-dish task consistently grids autocorrelations, including when the antenna parameter is empty. A cross-correlation is not a total-power spectrum. This follows the approved core's autocorrelations-only decision; interferometric imaging remains the separate tclean operator.

    An overflowing FIELD ID can select field zero¶

    CASA's MSFieldGram.yy converts decimal FIELD ID tokens using atoi. On the two-field selection fixture, stock CASA 6.7.6.14 reads the 322 rows of FIELD_ID 0 for field='4294967296', which exceeds the column's signed 32-bit range. Native tclean rejects an overflowing ID before looking it up in FIELD, so it cannot select an unrelated field. Issue #54 and the casacore FIELD grammar.

    A reversed feed interval aborts MeasurementSet selection¶

    With FEED_ID 0 and 2 present, CASA 6.7.6.14 split(feed="2~0&&") aborts the Python process. The feed grammar builds an array with negative length and the uncaught ArrayShapeError terminates the process.

    The native transform checks the range before reading MAIN. It returns a feed selector error and leaves the input and output paths untouched (#1614).

    Transform correlation arrays can disagree with their polarization metadata¶

    On a MeasurementSet with correlations XX, XY, YX and YY, CASA 6.7.6.14 split(correlation="YY,XX") and mstransform(correlation="YY,XX") write visibility and flag arrays in YY, XX order while POLARIZATION labels them XX, YY. getCorrMaps sorts the metadata indices, and the visibility iterator gets its slices from the parser's original order.

    Selecting XX, XY and YY can leave CORR_TYPE and CORR_PRODUCT undefined in an otherwise readable output. fillPolTable returns early for a partial selection of more than two products, and the caller continues writing.

    The native transform uses the original stored axis order for both metadata and arrays and writes complete metadata for every selected subset (#1614).

    Transforming an MS can omit defined FLAG_CATEGORY arrays¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 split and mstransform leave FLAG_CATEGORY undefined for an input with defined cells of shape (four correlations, eight channels, two categories). The rank check in MSTransformManager::checkFillFlagCategory compares the entire shape with the scalar 3.

    The native transform preserves defined category flags and slices their correlation and channel axes with the other MAIN arrays (#1614).

    A bare FIELD escape can silently change the selection¶

    Outside a quoted pattern or slash regex, the casacore FIELD scanner prints the backslash and consumes the next non-newline byte without adding it to the selector. On the two-field selection fixture with names Alpha.1 and Alpha2, stock CASA 6.7.6.14 reads the same 322 rows for \xAlpha.1 as it does for Alpha.1. It likewise reads the same 736 rows for \qAlpha2 as it does for Alpha2. Stock tclean proceeds to image the first selection.

    Native tclean explicitly rejects the invalid bare term before selecting any rows. This keeps an unintended prefix from changing the user's selection. Issue #54 and the casacore FIELD scanner.

    Roundoff in direction regridding can enlarge a CLEAN mask¶

    On refim_eptwochan.ms, use a standard natural-weighted 32 × 32 Stokes I MFS image with 8 arcsec cells and the binary mask box[[10pix,10pix],[21pix,21pix]]. Shift only the saved mask's first reference pixel from 16 to 16.5. The direction map is exactly (x, y) → (x + 0.5, y). Linear interpolation then gives 156 positive pixels, at x=9..21 and y=10..21.

    Stock CASA 6.7.6.14 produces 172 positive pixels. Its default decimation-10 direction mapping and linear interpolation give 16 samples from 6.39e-14 to 2.03e-12 just outside that rectangle, on analytically zero rows. SDMaskHandler::fillMask includes every positive sample. Calling CASA's same linear image regrid with decimation 1 changes the extra support and leaves 166 positives.

    Native tclean uses the exact affine direction map for a shared tangent plane. It samples signed mask values before taking positive support. Both native solvers match CASA when CASA reads the analytically aligned 156-pixel support. The direct CASA result with the rounding-only pixels leaves the Högbom products other than the mask unchanged within rounding. It changes the two-iteration MEM model by 0.315 normalized RMS and the restored image by 0.00389. The added pixels have zero analytical support and their locations follow roundoff in the coordinate grid.

    Issue #823 and the casacore coordinate-grid interpolation.

    Expanding a CLEAN mask can drop its direction and validity¶

    For a continuum mask used on a cube, CASA's SDMaskHandler::expandMask creates a temporary image with the output direction coordinates, then copies the input mask's raw pixels into it. Casacore's Lattice::copyDataTo leaves the input coordinates and default pixel mask behind.

    In the natural-weighted two-channel refim_eptwochan.ms cube, stock CASA 6.7.6.14 expands the half-pixel shifted continuum mask to 144 pixels per channel. These are exactly the unshifted rectangle. When an aligned input contains one default-invalid positive pixel, CASA keeps that pixel on both channels.

    Native tclean uses the saved direction grid and default validity before expanding support. Its shifted result contains the 156 analytically positive pixels on each channel, exact against CASA given the aligned 156-pixel support. It excludes the default-invalid pixel on both channels.

    Issue #823 and the casacore pixel-copy implementation.

    A sky polygon just outside the image can mask its first column¶

    On the natural-weighted 32 × 32 Stokes I MFS refim_eptwochan.ms fixture, a saved J2000 polygon projects wholly left of the image. Its vertices in pixel coordinates are (-0.6, 2.2), (-0.2, 2.5), (-0.2, 9.7), (-0.5, 10.2). No pixel centre lies inside. Stock CASA 6.7.6.14 nevertheless fills column zero at rows 3 through 10 and performs five Högbom iterations.

    LCPolygon::truncateEnd converts its fractional upper coordinate to an integer toward zero. When the upper crossing lies between −1 and 0, it becomes 0 and admits the first column. The near-integer branch also converts a crossing near −1 toward zero after adding 0.1. Native tclean floors both upper-bound branches before clipping. A wholly outside polygon therefore produces an empty mask and stops with code 7, as stock CASA does when the polygon is farther outside. Ordinary off-grid, partially clipped and pixel-boundary polygons retain CASA's exact support.

    The same clipping defect affects fractional CRTF pixel boxes. For box[[-0.6pix,5.2pix],[-0.2pix,10.7pix]] and one whose upper x bound is −0.999999, stock CASA paints column zero at rows 6 through 10. Both native masks are empty and return stop code 7. The ordinary fractional, near-integer and partly clipped controls retain CASA's support.

    Issue #823 and casacore's upper crossing bound.

    PCO pixel to world returns NaN on the central meridian¶

    • Date noted 2026-11-15
    • Status corrected in casa-rs (#1101, #1494)
    • Affected code WCSLIB 8.2.2 pcox2s in CASA 6.7.6.14

    Run tclean on refim_twochan.ms with projection="PCO", a 100 × 100 image, 8″ cells and phase centre J2000 19:59:28.500 +40.43.37.50. The dirty peak is at pixel [50, 53]. CASA's coordsys.toworld returns NaN in both sky coordinates there. Pixel [50, 50] and the off-meridian [50.001, 53] have finite coordinates. CASA's coordsys.topixel places the known sky direction, the reference longitude at the reference declination plus 24″, at [50, 53]. CASA shows this behaviour when reading either a CASA or a casa-rs image with the same PCO record.

    At zero intermediate x, WCSLIB initializes both residuals to zero and divides one by their difference in the first weighted interval step. The native inverse solves for the same sky with a finite latitude. On the central meridian the projected latitude is the native latitude. The frozen PCO checks verify both offset signs using CASA's finite world-to-pixel direction and compare ordinary pixels in both directions. The projection and parameters remain CASA-readable and image products agree at the original tolerance. WCSLIB 8.2.2 source

    Mosaic and W projection drop negative Taylor moments with empty cross-hands¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, MosaicFT::getImage and WProjectFT::getImage check max(weights) == 0.0 to decide whether all gridded weights are zero. The imaging weights start nonnegative, but MultiTermFTNew multiplies by a signed spectral offset for odd Taylor terms. Negative parallel-hand sums and zero sums on unobserved cross-hands have a maximum of zero. This skips the FFT and drops the whole dirty or PSF moment. The signed sum of weights still reaches disk, as does the separate sensitivity image in a mosaic.

    For mtmfs_polarized.mosaic_IQUV (refim_mawproject.ms, SPW 1, two terms), stock CASA writes a zero .psf.tt1 and .residual.tt1 despite a negative .sumwt.tt1. The same request in IV retains both terms and gives a central PSF first moment of about −8.01e−8. The narrowband principal spectral coefficient is poorly constrained. The same behavior also occurs across SPWs 0, 1 and 2, where the IV run has usable frequency coverage and the central PSF first moment is about −0.0196. A reference frequency of 1.4 GHz makes the total first moment positive on those three SPWs and the stock IQUV run retains it.

    The same SPW 1 request with gridder='wproject', wprojplanes=16 drops the odd PSF and residual moments in IQUV and retains them in IV. The native I and V Taylor products and controller agree with that stock IV run. With four observed correlations, the W-projection IQUV control on refim_point_linRL.ms agrees directly with stock CASA.

    casa-rs keeps nonzero signed moments for both gridders, so requesting additional empty Stokes planes cannot erase valid data. Its I and V products agree with stock CASA's IV controls, and it leaves Q and U data and models zero. The shared PSFs, sensitivities and sum-of-weights tables still cover all four Stokes planes. Imaging issue #1112

    Polarized AW projection publishes the first correlation sensitivity¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 computes the mean of the complex weight transforms across polarizations in AWProjectWBFT::makeWBSensitivityImage. The accumulator is a separate matrix. It never replaces the first plane of the input lattice, so the subsequent loop takes the magnitude of the original first plane and copies it into every output polarization.

    With refim_mawproject.ms (RR and LL), gridder='awproject', wbawp=True, conjbeams=True, psterm=False, both PA steps at 360, imsize=512, cell='10arcsec', two Taylor terms, reffreq='1.4GHz', stokes='IQUV' and pblimit=0.1, this changes the common weight image by about 4.4% NRMS and changes the valid PB support. The request uses the phase centre and field selection of test_widefield_wbaproj_mtmfs. A native probe that takes only the first correlation reproduces the stock products and controller with the same cache. The same first-correlation behavior is also verified on the four-correlation refim_point_linRL.ms fixture.

    The native AW path combines the parallel-hand transforms for the common IQUV sensitivity. Its Stokes I images, normalized weights and masks agree with a separate stock Stokes I run, which combines both parallel hands on one grid plane. The first-correlation rule for the published Taylor sum of weights follows CASA. It makes the IQUV sum of weights half the Stokes I sum for the equal-weight RR and LL case. Compare the shape of higher weight terms after dividing by their published sums. Imaging issue #1112

    Linear mosaicking counts weights for masked restored pixels¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, LinearMosaic::addOnToImage applies the restored image's mask to the weighted signal and uses the primary beam's mask for the coverage. With a constant 1.5 restored image and a primary beam of 0.5, masking only the restored pixel gives zero signal and a coverage weight of 0.25. In an overlap, that weight dilutes the flux from a valid pointing. With a 6 × 5 pointing aligned in a 10 × 6 output, CASA's copy path can carry both values through a pixel masked in both inputs. At the masked pixel in the same constant example it wrote 3 and 0.25.

    casa-rs uses both masks for both contributions. A pixel masked in either input contributes neither signal nor coverage, and valid pointings still contribute there. Imaging issue #1515

    bandpass suppresses its runtime exceptions¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-16
    • CASA version: 6.7.6.14
    • Affected code: casatasks/private/task_bandpass.py, bandpass

    Calling bandpass with field='missing-field' on an otherwise valid MS logs the C++ selection exception but returns a dictionary with an empty solve_tables instead of raising it. The task's finally block returns mycb.returndict(), suppressing any exception from selection, solving or writing. The existing caltable survives this selection failure. casa-rs preserves that product and raises its existing execution error so a failed calibration is visible to a calling script. tools/casa-compat/calibration_lifecycle_oracle.py exercises both outcomes.

    gaincal can append rows without storing their gain parameters¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-16
    • CASA version: 6.7.6.14
    • Affected code: SolvableVisCal::storeNCT, TableCopy::copyRows

    Create a G table with ten rows, remove CPARAM with the table tool, then run gaincal(append=True) on the same input. CASA succeeds and leaves twenty rows without a CPARAM column: copying common columns silently discards the new gain solutions. casa-rs refuses this incompatible append schema and preserves the previous product. Losing scientific values is not a useful compatibility behavior. calibration_lifecycle_oracle.py reproduces both outcomes; output_lifecycle checks the native refusal and preservation.

    Appending a first gain or B table tries to open nonexistent spectral metadata¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-16
    • CASA version: 6.7.6.14
    • Affected code: SolvableVisCal::discernAndSetSolnFrequencies

    With append=True and an absent caltable, the frequency precheck opens caltable/SPECTRAL_WINDOW unconditionally and fails. This precedes storeNCT, whose append logic checks whether the destination is readable and otherwise writes a new table. casa-rs follows that latter intent: append creates a first table, then requires compatible metadata for later appends. A solve without successful solutions still creates nothing. calibration_lifecycle_oracle.py records CASA's failure (suppressed by the bandpass wrapper) and native creation on otherwise identical valid inputs.

    table.summary(recurse=True) use-after-free and statistics NaN ordering¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, calling tb.summary(recurse=True) on a table with a Table: subtable keyword passes a destroyed String buffer when recursing into subtable paths in TableProxy::printValueHolder, aborting with a UnicodeDecodeError or corrupted path. Calling tb.statistics on floating columns containing NaN also violates strict weak ordering in casacore's GenSort::kthLargest, making median, medabsdevmed, quartile, min, and max depend arbitrarily on input array index positions.

    casa-rs safely canonicalizes and deduplicates Table: keyword subtable paths in tb.summary(recurse=True) without invalid memory access, and in tb.statistics ignores NaN for min/max while returning NaN deterministically for moments and order statistics on NaN inputs. Table tool issue #1028

    Calibration append accepts NumPy booleans in validation but rejects them in C++¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-17
    • CASA version: 6.7.6.14

    gaincal(..., append=numpy.bool_(True)) passes the generated cBool validator, which explicitly accepts bool and numpy.bool_. The unchanged scalar reaches calibrater.setsolve, whose SWIG bool conversion raises TypeError instead of appending. The existing calibration table is unchanged. The same call with builtin True succeeds.

    casa-rs converts the valid NumPy scalar to a native boolean and appends the same solutions as builtin True. This follows the validator's declared domain. tools/casa-compat/calibration_lifecycle_oracle.py reproduces the CASA error and compares the two native append products. Invalid boolean arguments retain the native exception policy documented in docs/python/api.md.

    Fitting through the legacy calibrater can crash without CORRECTED_DATA¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, use calibrater.setvi(old=True), open a MeasurementSet without CORRECTED_DATA with addcorr=False, and select one populated field. Calling calibrater.modelfit([], niter=0) exits with a segmentation fault. The uvmodelfit task opens with addcorr=True and initializes the column.

    The native fitter reports an error when the corrected samples are missing. The task initializes the column before fitting, as CASA does (#1520).

    Fluxscale negative and underdetermined spectral-fit orders¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 fluxscale allocates coefficient arrays with fitorder+1 entries before checking a negative order. With three valid spectral windows, fitorder=-1 logs "Using fitorder=1", then writes outside the zero-width array and segfaults (isolated Python oracle exit 139). casa-rs implements the advertised fallback to order 1 and allocates the corresponding two entries.

    With two valid windows and fitorder>1, SolvableVisJones::fluxscale advertises order 1 but leaves myfitorder at its initializer, zero. It fits a constant instead. With three windows and degree 3 or 5 it returns an underdetermined solution, including negative covariance diagonals; one probe returned a cubic coefficient of 1560 and a covariance diagonal of -2.95e20. casa-rs reduces the fitted degree to the number of valid windows minus one, warns, and pads the requested coefficient/error vectors with zeros. Valid, determined fits retain the requested degree and CASA's covariance convention. The existing singular order-one behavior is retained.

    For exactly determined fits, casacore LSQFit::solve subtracts accumulated moments (sum(w*y*y) - x*b + x*(N*x-b)) and divides by at least one when estimating residual scatter. Roundoff can become a positive uncertainty although the data determine every coefficient exactly. A five-window degree-4 probe returned coefficient 24697.456405169 and uncertainty 0.0038844, and a flux-density error of 6.1e-7. casa-rs retains zero residual uncertainty when there are no residual degrees of freedom, extending its existing two-window linear-fit convention. The oracle separately bounds these CASA artifacts to 1e-3 of the corresponding coefficient/flux scale and checks native zeros; determined coefficients, covariance, and overdetermined uncertainties retain their direct numerical comparisons. Existing stricter order-one checks remain.

    Source: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc, SolvableVisJones::fluxscale, coefficient allocation and myfitorder branches. Reproduction: tools/casa-compat/fluxscale_oracle.py builds the three-window input; the negative-order crash must be run in a separate Python process. Regression: casa-calibration's spectral-fit tests and captured CASA gain data.

    Explicit gencal wildcards can index outside the calibration array¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, gencal(vis, caltable, 'amp', antenna='*', parameter=2.0) passes -1 as an antenna index and aborts in ArrayBase. With spw='*' CASA similarly passes a negative window index and segfaults. A negated antenna clause such as A1;!A3 can pass -3 and abort after processing A1.

    For the specified gain, phase, delay and opacity types, casa-rs treats each whole-axis * as that axis's empty selector, which broadcasts the value. It refuses a negative antenna index before publishing the table, preserving any existing product. The paired calibration_gencal_specify_oracle.py compares the wildcard results to CASA's empty selectors and checks safe rejection of the negated clause (#32).

    The legacy antposvla path also aborts with an empty antenna selector when logging an element of the empty antenna array. Casa-rs safely rotates the first offset triple and broadcasts it to all antennas. Explicit antenna selections retain CASA's repeated in-place rotations when the parameter vector cycles. The paired tools/casa-compat/calibration_gencal_antpos_oracle.py checks both forms (#32).

    Opacity priors can index a nonexistent second receptor¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 constructs a scalar solution for gencal(caltype='opac'). Selecting the second receptor with pol='L' or both with pol='X,Y' terminates the Python process in the calibration array slicer. Casa-rs raises a task error and preserves an existing table. The empty selector, R and X select the scalar value.

    Source: synthesis/MeasurementComponents/SolvableVisCal.cc, SolvableVisCal::specify. The paired tools/casa-compat/calibration_gencal_opacity_oracle.py runs the CASA probes in isolated processes and checks native table preservation (#32).

    Fluxscale history can contain uninitialized object identifiers¶

    MSHistoryHandler::addMessage writes every required HISTORY column except OBJECT_ID. Repeated CASA 6.7.6.14 fluxscale calls return arbitrary integers there (one run produced -1045601744 and 32549); freshly allocated storage can instead read as zero. casa-rs initializes this required field to zero. The oracle compares all other history content, checks the native zero, and treats the current timestamp and implementation version as process-specific metadata.

    CASA 6.7.6.14 also accepts refspwmap=[] at the Python boundary but indexes element zero unconditionally in SolvableVisJones::fluxscale, causing a segfault. Native empty mapping means identity, as refspwmap=[-1] does.

    Fluxscale listfile can overwrite scientific table files¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 opens listfile with ofstream truncation without checking whether it names a file inside the input MS, input caltable or output product. Such a path can overwrite a scientific table file. Stream failures are also unchecked, so the task can report success without producing the listing. casa-rs refuses overlapping listing/product paths before mutation and reports listing write errors. This preserves data and implements the requested file output rather than silently losing it. Source: SolvableVisJones::fluxscale, the oListFile stream blocks.

    tablerow.__getitem__ process abort in CASA 6.7.6.14¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, indexing a tablerow accessor via r[rownr] (including valid and out-of-range row indices, e.g. r[0] or r[10]) terminates Python with segmentation fault (signal 11, exit code 139) inside the SWIG casac::tablerow wrapper (__getitem__), even though r.get(rownr) and len(r) succeed.

    casa-rs implements tablerow.__getitem__ safely under the GIL-detached Rust table accessor, supporting negative indices, slices, and sequences of row indices, and raising IndexError cleanly on out-of-range indices. Table tool issue #1028

    table.fromASDM can report success after discarding XML values¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, call table().fromASDM("out.table", "Scan.xml") with <ScanTable><row><scanNumber>12</scanNumber></row></ScanTable>. The table contains one row but its scanNumber is 0. The legacy SAX code only enables its value callback for fields listed in a working directory file named taglist.txt; even with the tag in that file, this build did not store the value. With a nonexistent XML file CASA returns True and creates no table.

    casa-rs uses the same ordered column descriptions and converts the XML values directly. It reports invalid or unreadable input before replacing the destination. The ASDM XML converter reads one table at a time; it does not import ASDM datasets or their binary data. See #1028.

    FITS TNULL cards can disappear from table.fromfits output¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 preserved the integer -32768 in a FITS 1J column but did not add a NULL column keyword for its TNULL2 = -32768 card. FITSTabular::nullsFromHDU looks up each indexed card with a zero-based column index. casa-rs preserves both the unscaled integer cell and the matching null marker so readers can interpret it. See #1028.

    Callibrary joint scan maps mark other observations unavailable¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-17
    • Status: corrected under the standing evident-intent rule (#1518)
    • Affected code: CASA 6.7.6.14 CLPatchPanel MS constructor, missing-scan catch.

    With scan 1 in observation 0 and scan 2 in observation 1 in both the MS and G table, obsmap='self' scanmap='self' skips all calibration. The constructor tries each requested scan within each calibration observation. When a scan is absent in the current observation, it calls recordBadMSIndices(reqMSobs, theseMSscan, ...), marking that scan bad in every requested observation, including the observation where the scan exists. With an existing corrected column, CASA leaves its values and weights unchanged; ordinary calflag adds no flags. This contradicts the explicitly independent observation/scan maps.

    casa-rs checks availability within the actual joint mapped context and calibrates its valid buffers. The public task oracle retains the raw CASA result and compares native output to an independent CASA control with two explicit library slices, obs='0' scan='1' and obs='1' scan='2', retaining both self maps and the identical input MS/calibration table. That control matches native corrected data, flags and weight/sigma columns. Missing joint contexts still use the ordinary unavailable-calibration rules. No tolerance is relaxed, and this correction is separate from same-input task matches.

    Gaincal can crash while opening a tracked virtual image¶

    On Linux, CASA 6.7.6.14 saved a virtual GridFT image with tclean(phasecenter='TRACKFIELD', savemodel='virtual') on an MS with an attached Uranus ephemeris. Calling gaincal on that MS terminated Python with signal 11. CASA wrote a nonconstant prediction to MODEL_DATA when savemodel='modelcolumn' used the same image settings and visibilities.

    casa-rs reads the saved source, tracking epoch and image direction and evaluates the tracked prediction in the visibility buffer. The gaincal_oracle.py --case ephemeris fixture compares native gains and downstream application from the virtual image to CASA gains and application from its scratch prediction. It also covers a tracked mosaic, an external ephemeris table and moving-field parallactic angles. See #30.

    Empty or ragged nested sequences in quantity records crash or corrupt values¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-18
    • Status: CASA crashes or truncates/zero-pads data; casa-rs refuses the inputs (#1170).
    • Affected code: casatools Python-to-Variant / QuantumHolder record conversion.

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, qa.quantity({'value': [[]], 'unit': 'm'}) terminates Python with a segmentation fault (exit code 139). For ragged nested floating-point lists, CASA silently discards trailing elements in longer rows ([[1.0, 2.0], [3.0]] becomes array([[1.], [3.]])) or zero-pads shorter rows ([[1.0], [2.0, 3.0]] becomes array([[1., 0.], [2., 3.]])).

    casa-rs checks nesting depth and row shapes in crates/casars-python/src/convert.rs and rejects empty or ragged nested Python sequences with CASA's catchable Illegal Quantum record in QuantumHolder::fromRecord RuntimeError instead of crashing or corrupting values (#1252, #1170).

    Component record imports announce Stokes but store an unknown polarization¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 cl.add and cl.fromrecord accept a flux record with four values and no polarisation or error field. FluxRep::fromRecord logs "Using the default of Stokes", then overwrites that default with its local UNKNOWN_POLARISATION enumerator. getcomponent consequently reports polarisation='Unknown'. A real-valued error vector instead makes the import fail because the local enumerator is still unknown.

    casa-rs retains the announced Stokes basis for accepted records, preserving all four flux values. It preserves CASA's rejection of the real error-vector form. This avoids storing an unusable polarization label or losing the component's numerical values. The correction follows the standing owner rule in AGENTS.md for behavior that contradicts CASA's evident intent; there is no new owner exclusion.

    Evidence: tools/simulation/componentlist_record_spectra_oracle.py, casa_componentlist_record_spectra.json (missing_pol_no_error), and test_componentlist_records.py (#1252).

    Gaussian component record imports abort on invalid axes or position angles¶

    CASA 6.7.6-14 cl.add(record) terminates the process with SIGABRT when a Gaussian record has zero or negative axes, a minor axis longer than its major axis, or a position angle outside ±360 degrees. GaussianShape::fromRecord constructs the shape before rejecting these values, and its constructor's AlwaysAssert(ok()) escapes the tool boundary.

    casa-rs rejects the invalid component with a catchable RuntimeError before appending it. Valid components retain CASA's geometry and position-angle normalization. This follows the standing rule to correct CASA crashes; callers retain their existing list and Python session. The isolated CASA subprocess receipts are in crates/casars-python/python/tests/data/casa_componentlist_record_validation.json, regenerated by tools/simulation/componentlist_record_validation_oracle.py.

    Duplicated imstat axes crash CASA¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-18
    • Status: CASA aborts; casa-rs refuses the request (#977).
    • Affected code: casatools image statistics, ImageStatsCalculator::statistics.

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, imstat(imagename, axes=[0, 0]) aborts the Python process (SIGABRT, exit 134) inside the statistics calculation; the same happens for any duplicated axis. An out-of-range or negative axis is refused with the catchable Invalid cursor axes: [4]. instead, so the crash on duplicates is an uncontrolled path, not a policy.

    casa-rs refuses a duplicated axis with Invalid cursor axes: [0, 0]: an axis is named twice; CASA 6.7.6.14 aborts on it (crates/casa-images/src/analysis.rs, parse_axes), keeping every other axes form CASA accepts, including the minimum-forms -1 for all axes (#977).

    Replacing one Multi-MS model can remove another partition's Fourier grid¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, split an MS with partition(separationaxis='baseline', numsubms=2), then write an image with ft(mms, field='0', model=...). The two physical SOURCE records can name the same Fourier image. Replacing the model on the first partition removes that image. A subsequent uvsub on the other partition fails to open the image its record names.

    casa-rs gives independent SOURCE or MAIN records their own Fourier grids. Replacing one record deletes the old grid that record owned. The other partition's recipe still opens in CASA and casa-rs. The standalone calibration_ft_oracle.py fixture exercises the replacement sequence.

    TaQL derived geometry treats an ephemeris field offset as a direction¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 msmetadata.phasecenter(field, epoch) follows the attached body and applies the field offset. The TaQL mscal.hadec1() and mscal.azel1() functions use the stored PHASE_DIR directly for the same field. For the Uranus fixture, mscal.hadec1() reports a declination of 0.00190 rad while the attached phase centre is near 0.20677 rad.

    The calibration engine and diagnostics read the attached direction at the requested epoch. The calibration_ephemeris_geometry_oracle.py fixture obtains the per-epoch phase centres from CASA and evaluates CASA geometry on fixed snapshots of those directions (#989).

    Rejected Tsys weight initialization can leave empty spectra¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 can add WEIGHT_SPECTRUM and SIGMA_SPECTRUM to a MeasurementSet before rejecting initweights(wtmode='tsys', dowtsp=True) with an invalid gainfield or interpolation setting. On that failure the new columns have undefined cells and the original weights are unchanged.

    casa-rs validates the setup before it creates either column, so a rejected call leaves the original spectrum schema available for retry. The paired calibration_initweights_tsys_oracle.py cases cover both setup errors and the unchanged weight, sigma and flag values (#1524).

    immath(mode="pola") crashes with UnboundLocalError on unitless polithresh¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-18
    • Status: CASA crashes with an uncaught Python UnboundLocalError; casa-rs accepts unitless thresholds in image brightness units (#977).
    • Affected code: casatasks/private/task_immath.py, _immath_new_pola.

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, when mode="pola" and polithresh is given without a unit (e.g. polithresh="0.1"), _immath_new_pola skips creating the temporary linear-polarization image lpol because if (myqa.getunit(polithresh) != ""): wraps both unit conversion and mypo.linpolint(..., outfile=lpol), yet the later if (polithresh): block unconditionally evaluates lpol and raises UnboundLocalError: cannot access local variable 'lpol'.

    casa-rs parses polithresh as an optional-unit quantity (crates/casa-images/src/analysis/immath/pol.rs), treats a unitless polithresh as already expressed in the input image's brightness unit, and thresholds linear polarized intensity normally (#977).

    immath polarization debiasing ignores non-conforming sigma units¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-18
    • Status: CASA silently corrupts/masks output; casa-rs rejects non-conforming or missing units when the image carries a brightness unit (#977).
    • Affected code: casatools/casacore quantity conversion in task_immath.py, _immath_new_poli.

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, _immath_new_poli converts a positive sigma via myqa.convert(qsigma, iunit) and reads .getvalue() without verifying that sigma's unit conforms to the input image's bunit (for example passing sigma="1K" or unitless sigma="10" on a "Jy/beam" image). On mismatch qa.convert leaves the value/unit invalid so downstream imagepol debiasing silently over-thresholds and masks the entire output plane.

    casa-rs validates unit conformance via get_conformant_value when sigma > 0 (crates/casa-images/src/analysis/immath/pol.rs) and refuses non-conforming or unitless sigma against non-empty image bunit with a clear error (#977).

    Median fringe smoothing wraps delay values as phase¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, smoothcal(smoothtype='median') on a Fringe Jones calibration table applies phase wrapping to every FPARAM component. The condition ipar == 0 || 4 in FringeJones::smooth is always true. A valid 8 ns delay therefore became about 1.717 ns in a window with only that sample. The same branch also skips the median filter for delays and dispersive delays.

    casa-rs wraps only the two phase parameters and applies the numerical median to the delay, rate, and dispersive delay parameters. The use of fringe rates for predicting phase wraps and the phase outputs follow CASA (#1525).

    Re-referencing a delay table crashes CASA¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, rerefant(refantmode='strict', refant='A0') terminates the process when the input is a K Jones delay table. SolvableVisJones::applyRefAnt reads CPARAM before dispatching on the table type. Delay tables use FPARAM, and only Fringe overrides the reference calculation for real-valued parameters.

    casa-rs raises a task error for unsupported table types before publishing a result. The paired oracle runs this CASA check in a child process (#1525).

    statwt creates spectra then fails on an explicitly selected subset¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 initializes a required WEIGHT_SPECTRUM or SIGMA_SPECTRUM column for the original MS when chanbin requests channel bins. When the selected table predates the new column, CASA then raises a RuntimeError and asks the caller to rerun the selection. The first call writes the initial spectrum across the MS and leaves the original flags and scalar columns intact.

    casa-rs initializes the physical MAIN tables and subsequently applies statistical weights to the requested rows in one call. Unselected rows retain the spectrum obtained by expanding their original scalar values. The paired fixture compares this result with CASA's documented two-call sequence and checks unselected rows (#1524).

    Column statistics change when the same NaN moves between rows¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 derives table.statistics and calstat quantiles from an ordering that includes NaN. The rows [0, 3, 1, NaN, 4, 8, 9] gave a finite interquartile range of 7 and a NaN median. Moving that NaN to the start gave an interquartile range of 8, a median of 3 and NaN for both bounds. The sum and variance were NaN in each case.

    casa-rs returns deterministic bounds from the non-NaN samples, counts all samples and returns NaN for the moments and quantiles when a NaN makes those quantities undefined. This preserves the existing native table tool behavior in the public calibration task (#1526).

    Accumulating a bandpass updates a fraction of the Jones cells¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14's calibrater.accumulate is available with setvi(old=True). In the paired eight-channel B Jones fixture it changed 20 of 160 valid complex cells. At each of two solution times, only the first five channels of antenna 0 received the Jones product. The other valid factors remained as they were.

    casa-rs multiplies the per-receptor, per-channel factors for every antenna. The analytic self-accumulation check squares all 160 valid factors and checks flags separately (#1525).

    Accumulation depends on the physical order of overlapping field times¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, the old accumulation method passes the incremental table's time column to CTTimeInterp1. The interpolator uses binary search on those times. Physically grouping rows by field can leave that column out of chronological order when time ranges overlap.

    CASA changed valid gains when the paired fixture was sorted by time. casa-rs orders its time series before interpolation and matches the time-ordered CASA control. It preserves the prior numerical payload at flagged cells. In a selected gain-field comparison, CASA replaced one flagged payload with complex unity. The flags and valid values matched (#1525).

    Selecting a field in simultaneous calibration rows modifies other fields¶

    The old CASA 6.7.6.14 accumulation method groups rows by spectral window and time. It uses the first row's FIELD_ID to decide whether to modify the entire group. In a fixture that divides antenna solutions at the same timestamp between two fields, selecting FLUX also changes PHASE rows. Selecting PHASE skips all the rows.

    casa-rs applies the correction within each selected field. The paired fixture checks the product on every eligible row and checks the unselected rows byte for byte (#1525).

    Accumulation repairs a legacy input even when a separate output is requested¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14's old accumulation path upgrades a pre-4.1 cumulative table when it opens the source. The paired input lost its original schema: CASA added OBSERVATION_ID and OBSERVATION before publishing the requested separate product.

    casa-rs applies the same legacy upgrade inside the output snapshot. The result matches the upgraded CASA product and a separately named original retains its legacy schema (#1525).

    Accumulation can terminate during contextual time interpolation¶

    With the old CASA 6.7.6.14 visibility iterator, accumulation terminated the process on the test fixtures for spline and linearperscan. linearperobs completed when there was one observation and terminated on the two-observation probe.

    casa-rs uses the calibration application's time interpolator with the cumulative rows' scan and observation metadata. In local comparisons, the per-scan output matched two independent single-field CASA controls. An independent SciPy natural spline matched 52 valid complex factors in the spline comparison (#1525).

    FITS import can abort on an empty primary HDU¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 aborts in ia.fromfits(infile=path, whichhdu=0) when the FITS primary HDU has NAXIS=0 and a later extension contains the image. The same file imports successfully when whichhdu=-1 selects the first image or whichhdu=1 selects its extension. casa-rs returns RuntimeError for the empty HDU and accepts both ways to select the extension. See #1625.

    exportfits drops characters when wrapping long HISTORY cards¶

    • Date noted: 2026-11-19
    • Status: CASA silently loses history text; casa-rs wraps losslessly (#977).
    • Affected code: casacore ImageFITSConverter FITS HISTORY card continuation writer.

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, exportfits(..., history=True) wraps logtable lines across 80-byte FITS HISTORY cards by placing 72 characters on the first card and > followed by 71 characters on continuation cards, but skips character index 143 on card 3 and subsequent continuation cards when the wrapped line spans more than 143 characters. On cube_i.im (crates/casa-images/tests/data/exportfits/cube_i.im), CASA drops 'y' from csys={...} in the 202-character ia.fromshape(...) message and drops the closing ')' of the 144-character ia.setrestoringbeam(...) message (emitting 47 HISTORY cards instead of 48); on a synthetic 160-digit "0123..." history entry, CASA drops '3' at index 143 between >...012 and >456....

    casa-rs (crates/casa-images/src/analysis/exportfits.rs, wrap_fits_history_text) splits at 72 characters on the first card and > plus 71 characters on each continuation card without skipping characters, emitting all 48 cards on cube_i.im so both casa-rs and CASA 6.7.6.14 ia.history(list=False) read back all 17 original history entries intact (#977).

    imsubimage aborts for an out-of-range keepaxes index¶

    On a four-axis image, CASA 6.7.6.14 aborts the process with itsAxes(i) >= naxes for imsubimage(dropdeg=True, keepaxes=[4]). The check is in casacore's AxesSpecifier::apply. casa-rs returns an out-of-range error before creating or replacing the output image. With dropdeg=False, CASA and casa-rs ignore keepaxes.

    imsubimage permutes coordinate labels after dropping transposed axes¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, take a cube in RA, Dec, Stokes, frequency order and apply imtrans(order="3012"). Then run imsubimage(box="1,2,4,5", chans="2", stokes="Q", dropdeg=True, keepaxes=[0]). The resulting shape is [1, 4, 4], with pixels in frequency, RA, Dec order. CASA labels the axes Dec, frequency, RA. In CoordinateUtil::dropRemovedAxes, the permutation passed to CoordinateSystem::transpose maps from creation order to pixel order, while transpose expects the inverse. casa-rs keeps the coordinates on their original pixel axes as it removes Stokes.

    plotbandpass passes a fractional platforming channel to range¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 raises TypeError while checking a wide bandpass for platforming. The check computes the two interior channel offsets with floating-point division, then passes those offsets to Python range(). The paired 64-channel fixture enters this branch.

    casa-rs evaluates the check at integer channel indices. In the fixture, a step at channel 32 produces a platforming report at the 10 percent threshold and a clean result at the 100 percent threshold (#1526).

    PlotMS disabling selection calls the selection setter with a missing argument¶

    The installed CASA PlotMS wrapper raises Exception("updateImmediately must be a boolean") for selectdata=False. The wrapper's no-selection call omits a selector argument before the update flag. casa-rs applies the requested unfiltered selection and leaves the caller's selector expressions inactive (#1526).

    plotbandpass references an undefined name when WEATHER is absent¶

    An atmospheric overlay on the paired bandpass with no associated WEATHER table raises NameError in CASA 6.7.6.14 when it tries to read needToClose_mymsmd. casa-rs evaluates the native atmospheric profile with the station defaults and plots the original calibration spectra with the resulting curve (#1526).

    Listing an incomplete antenna integration can terminate CASA¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14's listcal reshapes each spectral window's solution rows into complete antenna integrations. Removing the last antenna row from an otherwise regular G Jones table terminated the paired CASA subprocess after opening the requested listing file.

    casa-rs validates the antenna blocks before publishing a listing. The same input raises a recoverable error and leaves the destination absent. Complete integrations retain their original order, even when two have the same time and field (#1526).

    Beam removal can delete metadata before rejecting a record¶

    In CASA 6.7.6.14, ia.setrestoringbeam(remove=True, beam={'junk': 2}) on a native image with a restoring beam raises RuntimeError because the record has no major field. Reopening the image shows the beam was already removed. The call validates the record while preparing history after deleting the beam, even when history is disabled.

    casa-rs validates the supplied record before it saves any changes, so a rejected call preserves the restoring beam and history. See #1625.

    Naming a removed coordinate axis can terminate CASA¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 can terminate when coordsys.setnames is called with type="linear" and two names for a linear coordinate whose world and pixel maps are [0, -2]. casa-rs raises RuntimeError before applying either name. Passing the one visible axis name without a coordinate type renames that axis and preserves the stored removed-axis name (#1626).

    Transforming an MS with FLOAT_DATA can replace the requested complex DATA¶

    For an input with separately populated DATA and FLOAT_DATA, CASA 6.7.6.14 can fill output DATA from FLOAT_DATA, with zero imaginary components, when the requested layout includes DATA. This occurs with datacolumn="all", "float_data,data", and "lag_data,data" in the physical layout oracle.

    The native tasks select each requested physical column and preserve its own values through row, channel, and correlation selection (#1614).

    Transforming LAG_DATA leaves its cells undefined¶

    CASA 6.7.6.14 creates the LAG_DATA descriptor for split and mstransform but the visibility writer skips the column. Selecting only LAG_DATA can also leave FLAG cells undefined.

    The native tasks preserve the selected rows and correlations of LAG_DATA with its independent lag axis. The frequency-channel selection applies to the visibility and flag columns on the spectral axis (#1614).

    Transforming all columns can crash an MS with one scratch column¶

    On an MS with a single physical visibility column, either CORRECTED_DATA or MODEL_DATA, CASA 6.7.6.14 split(datacolumn="all") and mstransform(datacolumn="all") terminate Python with SIGSEGV in the pinned Linux build.

    The native tasks keep the original visibility column and its weights in these sparse layouts (#1614).
